34. [DONE] Total portfolio exposure cap (max_total_deployed_pct). Picked up as a "cheap adjustment" while the mechanical automation and LLM tab both sit and accumulate real outcome data rather than more construction -- closes long-standing NOT-DONE item 3. allocate_capital() (swingtrade/allocation.py) already caps concentration WITHIN one sector (max_sector_allocation_pct, default 40%), but nothing capped how much of the whole portfolio gets deployed at once ACROSS all sectors combined -- a day with signals spread evenly across many sectors could still greedily deploy 100% of cash even though no single sector ever breached its own cap.

    Since this is a capital-allocation change, the new config field ships defaulting to DISABLED (0.0, same "0 or negative disables" convention max_sector_allocation_pct already uses) -- v19/v27/v28's real live allocation behavior is unchanged by this commit. Because TradingConfig.from_dict() does cls(**data), a Mongo System_Config document that predates this field (all three real ones do) simply falls back to the dataclass default when loaded -- no migration needed, verified directly (see below). Turning the cap ON for any real strategy is a separate, deliberate config edit for later, not something this code change does on its own.

    swingtrade/config.py gained max_total_deployed_pct: float = 0.0 right next to max_sector_allocation_pct. swingtrade/allocation.py's allocate_capital() gained an optional max_total_deployed_pct parameter following the exact pattern already used for the sector cap: total_deployed_cap_dollars computed from the same portfolio_value already used for the sector cap; total_deployed_so_far seeded from existing_holdings (mirrors sector_spent's own seeding) and incremented alongside remaining_cash on every successful allocation; a trade that would push cumulative total spend past the cap is relabeled "Portfolio Limit Reached" -- same non-consuming, keep-walking treatment as Insufficient Funds/Sector Limit Reached, so a cheaper trade further down the list can still fund. Documented explicitly (mirroring the sector cap's own documented limitation) that this new cap does NOT coordinate across dip_buy_analyzer.py's three independent allocate_capital() calls per page load (primary + two secondary sections) -- existing_holdings isn't updated between them, so each call is evaluated against real holdings only, not against what another strategy hypothetically proposed the same run.

    dip_buy_analyzer.py: both allocate_capital() call sites (primary path and render_secondary_section()) now pass max_total_deployed_pct=config.max_total_deployed_pct. SIGNAL_COLORS gained a "Portfolio Limit Reached" entry (distinct color) so style_results() colors it correctly without erroring. A new caption (shown only when the cap is actually enabled) reports the cap and portfolio value next to the existing Starting Cash/Capital Allocated/Remaining Idle Cash metrics, in both the primary section and each secondary section. Sidebar's "Active trading parameters" JSON dump, the "Total Available Cash" number_input help text, the "Apply capital allocation" checkbox label/help text, and the module's numbered docstring walkthrough all updated to mention the new cap alongside the existing sector cap. TUTORIAL.md gained a paragraph mirroring the existing max_sector_allocation_pct explanation.

    Verified: an ad-hoc script (14 checks, no permanent tests/ suite, consistent with every other item this session) directly against allocate_capital() confirmed: trades correctly relabel Portfolio Limit Reached once cumulative spend would cross the cap (including an exact-boundary case); a cheaper trade further down the list still funds after a pricier one is rejected (non-stopping walk preserved); large existing_holdings correctly leaves little/no portfolio-cap room for new trades; passing max_total_deployed_pct=None or 0.0 reproduces the pre-change function's output byte-for-byte (explicit regression check); the sector cap (tighter in the test case) correctly triggers before the portfolio cap when both would independently apply to the same trade. Separately confirmed TradingConfig.from_dict() on a dict with max_total_deployed_pct deliberately deleted (simulating v19/v27/v28's real stored Mongo documents, all of which predate this field) correctly falls back to the dataclass default of 0.0 -- direct proof today's real active configs are unaffected. py_compile confirmed all three changed files parse cleanly. Full-dashboard AppTest (real 253-ticker watchlist, real v19/v27/v28 configs) completed with 0 exceptions, all 7 subheaders rendered correctly; the new caption correctly did NOT appear (cap is disabled by default for all three real configs today, exactly as intended) -- the "cap enabled" display path was verified separately via the direct ad-hoc script above, not by temporarily mutating a live config.

    Not done: no sidebar UI control for max_total_deployed_pct (same as max_sector_allocation_pct -- it's a config-document field, not a per-session dashboard toggle); no cross-section coordination of the cap across the dashboard's three independent allocation passes (documented as a known, inherited limitation, not fixed); the cap is NOT turned on for v19, v27, or v28 -- ships disabled everywhere, enabling it for any real strategy is a separate decision left to the user.

35. [DONE -- MIXED RESULT, shipped experimental/tracked-only regardless] New "Daily Signals" dashboard tab + momentum_burst, a sixth strategy built specifically to fire MORE OFTEN than any prior one. User's complaint: even with three validated strategies now automated, signals still don't fire often enough. Considered and rejected moving to intraday trading (yfinance's intraday data is severely limited, and the whole backtest/validation/automation pipeline here assumes daily bars with no execution automation) -- instead built a new dedicated tab for faster-firing DAILY strategies, starting with one candidate. Recommended momentum_burst over a simply-shortened breakout lookback: a shorter lookback dilutes the exact long-window-confirmation mechanism that makes v19 actually work (risking noise, not more signal), whereas momentum_burst captures a genuinely different phenomenon (single-day price gain CONFIRMED by unusually high volume) that can fire on days none of the other five strategies do.

    Unlike the LLM Agent tab, momentum_burst IS a mechanical strategy -- fully backtestable immediately via the existing benchmark_random_entry.py machinery, the same bar every strategy here was held to (pullback/rsi lost, breakout/breakout_retest/week52_high won). Per explicit user instruction, this tab ships EXPERIMENTAL / tracked-only (signals logged to MongoDB for real record-keeping, but NO capital allocation -- no cash pool, no allocate_capital() call) REGARDLESS of the backtest result -- promoting it to capital-eligible or automating it into ingest.py are both separate, later decisions, not part of this batch.

    Signal definition: Day_Gain_Pct (today's Close vs. prior Close, new column) >= momentum_burst_gain_pct_min (new config field, default 3.0) AND Volume_Ratio (today's Volume / prior 20-day average -- reused inline from breakout's own AvgVolume_Prior, same computation breakout_levels_from_frame already does for its own optional filter, NOT a new frame column) >= momentum_burst_volume_ratio_min (new config field, default 2.0). Both thresholds required together -- price movement alone (low-conviction volatility) and volume alone (elevated trading, no real move) are each individually weak evidence.

    swingtrade/config.py gained the two new fields (real, non-disabled defaults -- they DEFINE the trigger, unlike an optional 0-disables filter). swingtrade/levels.py gained precompute_momentum_burst_frame() (built on precompute_breakout_frame() wholesale, reusing SMA_TREND/RSI/ATR/AvgVolume/AvgVolume_Prior/ADX/etc. for free, adding one new Day_Gain_Pct column), momentum_burst_levels_from_frame(), and compute_momentum_burst_levels() -- mirrors breakout_retest's/week52_high's exact shape. Buy_Price = today's own Close (same convention week52_high uses -- a same-day confirmation, not a level to wait for); Distance_to_Buy_Pct is therefore always 0 by construction, same uninformative-but-schema-compatible treatment breakout_retest's own fallback case already established. swingtrade/scoring.py gained add_momentum_burst_trade_score() (identical RRR+Distance formula as breakout_retest/week52_high, hard-gated on Momentum_Signal). swingtrade/backtest.py gained simulate_momentum_burst_signals()/simulate_random_momentum_burst_entries() (mirrors simulate_week52_signals()'s structure) and a 7th run_backtest() dispatch branch. Entry-fill mechanic deliberately uses the existing _find_entry_fill() (resting limit, same as week52_high) rather than inventing a new "buy at next Open" mechanic originally sketched in planning -- discovered during implementation that week52_high already established the precedent for "Buy_Price = today's Close" strategies, and matching it keeps this an apples-to-apples comparison rather than introducing a new, unprecedented fill model. swingtrade/__init__.py re-exports everything new, same pattern as every prior strategy.

    benchmark_random_entry.py gained --strategy momentum_burst (choices list + two dispatch points). Its existing load_config_to_test() always loads the ACTIVE config (v19) merged with DEFAULT_CONFIG for any field the active doc predates -- since v19's real document has no key for the two new momentum_burst fields (they didn't exist when v19 was saved), this naturally and correctly tested momentum_burst at its DEFAULT_CONFIG threshold values (3.0/2.0) with zero extra config-loading plumbing needed, exactly the "untuned defaults" first-pass test every other strategy got.

    THE REAL VALIDATION RESULT (benchmark_random_entry.py --strategy momentum_burst, 5-year window 2021-08-08..2026-08-07, 253 tickers, holdout-frac=0.25/seed=42, 190 tune/63 holdout): REAL beats RANDOM on the HOLDOUT cut -- sharpe_like 0.091 vs -0.049, win_rate 57.1% vs 55.43%, avg_pnl_pct 0.62% vs -0.40% (RANDOM is actually net-negative on holdout, REAL is clearly positive) -- and on the ALL-tickers aggregate (sharpe 0.105 vs 0.073). BUT REAL LOSES to RANDOM on the TUNE cut -- sharpe 0.092 vs 0.122, win_rate 59.12% vs 62.4%. This is a genuinely mixed result, reported honestly rather than cherry-picked: holdout is the cut this project's whole methodology treats as decisive (specifically to avoid being fooled by tune-set curve-fitting), and momentum_burst passes there plus on the full aggregate -- by the same bar breakout_retest/week52_high cleared, this is a real pass. But the tune-cut shortfall is a genuine anomaly worth flagging plainly, not glossed over -- unlike a classic overfit pattern (great on tune, fails on holdout), this is closer to the reverse, which argues against curve-fitting but doesn't fully explain the split either. A real candidate for Optuna-tuning as a follow-up, which might resolve or might not survive closer scrutiny -- exactly the open question tuning-then-reverifying answered for pullback (failed) and breakout_retest/week52_high (held up) previously.

    Created System_Config v29 (storage.write_candidate(), status="candidate", active untouched) holding the exact config that was tested (v19's real params merged with DEFAULT_CONFIG's momentum_burst thresholds, strategy="momentum_burst") plus the full ALL/TUNE/HOLDOUT/RANDOM metrics and an honest notes field, mirroring how every prior candidate got persisted. config_loader.py gained a NEW, SEPARATE EXPERIMENTAL_STRATEGY_VERSIONS = {"Momentum Burst": 29} dict -- deliberately NOT merged into SECONDARY_STRATEGY_VERSIONS, since that's the actual mechanism keeping momentum_burst out of ingest.py's automation loop (which only ever iterates SECONDARY_STRATEGY_VERSIONS) and out of any capital-allocation path, not just a documentation convention.

    market_data.score_bundle_for_strategy() and dip_buy_analyzer.py's own _score_for_strategy() both gained a momentum_burst dispatch branch (the shared per-ticker-levels and per-row-scoring dispatchers every strategy goes through) -- purely additive, and inert for ingest.py specifically since ingest.py never iterates EXPERIMENTAL_STRATEGY_VERSIONS so it's never handed a momentum_burst config to score. dip_buy_analyzer.py's st.tabs() is now 3-way (Mechanical Strategies / Daily Signals (experimental) / LLM Agent (experimental)). [DONE -- TUNING FAILED, real and conclusive, momentum_burst NOT recommended for promotion] Optuna-tuned momentum_burst (the follow-up item 35 explicitly flagged) -- resolved the open question from v29's mixed untuned-defaults result, but not in momentum_burst's favor. optimize.py wired identically to how pullback/breakout_retest/week52_high were each added (module docstring paragraph, new MOMENTUM_BURST_GAIN_PCT_RANGE=(1.0,10.0)/MOMENTUM_BURST_VOLUME_RATIO_RANGE=(1.0,5.0) search-space constants, a new elif branch in build_objective()'s objective() closure, momentum_burst added to --strategy choices) -- required ZERO changes to swingtrade/backtest.py, since run_walk_forward()/run_backtest() already dispatched on strategy="momentum_burst" from item 35's batch. Smoke-tested first (3 trials, 5 tickers, --holdout-frac 0) to confirm the new branch executes correctly before committing to a full run -- correctly ran real backtest folds and correctly refused to write an under-sampled candidate.

    Real search: python optimize.py --strategy momentum_burst --trials 30 --start 2021-08-08 --end 2026-08-07 (same 5-year window benchmark_random_entry.py used for v29, full 253-ticker watchlist, default --holdout-frac 0.25/--holdout-seed 42 -- same 190 tune/63 holdout ticker split every other strategy's tuning used, for a directly comparable holdout result).

    TUNE-set result looked like a clear win: baseline (DEFAULT_CONFIG) sharpe_like=0.046, win_rate=43.59% -> best trial (#28) sharpe_like=0.215, win_rate=70.78% -- params momentum_burst_gain_pct_min=8.20 (near the top of its 1-10 range), momentum_burst_volume_ratio_min=2.36, atr_take_profit_multiplier=1.06, stop_loss_atr_multiplier=2.09.

    HOLDOUT result (63 tickers Optuna never saw, the decisive cut this project's whole methodology exists to protect): baseline sharpe_like=0.171 -> tuned candidate sharpe_like=-0.207. The tuned candidate performs WORSE than even the untuned baseline on unseen tickers, and goes net NEGATIVE -- win_rate actually rose (55.78% vs 47.14%) but avg_pnl_pct collapsed (-1.54% vs +0.97%), meaning it wins more often but loses much bigger on the losses it does take. This is the textbook overfitting signature ticker-holdout validation exists specifically to catch: a large tune-set improvement paired with a holdout collapse. Tuning did not rescue momentum_burst's earlier mixed result -- it produced a real, conclusive answer that the mixed result was NOT just "v1 defaults were poorly chosen." Effective_trade_count on holdout for the tuned candidate (35.3) is also much thinner than baseline's (105.1), since the tuned thresholds (gain_pct_min pushed to 8.2%) fire far less often -- both cleared MIN_TRADES_FOR_SCORE, so this isn't a pure sample-size artifact, but it's a contributing factor worth naming.

    optimize.py's existing flow automatically wrote this as System_Config v30 (status="candidate", active untouched, v19 still live) -- same as every prior search, regardless of outcome (several early breakout candidates from earlier this session, e.g. v11/v13/v15/v18, sit unpromoted in the historical record the same way). v30 was deliberately NOT promoted (promote_config.py --promote 30 was never run) -- this writeup IS the human review promote_config.py's own docstring describes, and the honest conclusion is don't promote it.

    Verdict: momentum_burst, at least in this v1 formulation (single-day gain + volume-ratio confirmation), does not currently hold up to tuning-then-holdout-reverification the way breakout_retest/week52_high did. Worth revisiting either by simplifying the search space further (this 4D space may still be too flexible for the trade volume available), trying a materially different signal formulation (squeeze-breakout or ADX-trend-entry, both discussed as alternative "faster-firing" candidates when this tab was first scoped), or leaving momentum_burst as-is (still beats random-entry timing at UNTUNED defaults on holdout, per item 35 -- that result stands on its own and is unaffected by this tuning attempt failing) and deprioritizing further work on it. No dashboard/tab changes in this batch -- the "Daily Signals" tab still shows momentum_burst exactly as item 35 left it (untuned defaults, v29), NOT the failed tuned v30. v19's active status untouched throughout.

37. [DONE -- genuine, real methodology question tested, result is AMBIGUOUS not a clean fix] Compared entry-fill models for momentum_burst, prompted by a direct user question: is the backtest adequate for fast-firing daily strategies at all? Real, specific concern: _find_entry_fill() (used by momentum_burst, same convention week52_high established) models a resting LIMIT order waiting for price to pull back down to the signal day's Close. For a genuine momentum continuation -- the whole premise of this strategy -- price often never pulls back, so the backtest may be systematically excluding the strongest continuation cases. Built a real, reusable alternative to test this rather than speculate.

    swingtrade/config.py gained momentum_burst_entry_fill: str = "limit" (default preserves today's exact behavior -- v29/v30 and any config missing this key, which is all of them, fall back to "limit" via TradingConfig.from_dict()'s existing safe-migration pattern). swingtrade/backtest.py gained _find_next_open_fill(bars_after_signal) -- buys the very next session's Open unconditionally, no waiting for any specific price to be touched or crossed (contrast _find_entry_fill()/_find_breakout_fill(), both of which wait, possibly days, for a level) -- and both simulate_momentum_burst_signals()/simulate_random_momentum_burst_entries() now branch on config.momentum_burst_entry_fill to pick which fill helper runs, isolating the fill mechanic as the only variable. benchmark_random_entry.py gained --momentum-burst-entry-fill {limit,next_open}, mirroring the EXACT existing --breakout-lookback-days override pattern already in that file -- a real, reusable comparison tool now, not a throwaway script.

    Verified: an ad-hoc script (11 checks) confirmed _find_next_open_fill() returns the very next bar's Open unconditionally (even across a large gap -- no level-touching logic at all, by design), returns None only when there's no bar after the signal, and confirmed simulate_momentum_burst_signals()/simulate_random_momentum_burst_entries() run correctly and produce real trades under both fill modes.

    THE REAL COMPARISON (benchmark_random_entry.py --strategy momentum_burst --momentum-burst-entry-fill next_open, identical 5-year window/holdout split/253-ticker universe as the recorded "limit" result from item 35, untuned defaults on both sides): next_open FIXES the earlier tune-cut anomaly -- REAL now beats RANDOM on tune (sharpe 0.083 vs 0.059, vs limit's REAL-loses 0.092 vs 0.122) -- but WEAKENS the holdout result that was limit's strongest evidence: REAL vs RANDOM on holdout goes from a clear win under limit (0.091 vs -0.049, RANDOM net-negative) to an essential TIE under next_open (0.093 vs 0.095, RANDOM now also positive and marginally ahead). ALL-tickers aggregate stays a similar modest REAL win either way (0.089 vs 0.070 under next_open, 0.105 vs 0.073 under limit). Notably, RANDOM's own performance shifts substantially between fill models too (holdout RANDOM: -0.049 under limit vs +0.095 under next_open) -- the fill mechanic materially changes backtest characteristics for BOTH real and random entries, not just real ones, which makes sense since it changes execution price/timing uniformly.

    Honest verdict: this is NOT a clean "the fill model was the bug" finding. next_open trades one problem (tune-cut loss) for a different, arguably more concerning one (the standout holdout win -- the single strongest piece of evidence for momentum_burst -- disappears into statistical noise). Neither fill model produces an unambiguous, clean win across all three cuts. This is itself a useful, honest answer to the adequacy question: the backtest machinery is doing its job (both fill models get real, internally-consistent results; nothing here suggests the comparison itself is broken), but momentum_burst's edge -- under either fill convention -- is thin enough that a reasonable, defensible modeling choice (how does a limit order behave) meaningfully moves the conclusion. A genuinely robust signal shouldn't be this sensitive to that choice.

    Not done: did NOT re-run Optuna tuning under next_open (would directly test whether v30's overfitting collapse was fill-model-specific) -- deliberately deferred, since the untuned next_open comparison itself is ambiguous rather than clearly promising, so spending a full tuning run to chase it needs an explicit decision, not an assumption. No dashboard/tab changes -- the Daily Signals tab is unaffected by this batch (default "limit" fill, same as before). v19's active status untouched throughout.

38. [DONE -- CLEAN, CONSISTENT WIN under both entry-fill models, strong candidate] squeeze_breakout, an 8th strategy, built after momentum_burst was deprioritized. Same "fire more often" goal, deliberately different trigger to avoid a near-duplicate: fires on a real directional expansion (Day_Gain_Pct >= squeeze_breakout_gain_pct_min, default 2.0%) following a recent volatility CONTRACTION -- a squeeze -- reusing Squeeze_Zscore (already computed for breakout's own optional breakout_squeeze_zscore_max filter), gated via a new Recent_Min_Squeeze_Zscore rolling-min column so the squeeze can have occurred at any point in the trailing squeeze_breakout_lookback_days (default 5), not only yesterday specifically.

    Design correction made and stated plainly during planning, before any code was written: the original pitch ("break out above the squeeze range's own high") was reconsidered -- requiring BOTH a genuine squeeze AND a fresh high over the same window is the intersection of two conditions, necessarily rarer than either alone, which would have quietly defeated the whole point of a faster-firing tab. Redesigned around squeeze -> expansion instead, dropping the "fresh high" requirement entirely (and dropping volume confirmation too, unlike momentum_burst, to stay a genuinely distinct trigger rather than a near-duplicate of the existing fast-firing candidate).

    Applied the entry-fill sensitivity lesson from item 37 proactively this time, not retroactively: squeeze_breakout_entry_fill ("limit"/"next_open") was built into swingtrade/config.py from the start, and BOTH fill models were checked as part of this strategy's initial validation, not added later only because a result looked borderline.

    swingtrade/levels.py gained precompute_squeeze_breakout_frame() (built on precompute_breakout_frame() wholesale -- Squeeze_Zscore/SMA_TREND/RSI/ATR/AvgVolume all reused for free -- plus one new Recent_Min_Squeeze_Zscore column and the same Day_Gain_Pct concept momentum_burst introduced, computed independently here), squeeze_breakout_levels_from_frame(), and compute_squeeze_breakout_levels() -- mirrors momentum_burst's exact shape (Buy_Price = today's Close, same-day confirmation convention; Distance_to_Buy_Pct always 0 by construction). swingtrade/scoring.py gained add_squeeze_breakout_trade_score() (identical RRR+Distance formula, hard-gated on Squeeze_Signal). swingtrade/backtest.py gained simulate_squeeze_breakout_signals()/simulate_random_squeeze_breakout_entries() (mirrors simulate_momentum_burst_signals(), including the SAME config.squeeze_breakout_entry_fill branch built in from the start) and an 8th run_backtest() dispatch branch. swingtrade/__init__.py re-exports everything new. benchmark_random_entry.py gained --strategy squeeze_breakout plus --squeeze-breakout-entry-fill {limit,next_open} (mirroring the momentum_burst override pattern). market_data.score_bundle_for_strategy() and dip_buy_analyzer.py's _score_for_strategy() both gained a squeeze_breakout dispatch branch (same shared per-ticker-levels/per-row-scoring dispatchers every strategy goes through, inert for ingest.py since it never iterates EXPERIMENTAL_STRATEGY_VERSIONS). dip_buy_analyzer.py's render_experimental_section() needed one small fix: its display-column list was hardcoded to momentum_burst's own extra columns (Day_Gain_Pct/Volume_Ratio) -- now branches per config.strategy so squeeze_breakout's own extra columns (Day_Gain_Pct/Recent_Min_Squeeze_Zscore) display correctly instead of raising a KeyError.

    Verified: an ad-hoc script (18 checks) confirmed Squeeze_Signal fires exactly when BOTH a genuine recent squeeze (hand-verified: a real sideways low-volatility consolidation fixture produced Recent_Min_Squeeze_Zscore=-1.34, comfortably past the -1.0 threshold, vs. +0.34 for a no-squeeze control -- confirming the synthetic fixture and the underlying z-score math both behave as intended, not just that thresholds compare correctly) AND a real expansion day are present; a squeeze with too-small a gain does not fire; a real gain with no recent squeeze does not fire; insufficient history raises the expected RuntimeError; Recent_Min_Squeeze_Zscore matches a hand-computed rolling min; compute_squeeze_breakout_levels() matches the frame-based path exactly; add_squeeze_breakout_trade_score() correctly hard-gates; run_backtest() dispatches correctly and still rejects unknown strategies; both entry-fill modes run without exception.

    THE REAL VALIDATION RESULT, both fill models (benchmark_random_entry.py, 5-year window 2021-08-09..2026-08-08, 253 tickers, holdout-frac=0.25/seed=42, 190 tune/63 holdout) -- a clean, consistent win, unlike momentum_burst's fragility at every stage:
    LIMIT: ALL sharpe 0.090 vs 0.045, TUNE 0.091 vs 0.057, HOLDOUT 0.089 vs 0.028 -- REAL beats RANDOM on every cut.
    NEXT_OPEN: ALL sharpe 0.079 vs 0.045, TUNE 0.083 vs 0.063, HOLDOUT 0.092 vs 0.006 -- REAL beats RANDOM on every cut here too.
    Both fill models agree: REAL genuinely beats RANDOM-entry timing everywhere tested, no anomalous cut, no fill-model sensitivity flip. Also fires far more often than any prior strategy -- 4,779-5,289 raw signals across 253 tickers over 5 years (vs. momentum_burst v29's 1,542) -- the actual "fire more often" goal this whole tab was built for, genuinely achieved this time (confirming the earlier "squeeze + fresh high" design would NOT have achieved this, validating the mid-planning correction).

    Created System_Config v31 (storage.write_candidate(), status="candidate", active untouched, v19 still live) holding the exact tested config (v19's real params merged with DEFAULT_CONFIG's squeeze_breakout thresholds, strategy="squeeze_breakout") plus the full metrics under both fill models and an honest notes field. config_loader.py's EXPERIMENTAL_STRATEGY_VERSIONS gained "Squeeze Breakout": 31 (alongside "Momentum Burst": 29, still separate from SECONDARY_STRATEGY_VERSIONS).     Not done, by design: NOT promoted to capital-eligible; NOT automated into ingest.py; no Optuna tuning yet -- this is a genuinely strong candidate for it given the clean untuned result (unlike momentum_burst, where tuning was attempted and made things worse), a natural next step but not committed to as part of this batch. v19's active status untouched throughout.

39. [DONE -- NUANCED result, NEITHER a clean pass NOR a clean fail, untuned v31 remains the better candidate] Optuna-tuned squeeze_breakout, the follow-up item 38 flagged. Wired into optimize.py identically to every prior strategy (module docstring paragraph, new SQUEEZE_BREAKOUT_ZSCORE_MAX_RANGE=(-3.0,-0.25)/SQUEEZE_BREAKOUT_LOOKBACK_DAYS_RANGE=(2,15)/SQUEEZE_BREAKOUT_GAIN_PCT_RANGE=(0.5,6.0) search-space constants, a new elif branch in build_objective(), squeeze_breakout added to --strategy choices) -- zero swingtrade/backtest.py changes needed, same as every prior strategy's optimize.py wiring.     Real search: python optimize.py --strategy squeeze_breakout --trials 30 --start 2021-08-08 --end 2026-08-07 (same window/holdout split as item 38's untuned validation). Best trial (#28) reported a striking TUNE-set sharpe_like=0.773 (vs. baseline 0.057) -- but on only 44 raw / 22.2 EFFECTIVE trades (vs. baseline's 3,174 raw / 898.4 effective), and optimize.py's own ticker-holdout check came back on just 11 raw / 6.3 effective trades, with the script's own [WARN] explicitly flagging it as "too thin to trust, not proof either way." Both winning params also sit near their search-range boundaries (zscore_max=-2.22 of a (-3.0,-0.25) range, gain_pct_min=4.97 of a (0.5,6.0) range) -- the exact boundary-pinning red flag this project's own validation pipeline has flagged before as a sign of chasing an overfit corner of parameter space, not a genuine interior optimum.

    Rather than trust a single thin-sample read either way, applied validation-pipeline step 5 directly: cross-verified v33's ACTUAL tuned params against the SAME real-vs-random-entry methodology benchmark_random_entry.py uses (flat single 5-year window, not WFO-fold-based -- a genuinely independent second measurement, not just re-reading the same number). Wrote a small one-off script (this repo's established ad-hoc-verification pattern) loading v33 directly from Mongo and running swingtrade.simulate_squeeze_breakout_signals()/simulate_random_squeeze_breakout_entries() against the full watchlist.

    Cross-verification result: v33's tuned thresholds fire EXTREMELY rarely under this methodology too -- only 61-66 signals across 253 tickers/5 years (vs. the untuned v31's 4,779-5,289) -- confirming the thin-sample read was real, not a WFO-fold-counting artifact. But where signals DO fire, REAL beats RANDOM by a wide, consistent margin on every cut: ALL sharpe 0.545 vs -0.049 (RANDOM net-negative), TUNE 0.535 vs -0.054, HOLDOUT 0.577 vs -0.03 (14 trades). Directionally striking and consistent across two independent measurements (WFO-fold and flat-window), but the absolute sample remains genuinely small (66 total trades, holdout right at 14 -- close to but not clearly above the ~15-trade floor used elsewhere in this project).

    Honest verdict, stated plainly: this is neither a clean pass (like breakout_retest/week52_high's tuned candidates) nor a clean fail (like momentum_burst's v30, which went net-negative on holdout). The direction is genuinely promising and appeared twice independently, but the sample size doesn't yet support full confidence, AND -- separately, regardless of the sample-size question -- tuning traded away the exact property that made squeeze_breakout attractive in the first place: it now fires ~70-80x less often than the untuned defaults, working against the whole point of this "Daily Signals" tab (built specifically because signals fire too rarely). Even if v33's edge is completely real, promoting it would defeat the tab's own purpose.

    Recommendation: the UNTUNED v31 defaults remain the better candidate for this tab's actual goal -- already validated cleanly on a large sample (item 38), fires often, no sample-size caveats. v33 is an interesting data point (a much more selective, possibly higher-quality-per-signal variant) worth remembering if "quality over frequency" ever becomes the priority for a future, separate strategy variant, but NOT a replacement for v31 here. Dashboard unaffected -- config_loader.EXPERIMENTAL_STRATEGY_VERSIONS still points "Squeeze Breakout" at v31, not v33.

    Wrote System_Config v33 (status="candidate", active untouched) via optimize.py's normal flow -- NOT promoted (promote_config.py --promote 33 never run). v32 (smoke-test throwaway) also sits unpromoted. v19's active status untouched throughout.

40. [DONE -- Phase 1, real but MODEST edge, distinct from both prior strategies] adx_trend_entry, a 9th strategy and the third Daily Signals candidate, built specifically to be taken through breakout's OWN real refinement history as the template: v19 didn't launch with its six optional "sharpening" filters (breakout_rsi_overbought_threshold, breakout_relative_strength_min, breakout_volume_ratio_min, breakout_adx_min, breakout_obv_zscore_min, breakout_squeeze_zscore_max) either -- they were added incrementally AFTER v19 was already a trusted baseline. This item is Phase 1 (lean v1 + validation); Phase 2 (the same filter family, added conditionally) is the immediate follow-up.

    Signal definition: a continuous STATE (like week52_high/squeeze_breakout, not a discrete event) -- ADX (already computed, config.adx_window) at/above adx_trend_entry_threshold (new field, default 25.0 -- the classic "trending" reading) AND Close above a new short-term Short_MA (config.adx_trend_entry_ma_window, default 10) for directional confirmation, since ADX alone measures trend STRENGTH independent of direction. swingtrade/levels.py gained precompute_adx_trend_entry_frame() (built on precompute_breakout_frame() wholesale -- ADX/SMA_TREND/RSI/ATR/AvgVolume all reused for free, one new Short_MA column), adx_trend_entry_levels_from_frame(), compute_adx_trend_entry_levels() -- same Buy_Price=Last_Close/Distance_to_Buy_Pct=0 convention as week52_high/momentum_burst/squeeze_breakout. swingtrade/scoring.py gained add_adx_trend_entry_trade_score() (identical RRR+Distance formula). swingtrade/backtest.py gained simulate_adx_trend_entry_signals()/simulate_random_adx_trend_entry_entries() (mirrors simulate_squeeze_breakout_signals(), including the adx_trend_entry_entry_fill limit/next_open toggle built in from the start) and a 9th run_backtest() dispatch branch. Same dispatch-branch pattern added to market_data.score_bundle_for_strategy(), dip_buy_analyzer.py's _score_for_strategy(), and benchmark_random_entry.py (--strategy adx_trend_entry, --adx-trend-entry-entry-fill).

    Verified: an ad-hoc script (17 checks) confirmed ADX_Trend_Signal fires on a genuine sustained uptrend (real fixture measured ADX=28.39, above the 25.0 threshold) and does NOT fire on real chop (a fast ~5-day-period oscillation deliberately shorter than adx_window=14, so individual swing legs can't register as sustained trend -- measured ADX=5.89, confirming the fixture and threshold logic both behave correctly, not just that the comparison operator works); confirmed a high-ADX-but-recently-reversed fixture correctly requires BOTH conditions (direction check gates a lagging ADX reading); confirmed insufficient-history RuntimeError, hard-gating, and run_backtest() dispatch (including still rejecting unknown strategies with an updated message).

    THE REAL VALIDATION RESULT, both fill models (benchmark_random_entry.py, 5-year window 2021-08-10..2026-08-09, 253 tickers, holdout-frac=0.25/seed=42) -- by far the most frequently-firing strategy built so far (39,713-42,561 raw signals vs. squeeze_breakout v31's 4,779-5,289), genuinely achieving high frequency via the continuous-STATE design:
    LIMIT: ALL sharpe 0.097 vs 0.071, TUNE 0.098 vs 0.071, HOLDOUT 0.056 vs 0.042 -- REAL beats RANDOM on every cut, real but noticeably MODEST margins compared to squeeze_breakout's untuned win (item 38: holdout 0.089 vs 0.028).
    NEXT_OPEN: ALL sharpe 0.081 vs 0.056, TUNE 0.081 vs 0.054 -- REAL still wins -- but HOLDOUT is essentially a statistical TIE, 0.044 vs 0.046, on a large sample (~7,100 effective trades -- not a thin-sample artifact the way squeeze_breakout's tuned v33 holdout was).

    Honest verdict: a real, modest, consistent edge under the primary/default (limit) fill model across all three cuts, on an unusually large and credible sample -- but the edge is thinner than squeeze_breakout's, and weakens to a genuine tie on holdout specifically under next_open. This is a third distinct outcome shape, different from both prior strategies: not fragile/negative like momentum_burst (v29/v30), not a clean win under both fill models like squeeze_breakout (v31). Judged as clearing the "worth refining" bar (a real, non-trivial base edge exists to sharpen) rather than the "fragile, filters can't rescue it" bar -- Phase 2 proceeds.

    Created System_Config v34 (storage.write_candidate(), status="candidate", active untouched) holding the exact tested config plus full metrics under both fill models. config_loader.py's EXPERIMENTAL_STRATEGY_VERSIONS gained "ADX Trend Entry": 34. Full-dashboard AppTest completed with 0 exceptions; "ADX Trend Entry (v34, experimental)" subheader rendered correctly alongside all 9 pre-existing subheaders.

41. [DONE -- Phase 2, filters built and verified, no promoted result yet] adx_trend_entry's optional "sharpening" filters -- the follow-up item 40 explicitly scoped, mirroring breakout (v19)'s own real history exactly. Judged worth doing because Phase 1's base trigger showed a real, non-trivial (if modest) edge to sharpen, not a fragile one to abandon.

    Added the SAME five filter dimensions breakout's own six cover (ADX itself is excluded here -- it's already this strategy's core trigger, not an add-on): adx_trend_entry_rsi_overbought_threshold, adx_trend_entry_relative_strength_min, adx_trend_entry_volume_ratio_min, adx_trend_entry_obv_zscore_min, adx_trend_entry_squeeze_zscore_max -- all five new swingtrade/config.py fields default to the IDENTICAL "practical no-op" values breakout's own filters use (100.0/-100.0/0.0/-100.0/100.0), so this batch changes nothing about the already-recorded v34 result until explicitly tuned.

    Architecture mirrors breakout's own EXACTLY, verified by reading breakout_levels_from_frame()/add_breakout_trade_score()/simulate_breakout_signals() line-by-line first rather than assuming: the filters are NOT baked into ADX_Trend_Signal itself (that stays purely the ADX-threshold + short-MA-direction trigger) -- they're applied as ADDITIONAL gates in TWO places kept deliberately in sync: add_adx_trend_entry_trade_score() (vectorized DataFrame masks, `&`'d into an `eligible` mask alongside ADX_Trend_Signal) and simulate_adx_trend_entry_signals() (the identical logic as sequential `if ...: continue` checks in the walk-forward loop) -- "so live and backtested definitions can't silently disagree," the exact phrase breakout's own docstrings use, now true here too. Missing/NaN filter values never exclude a ticker on their own (informational until the filter is actually tuned away from its disabled default), same treatment as breakout's own filters.

    Also matched a subtler, easy-to-miss precedent: simulate_random_adx_trend_entry_entries() (the random-entry benchmark side) was deliberately left UNCHANGED -- no market_df, no filters -- after confirming simulate_random_breakout_entries() does the same, with its own explicit comment ("the random-entry benchmark never applies the relative-strength/volume/overbought filters, only the shared macro-uptrend/liquidity gates"). The random side must keep drawing from the full macro/liquidity-eligible universe regardless of which optional filters are enabled on the real side, or the "matched-count random baseline" comparison stops being a fair TIMING test.

    adx_trend_entry_levels_from_frame() gained the four new informational fields (Relative_Strength, Volume_Ratio, OBV_Zscore, Squeeze_Zscore), extracted with the identical inline logic breakout_levels_from_frame() uses (including the same Volume_Ratio zero/NaN guard). compute_adx_trend_entry_levels() and precompute_adx_trend_entry_frame() both gained a market_df parameter (needed for Relative_Strength, same role as compute_breakout_levels()'s own) -- market_data.py's dispatch call updated to pass it through.

    Verified: a dedicated ad-hoc script (15 checks) confirmed: all four new informational fields populate correctly when market_df is given and Relative_Strength correctly stays None when it isn't (backward compatible); with all filters at disabled defaults, a real signal still scores normally; missing/NaN filter values don't exclude a ticker; each of the five filters, when individually enabled with a threshold set just past the real observed value, correctly zeroes out that same trade (proving each filter genuinely gates, not just that the code runs); an aggressively strict Volume_Ratio filter measurably reduces real backtest trade count. Also re-ran the ORIGINAL Phase 1 verification script -- everything before its now-outdated hard-coded scoring fixture (built before Phase 2 added required columns) still passed unchanged; the equivalent case is already covered correctly by the new Phase 2 script.

    Real regression check (not just logical proof): re-ran the exact same benchmark_random_entry.py --strategy adx_trend_entry (limit fill, full 253-ticker/5-year window) that produced Phase 1's recorded numbers -- came back BYTE-FOR-BYTE IDENTICAL (39,713 raw signals; ALL/TUNE/HOLDOUT sharpe_like/win_rate/avg_pnl all unchanged to the decimal), confirming the disabled-by-default filters are genuine no-ops on the real backtest path, not just in the hand-built unit checks -- the v34 Mongo candidate's already-recorded metrics remain accurate. Full-dashboard AppTest re-run after all Phase 2 changes: 0 exceptions, all 9 subheaders including "ADX Trend Entry (v34, experimental)" still render correctly.

    Not done: no specific filter combination has been tuned or validated yet -- that's what optimize.py wiring (a separate follow-up, same batching precedent as momentum_burst/squeeze_breakout) would explore, letting Optuna decide which filters (if any) actually earn their keep rather than hand-picking. v34's Mongo document and config_loader.EXPERIMENTAL_STRATEGY_VERSIONS entry are unchanged by this batch -- still the Phase 1 (all filters disabled) config; a new candidate version would only be created once real filter combinations are actually searched. No dashboard/tab changes beyond what Phase 1 already added. v19's active status untouched throughout.

42. [DONE, major finding] The RRR/Trade_Score ceiling bug -- discovered live-testing adx_trend_entry (v34/v37), traced to a systemic issue affecting ALL THREE real, capital-allocating strategies (v19 breakout, v27 breakout_retest, v28 week52_high), and ultimately revealing that two of those three strategies' validated edges were substantially illusory. The single biggest investigation this session.

    ROOT CAUSE: swingtrade/scoring.py's Trade_Score = rrr_score (0-40 pts, from RRR) + distance_score (0-20 pts, from Distance_to_Buy_Pct), rescaled to 0-100. RRR is ALWAYS exactly atr_take_profit_multiplier/stop_loss_atr_multiplier -- a config constant, not ticker-specific, since Sell_Price/Stop_Loss are always ATR multiples of Buy_Price. signal_buy_threshold=60 is mathematically unreachable below RRR=1.6 (given rrr_score_cap=4.0/rrr_score_weight=40/distance_score_weight=20), regardless of how good Distance_to_Buy_Pct is. optimize.py's Optuna search sampled atr_take_profit_multiplier/stop_loss_atr_multiplier independently, optimizing purely for backtested $ P&L with zero visibility into this separate live-scoring/allocation gate -- and landed all three real strategies below the floor: v19 RRR=0.393 (best-case Trade_Score 39.9, never even reaches Watch), v27 RRR=0.293 (38.2, same), v28 RRR=0.408 (40.1, barely). Confirmed against real production data: Trade_Signals had 177 documents, ALL strategy="rsi" from before v19 was promoted (2026-08-03) -- zero breakout/breakout_retest/week52_high documents ever, and Current_Holdings completely empty. All three real strategies had structurally never logged a signal or allocated a dollar since being tuned/promoted.

    PART 1 -- optimize.py fix (prevents recurrence for every future tune): hoisted the previously-duplicated (7 call-site pairs across rsi/breakout/pullback/breakout_retest/week52_high/momentum_burst/squeeze_breakout) independent atr_take_profit_multiplier/stop_loss_atr_multiplier sampling into one shared block with a new RRR_FLOOR=1.6 constant enforced by construction: stop_loss_atr_multiplier sampled first from a narrowed STOP_LOSS_ATR_RANGE=(0.5,2.5) (was 0.25-5.0), then atr_take_profit_multiplier sampled from max(ATR_TAKE_PROFIT_RANGE[0], stop_loss*RRR_FLOOR) to a widened ATR_TAKE_PROFIT_RANGE=(1.0,5.0) (was 1.0-3.0) so the space stays feasible across the full stop range. Verified via a 500-trial Optuna sanity check: every sampled RRR >= 1.6, full range spread confirmed, RRR_FLOOR itself computed to exactly clear Buy (60.0) at best-case Distance. Also bumped swingtrade/config.py's DEFAULT_CONFIG.atr_take_profit_multiplier 1.5->2.0 (RRR=2.0, comfortably above the floor) so brand-new untuned configs start compliant too.

    PART 2 -- experimental candidates (momentum_burst/squeeze_breakout/adx_trend_entry): two-stage fix. First just corrected the inherited-from-v19 ATR multipliers (v29/v31/v34 -> v35/v36/v37, RRR 0.393->1.5) -- but this only revealed a SECOND, deeper problem: Distance_to_Buy_Pct is always exactly 0 for these continuous-state strategies (Buy_Price IS Last_Close), so Trade_Score collapsed to one FIXED value for every eligible ticker on any given day, regardless of real signal strength -- differentiation was structurally impossible, not just capped. Fixed by adding a new Signal_Strength_Pct field to each strategy's *_levels_from_frame() (swingtrade/levels.py) -- how far the strategy's own primary trigger magnitude clears its own minimum bar (Day_Gain_Pct minus momentum_burst_gain_pct_min / squeeze_breakout_gain_pct_min; ADX minus adx_trend_entry_threshold for adx_trend_entry) -- and swapping it in for Distance_to_Buy_Pct in the three add_*_trade_score() functions (swingtrade/scoring.py), same clip-then-scale shape rrr_score already uses (bigger is better, capped at 3 new config fields: momentum_burst_strength_cap_pct/squeeze_breakout_strength_cap_pct=5.0, adx_trend_entry_strength_cap=15.0). Verified via a dedicated ad-hoc script: scores now strictly increase with real signal strength, capped correctly, Phase 2 filters and other-strategy scoring unaffected. Then wrote final candidates v38/v39/v40 (superseding v35/v36/v37) with atr_take_profit_multiplier=3.0/stop_loss_atr_multiplier=1.0 (RRR=3.0, chosen specifically so a strength-maxed signal clears BOTH Buy>=60 AND Strong Buy>80, confirmed by direct computation: 83.3). Real spot-check against the live watchlist confirmed genuine differentiation: adx_trend_entry alone showed 7 distinct Trade_Score values across Watch/Buy/Strong Buy on real current tickers (ADP 80.2 Strong Buy, FTNT/SNOW/PANW/CRWD 69.8-60.9 Buy, MSFT/NET 58.5/52.1 Watch) -- previously every eligible ticker would have shown the exact same score. config_loader.EXPERIMENTAL_STRATEGY_VERSIONS updated to {"Momentum Burst": 38, "Squeeze Breakout": 39, "ADX Trend Entry": 40}, dashboard restarted and confirmed live.

    PART 3 -- breakout (v19), attempted three approaches, honest result on the third: (a) full Optuna re-tune, 50 trials, within the new RRR_FLOOR-constrained space -> v41 (RRR=2.71, best-case score 78.5, clears Buy) but tune trade_count collapsed 864->22 (effective 20.4), holdout collapsed 300->7 (effective 6.9, explicitly flagged by the script itself as too thin to trust). (b) Re-ran with 200 trials hypothesizing more search budget would find a less-selective combination -- made it WORSE, not better: v42's tune trade_count fell further to 18, and HOLDOUT SHARPE WENT NEGATIVE (-0.706, 0% win rate on 6 trades) -- classic sharpe-chasing overfitting, Optuna given free rein over all 7 other filter params simultaneously within the RRR-constrained tp/sl space found room to cherry-pick a tiny historically-lucky sample rather than a real edge. Both v41 and v42 REJECTED, not promoted. (c) Surgical fix instead: kept v19's exact already-validated filter params (breakout_lookback_days=71 -- no, v19's ORIGINAL values, unchanged -- breakout_rsi_overbought_threshold/relative_strength_min/volume_ratio_min/adx_min/obv_zscore_min/squeeze_zscore_max all untouched), changed ONLY atr_take_profit_multiplier/stop_loss_atr_multiplier to 2.0/1.0 (RRR=2.0, best-case score 66.7, clears Buy) -> v43. Verified via a direct walk-forward comparison (optimize.py's own fetch/fold/holdout-split/summarize machinery reused, no fresh search -- had to fix a real Windows multiprocessing bug in the verification script first: run_walk_forward()'s ProcessPoolExecutor spawning at module top-level without an `if __name__ == "__main__":` guard caused a 3+-hour silent deadlock on the first attempt, each spawned worker re-importing the script and re-triggering the same top-level call in a bootstrapping race -- fixed by wrapping the script body in main()). Result: trade frequency stayed healthy (213 tune / 52 holdout, effective 157.9/44.5 -- NOT thin, unlike v41/v42), and both cuts stayed consistently POSITIVE, but the edge itself roughly HALVED: sharpe 0.312->0.15 (tune), 0.553->0.139 (holdout); win_rate collapsed 72.9%->39.34% (tune), 76.55%->38.62% (holdout). v43 written to Mongo, NOT promoted -- v19's original outsized win rate appears to have been substantially built on its unusual tight-target/wide-stop payoff shape; a real, credible, but much weaker edge survives normalization.

    PART 4 -- breakout_retest (v27) and week52_high (v28), same surgical approach applied -- MUCH more serious result than breakout's. Both strategies' edges effectively EVAPORATED, not just weakened: v27 tune sharpe 0.174->-0.013 (NEGATIVE), win_rate 64.8%->32.53%; holdout sharpe 0.106->0.023 (near zero), win_rate 62.25%->32.77%. v28 tune sharpe 0.164->0.018 (near zero), win_rate 55.01%->33.86%; holdout sharpe 0.096->-0.106 (NEGATIVE), win_rate 54.41%->27.34%. Both on massive samples (thousands of effective trades -- no thin-sample caveat) -- both win rates landed almost exactly on DEFAULT_CONFIG's own unfiltered baseline win rate (~27-34%), i.e. statistically indistinguishable from no edge at all. Ran a follow-up diagnostic to isolate WHETHER moving the stop or the target caused this (the surgical fix moved both simultaneously): kept each strategy's ORIGINAL stop_loss_atr_multiplier completely unchanged, raised ONLY atr_take_profit_multiplier to the minimum needed to hit RRR_FLOOR=1.6 exactly (v27: target 1.458->7.972 ATR; v28: 1.864->7.312 ATR). Result: win rate collapsed even further under this gentler treatment (v27: 0.93%/3.41% tune/holdout; v28: 2.01%/2.65%) though sharpe stayed weakly positive in most cuts (0.095/0.067 v27, 0.073/-0.007 v28) -- a high-variance "rare huge winner, many small losses/timeouts" profile, mechanically inevitable once the target sits ~8x ATR away (almost nothing travels that far before either the wide stop or max_holding_days ends the trade). CONCLUSION: it doesn't matter which end of the RRR moves -- ANY shape that could actually clear the live scoring ceiling destroys the original ~55-65% headline win rate for both strategies. Strong evidence that v27/v28's original validated win rate was substantially a MECHANICAL ARTIFACT of an extreme tight-target/wide-stop payoff shape (almost any non-reversing move counts as a "win" against a very close target), not genuine entry-timing skill -- the $-weighted sharpe survives somewhat better but is far weaker and less consistent than originally claimed, and both strategies now go negative on at least one cut. Both variants (the "both changed" sl=1.0/tp=2.0 shape) written to Mongo as v44 (breakout_retest) and v45 (week52_high), explicitly marked REJECTED in their notes -- NOT promoted, NOT recommended.

    BOTTOM LINE: v19/v27/v28 remain untouched (still active/wired-into-dashboard exactly as before) -- nothing here changes real capital allocation without further explicit action. But the investigation surfaced that the entire "real-capital-eligible" status of breakout_retest and week52_high may need reconsidering independent of the scoring-ceiling bug -- their original validation, run through this same RRR-dependent Trade_Score/win-rate lens, may have been measuring a payoff-geometry artifact rather than real timing value. This is flagged for the user's own judgment, not acted on unilaterally. breakout (v19) fares better -- v43 shows a real, weaker-but-genuine edge survives -- but even v43 sits unpromoted pending explicit review. Six new candidates total this investigation: v38/v39/v40 (experimental, ready to consider), v43 (breakout surgical fix, real-but-weaker edge), v44/v45 (breakout_retest/week52_high, REJECTED for the record). See [[strategy-validation-pipeline]] -- this whole investigation is itself a new, durable lesson for that pipeline: a strategy's backtested win rate/sharpe can be strongly shaped by its RRR/payoff geometry independent of real timing skill, and any strategy whose config wasn't itself checked for RRR-vs-scoring-ceiling compatibility should be treated as unvalidated for live capital purposes until it is.

43. [DONE, inconclusive result -- consolidation attempted, tuning methodology diagnosed and partially fixed, but no new validated candidate produced] Consolidate to 1 refined strategy per timeframe (breakout for real capital, squeeze_breakout for Daily Signals), per explicit user request after item 42's findings ("i would rather have 1 highly refined strategy for both timeframes... since whatever enhancements in the past... could all have been falsified"). Chose breakout (v43) for the real-capital slot (only real-capital strategy whose edge survived RRR normalization) and squeeze_breakout (v39) for the Daily Signals slot (the only one of the three Daily Signals candidates that beat its own random-entry baseline on ALL/TUNE/HOLDOUT under RRR=3.0 -- adx_trend_entry and momentum_burst both lose to random on holdout, confirmed via a dedicated real-vs-random 5-year benchmark run against all three current candidates).

    Built squeeze_breakout its own filter family for the first time -- 5 "sharpening" filters mirroring breakout's own six (minus Squeeze_Zscore itself, already the core trigger, same exclusion adx_trend_entry's Phase 2 made for ADX): squeeze_breakout_rsi_overbought_threshold/relative_strength_min/volume_ratio_min/adx_min/obv_zscore_min (swingtrade/config.py), all practical no-op defaults. Threaded market_df through compute_squeeze_breakout_levels() (was missing the param -- precompute_squeeze_breakout_frame() already accepted and forwarded it), added Relative_Strength/Volume_Ratio/ADX/OBV_Zscore to squeeze_breakout_levels_from_frame()'s returned dict, added the 5 filter gates to add_squeeze_breakout_trade_score() (scoring.py) and simulate_squeeze_breakout_signals() (backtest.py, kept in sync), left simulate_random_squeeze_breakout_entries() deliberately unchanged (matches every prior strategy's precedent). Fixed market_data.py's dispatch (wasn't passing market_df). Verified via a dedicated 16-check ad-hoc script (each filter individually gates a real trade; disabled filters are no-ops; missing/NaN values don't exclude on their own; Relative_Strength None without market_df) PLUS a real regression re-run of the full 5-year/253-ticker benchmark confirming BYTE-IDENTICAL results to v39's own numbers with filters at their disabled defaults (4845 raw signals, all ALL/TUNE/HOLDOUT metrics matching exactly).

    Added a reusable tp/sl PINNING mechanism to optimize.py (--pin-atr-take-profit-multiplier/--pin-stop-loss-atr-multiplier, optional, both-or-neither) -- applying item 42's own hard lesson (letting Optuna freely search tp/sl AND every filter simultaneously cherry-picks a tiny overfit sample even within the RRR_FLOOR-constrained space) by isolating exactly what gets tuned: pin tp/sl at each strategy's own already-verified value (breakout 2.0/1.0 from v43, squeeze_breakout 3.0/1.0 from v39), let Optuna search ONLY the filter/trigger shape params. Verified the pinning mechanism itself via a fast 2-trial/2-ticker smoke test (confirmed atr_take_profit_multiplier/stop_loss_atr_multiplier never appear in Optuna's sampled params when pinned).

    FIRST pinned re-tune attempt (100 trials each): breakout -> v46 (tune sharpe 0.434, 26.6 effective trades -- looked good) but HOLDOUT collapsed to 4.9 effective trades, sharpe -0.108 (negative). squeeze_breakout -> v47 (tune sharpe 0.391, 25.8 effective) but HOLDOUT collapsed to 8.6 effective trades, win_rate 12%, sharpe -0.846 (badly negative). Both REJECTED -- same overfitting signature as item 42's original breakout re-tune attempts (v41/v42), just now happening via the filter thresholds alone since tp/sl pinning eliminated that one vector but not the underlying issue.

    ROOT CAUSE DIAGNOSED (not guessed -- verified against real stored metrics): pulled every tuning attempt's TUNE/HOLDOUT effective_trade_count this session (v30, v33, v41, v42, v46, v47) and found that 5 of 6 winning trials converged to a TUNE effective_trade_count clustered tightly in the high-teens-to-twenties (17.1-26.6) -- just above the OLD MIN_TRADES_FOR_SCORE=15 under-sampling floor. With 100 trials all competing to maximize sharpe_like and no penalty for merely clearing that floor, Optuna was structurally incentivized to find the smallest sample that dodged the penalty, since small samples have the highest sharpe variance and thus the highest chance of a lucky-looking spike -- a textbook selection-bias/multiple-testing pattern (the same phenomenon Bailey & Lopez de Prado's backtest-overfitting literature calls "backtest overfitting via excessive trials"). The holdout check correctly caught every one of these (3 of 5 went net negative) -- proof the VALIDATION half of the pipeline is sound, even though the SEARCH half was structurally wasting effort chasing samples that were statistically never going to generalize. (Separately: momentum_burst's v30 had a much larger tune sample, 112.3 effective, and still failed holdout -- a distinct, simpler "no real edge exists" case, not the same sample-gaming failure mode.)

    FIX: raised MIN_TRADES_FOR_SCORE from 15 to 50 (optimize.py) -- well below what a genuinely non-overfit config achieves (v43's breakout: 157.9 tune-effective; v39's squeeze_breakout: 2491.2) but high enough to rule out the observed 17-29 overfit zone outright.

    SECOND pinned re-tune attempt, under the corrected threshold: breakout -> v48, tune sample DID improve substantially (60.2 effective, above the new floor, real progress) but HOLDOUT STILL went negative (17.4 effective, sharpe -0.281) -- confirms this specific overfitting instance was not purely a sample-size artifact; whatever filter combination wins on the tune tickers genuinely does not generalize to the holdout tickers. squeeze_breakout -> EVERY SINGLE ONE of 100 trials came back under-sampled (0 effective trades on the winning "best" trial) -- the search could not find ANY combination of the 3 core trigger params + 5 filters that clears 50 effective trades within the given search ranges; no candidate was written at all. The corrected threshold is working exactly as intended (refusing to output an overfit candidate) but produced no usable tuned result for either strategy.

    CONCLUSION: filter-tuning breakout and squeeze_breakout, via Optuna, on this 253-ticker/190-tune-ticker universe, has now failed SIX separate times across two different search configurations (free tp/sl -- item 42's v41/v42; pinned tp/sl at old threshold -- v46/v47; pinned tp/sl at corrected threshold -- v48/squeeze_breakout's total failure). The untuned, filterless baselines (v43 breakout, v39 squeeze_breakout) remain the ONLY candidates from this entire multi-day session with a real, validated, holdout-positive edge. See strategy_selection.txt for recommendations on where to go from here -- written specifically because further Optuna-based refinement of these same two strategies does not appear to be a productive path without a materially larger ticker universe or a much lower-dimensional search (see that file for detail). v19/v27/v28 (and all prior candidates) remain completely untouched throughout -- nothing promoted, nothing committed.

44. [URGENT FINDING -- process gap, not yet resolved] v43 (promoted to ACTIVE/real capital 2026-08-10) does NOT clearly beat random-entry timing over the full 5-year window, confirmed via the official benchmark_random_entry.py tool (the established gold-standard check, validation-pipeline step 3): ALL tickers REAL sharpe=0.049 vs RANDOM=0.05 (tied); TUNE REAL=0.053 vs RANDOM=0.103 (REAL LOSES); HOLDOUT REAL=0.004 vs RANDOM=-0.131 (REAL "wins" only because RANDOM is negative -- REAL's own absolute sharpe is ~0).

    ROOT CAUSE: the pre-promotion verification (improvements.txt item 42/43, "surgical fix") compared v43 against DEFAULT_CONFIG (a genuinely unfiltered breakout) via a WFO tune/holdout comparison -- NOT against the actual gold-standard random-entry-timing benchmark for v43's exact final params. That comparison showed v43's filters clearly beat "no filters" (tune 0.15 vs 0.015, holdout 0.139 vs -0.011), which is real and true, but is a DIFFERENT, weaker question than "does the breakout signal beat a random day in the same uptrending/liquid/volume-confirmed universe" -- the specific check this session's own validation pipeline calls the critical gate (step 3), and the one every OTHER strategy in this project was held to before being trusted. Substituting the DEFAULT_CONFIG comparison for the random-entry benchmark was a real process gap, made while moving quickly on an explicit "size a position today" request.

    WHAT THIS MEANS: it does not mean v43 is doing something actively harmful -- it means the breakout SIGNAL itself (a fresh N-day closing high, even with RSI/relative-strength/volume-ratio filters) does not clearly carry timing information beyond what the shared macro-uptrend + liquidity + volume gates already provide for free (the random-entry benchmark uses those SAME gates, just skips the specific breakout-trigger day). Discovered incidentally while grid-searching individual filters for further refinement (see item 45) -- a combined-filter+shorter-holding-period variant was ALSO checked this same way and came out worse (loses to random on all 3 cuts, not just 2).

    STATUS: flagged to the user immediately upon discovery, NOT acted on unilaterally -- v43 remains active/real-capital-eligible exactly as promoted. Un-promoting or reverting to a different config is a capital-allocation decision requiring the user's own explicit direction, same as promoting was. This item exists so the gap and its cause are on the permanent record regardless of what's decided next.

45. [DONE -- exploratory research, autonomous session] Single-filter grid search + holding-period sweep + volatility-regime prototype for breakout (v43) and squeeze_breakout (v39), run autonomously per explicit user request ("full autonomy... test various strategies and/or filters for various timeframes... more reliable and accurate strategies"). Nothing promoted or committed -- pure research, documented for the record.

    METHOD: single-filter grid search (sweep ONE filter at a time against each strategy's live baseline, holding all others fixed -- much lower-dimensional than the multi-dimensional Optuna searches that failed 6/6 times in item 43, per strategy_selection.txt's own recommendation) using a 1-year WFO tune/holdout summary (optimize.py's own methodology).

    BREAKOUT: three filters showed mild, non-thin, same-direction improvements on the 1-year WFO check: breakout_relative_strength_min -10->0 (tune sharpe 0.148->0.17, hold unchanged), breakout_obv_zscore_min -100->0.5 (tune 0.148->0.159, hold unchanged), breakout_adx_min 0->15 (tune 0.148->0.155, hold 0.139->0.147). Larger ADX thresholds (20-30) looked spectacular but thinned the holdout sample fast (16-24 effective trades) -- treated as the same overfitting red flag as item 43, not trusted. Holding-period sweep (max_holding_days) found the strategy remarkably stable from 5-45 days, with max_holding_days=8 showing a real, non-thin holdout improvement (sharpe 0.139->0.172, same 44.5 effective trades).

    CRITICAL FOLLOW-UP: combined all three filters + max_holding_days=8 into one candidate and verified it PROPERLY (real-vs-random benchmark, 5-year window, not just the 1-year WFO check that found it promising) -- the combined refinement LOSES to random on ALL THREE cuts (worse than v43's own baseline, which at least ties/weakly-wins on 2 of 3). This is the single most important methodological lesson from this session's autonomous research: **the 1-year WFO tune/holdout sharpe check (this project's own historical validation shorthand, and what Optuna itself optimizes) is NOT a reliable substitute for the full random-entry-timing benchmark** -- a filter change can cleanly improve the WFO metric while making the strategy WORSE at beating random entry. This is what surfaced item 44's v43 finding in the first place, and is now added to [[strategy-validation-pipeline]] as a new step.

    SQUEEZE_BREAKOUT: no single filter fixes v39's negative 1-year TUNE sharpe (-0.122) cleanly. Notable structural finding, consistent across every max_holding_days tested (5-45 days): TUNE stays negative (-0.069 to -0.132) while HOLD stays modestly positive (0.087-0.172) throughout -- NOT a holding-period artifact, a genuine TUNE/HOLD divergence. Investigated further across multiple window lengths (1y/2y/3y/5y, each fetched independently -- an EARLIER attempt at this same check sliced a single 5-year fetch down to shorter windows via pandas boolean indexing, which silently strips out fetch_history()'s own 420-day lookback buffer needed for SMA-200/ATR/Squeeze_Zscore warmup, corrupting the shorter-window numbers; caught by noticing the results didn't match the already-trustworthy 1-year figures from the single-filter grid search, and re-run correctly -- see the note on this below). Clean result: TUNE sharpe rises monotonically with window length (1y=-0.122, 2y=0.016, 3y=0.025, 5y=0.035) while HOLD stays consistently positive throughout (1y=0.087, 2y=0.083, 3y=0.056, 5y=0.065). This is a genuine, coherent finding, not noise: squeeze_breakout's edge is real and positive over multi-year history (consistent with item 42/43's original 5-year real-vs-random validation) but has specifically softened/gone negative on the TUNE-ticker subset over the most recent 12 months. Not a reason to abandon the strategy -- HOLD (the more trustworthy, never-touched-during-search cut) stayed positive throughout every window tested -- but a real, useful piece of context: squeeze_breakout has had a rougher-than-average recent year.

    METHODOLOGICAL NOTE (own mistake, corrected): the buffer-stripping bug above is a durable lesson for any future ad-hoc script that wants to test multiple window lengths cheaply by fetching once and slicing -- do NOT do this. Always call fetch_history(ticker, start, end) directly for each window length; it manages its own LOOKBACK_BUFFER_DAYS=420 buffer correctly, and naive slicing of a longer fetch silently defeats that. Caught by noticing inconsistent results between two supposedly-equivalent checks, not by inspection -- a reminder that surprising numbers deserve a methodology audit before being trusted, not just a bigger reaction.

    VOLATILITY-REGIME CONDITIONING: prototyped cheaply (bucket already-simulated real trades by SPY's own trailing realized-vol percentile, no new config/scoring/backtest code written) before investing in real plumbing. Result: no meaningful differentiation for either strategy (breakout sharpe 0.067/0.108/0.095 across low/mid/high vol; squeeze_breakout 0.086/0.082/0.062) -- dropped, not worth building.

49. [DONE -- new strategy, promising first-pass result] Built ma_crossover (10th strategy), a genuinely different mechanical trigger for the now-empty breakout-timeframe slot, per explicit user request ("start looking through a new avenue"). Motivated by a real pattern noticed across this whole project's history: every "buy strength/momentum" concept tried has failed or had its edge evaporate (rsi, pullback, breakout in all its variants, breakout_retest, week52_high, momentum_burst, adx_trend_entry) -- the ONE strategy that actually beats random-entry timing, squeeze_breakout, isn't a momentum signal at all, it's a volatility-REGIME signal (contraction, then expansion). ma_crossover tests a third, unrelated mechanical family: trend CONFIRMATION via a short (20d) SMA crossing above a long (50d) SMA -- not a price level, not a volatility shift, not RSI, not a raw trend-strength threshold.

    Built via the exact same architecture every strategy this session uses (swingtrade/config.py: ma_crossover_short_window/long_window/entry_fill/strength_cap_pct; swingtrade/levels.py: precompute_ma_crossover_frame()/ma_crossover_levels_from_frame()/compute_ma_crossover_levels(), built on precompute_breakout_frame() wholesale; swingtrade/scoring.py: add_ma_crossover_trade_score(), same RRR+Signal_Strength_Pct formula as momentum_burst/squeeze_breakout/adx_trend_entry; swingtrade/backtest.py: simulate_ma_crossover_signals()/simulate_random_ma_crossover_entries(), 10th run_backtest() dispatch branch). Deliberately lean v1, no optional filters -- and critically, RRR set explicitly floor-compliant (2.0/1.0) from this candidate's very first version, unlike breakout's v43 which inherited a non-compliant ratio from v19 and had to be fixed after promotion (item 44) -- this project's own hardest-won lesson applied proactively this time, not discovered the hard way again.

    Caught and fixed two real bugs during verification, not after: (1) forgot to import precompute_ma_crossover_frame/ma_crossover_levels_from_frame into backtest.py's own namespace -- simulate_ma_crossover_signals() would have raised NameError the first time it actually ran; caught immediately by the unit-style verification script (15 checks: crossover fires exactly on the cross day, not every day the relationship merely holds; RuntimeError on insufficient history; Buy_Price/Distance_to_Buy_Pct/Signal_Strength_Pct/RRR schema checks; run_backtest dispatch; scoring regression) before it ever reached the critical benchmark. (2) the first test fixture (flat-then-uptrend) had a genuine design flaw -- with a 200-day macro-trend SMA gate, a flat-then-sharp-uptrend pattern let the crossover and the macro-uptrend gate's own warmup fight each other on timing, producing zero signals in the fixture even though the underlying logic was correct; fixed by redesigning the fixture as a realistic multi-year uptrend with a mid-trend pullback-then-resumption (a proper "buy the resumption" pattern), which produced the expected single clean crossover event and let all 15 checks pass.

    CRITICAL GATE result (real vs random, full 5-year/253-ticker window, via the OFFICIAL benchmark_random_entry.py tool, checked BEFORE any tuning or filter work -- this project's own established discipline, applied from the first candidate this time): REAL beats RANDOM on ALL THREE cuts, same direction throughout -- ALL sharpe 0.02 vs -0.029, TUNE -0.005 vs -0.033, HOLDOUT 0.088 vs -0.064 -- on healthy, non-thin samples (1552.8/1196.3/409.8 effective trades). This is the most promising untuned first-pass result for any NEW strategy this entire session. Honest caveat: TUNE's own absolute performance is weak/near-zero (even though it beats its random baseline), so this is a real but modest edge, not a spectacular one -- HOLDOUT's clearer gap (0.088 vs -0.064) is the more credible signal of the two.

    Written to Mongo as candidate v49 (params = DEFAULT_CONFIG's own, strategy="ma_crossover", untuned). NOT promoted, NOT wired into any dashboard tab or SECONDARY/EXPERIMENTAL_STRATEGY_VERSIONS dict yet -- next step, if pursued, is the same single-filter-at-a-time refinement discipline established this session (never multi-dimensional Optuna, per breakout's own 6 failed tuning attempts), with every candidate re-verified via this same real-vs-random benchmark before being trusted, not just a cheap WFO check.
