52. [FIXED -- real bug, root-caused] LLM Agent tab returning no response for every ticker, per explicit user report ("all of them failed to give any response"). Bypassed `llm_agent.py`'s own exception-swallowing (`_call_gemini`/`_call_groq` both catch-and-return-None by design, so the dashboard just shows "unavailable" with no diagnostic trail) to call each provider directly and see the real error.

    ROOT CAUSE: `gemini-2.5-flash` -- the PRIMARY provider, hardcoded in both `llm_agent.py` (`GEMINI_MODEL`) and `ai_context.py` (`MODEL`) -- has been retired for this account: every call 404s with "This model models/gemini-2.5-flash is no longer available to new users." This is a permanent condition, not a transient outage (confirmed by listing the account's actually-available models -- `gemini-2.5-flash` still shows up in the list endpoint, but returns 404 on generateContent regardless of the "models/" prefix or lack thereof; `gemini-2.5-flash-lite` is equally dead). Because Gemini is PRIMARY, every single call was silently failing over to Groq (FALLBACK) -- meaning 100% of this tab's real load has been landing on Groq's much smaller free-tier quota (250 requests/day vs. Gemini's 1,500/day) ever since Google made this change, without any visible error anywhere in the app. That mismatch plausibly explains "all of them failed" even though a single isolated Groq call succeeds fine in isolation -- sustained dashboard use burns through a 250/day cap far faster than a 1,500/day one, and once Groq's own quota/rate limit is hit, its calls ALSO return None (same silent-swallow design), so at that point both providers genuinely produce nothing, for every ticker, exactly as reported.

    FIX: swapped `GEMINI_MODEL`/`MODEL` to `gemini-3.5-flash` in both `llm_agent.py` and `ai_context.py` (verified working via a direct `client.models.list()` + `generate_content()` probe across several candidate model names). Caught a SECOND issue during that same verification: `gemini-3.5-flash` is a "thinking" model by default, and with no `thinking_config`, it was spending its entire `max_output_tokens` budget (400/300) on internal reasoning tokens and returning empty/truncated text -- `_parse_response` correctly rejected the truncated JSON as unusable, but this would have looked identical to "the model just isn't responding," a second silent failure mode stacked on the first. Fixed by adding `thinking_config=types.ThinkingConfig(thinking_budget=0)` to both files' `GenerateContentConfig` calls, matching gemini-2.5-flash's original fast/non-reasoning behavior. Both fixes verified together via direct calls to the real public functions (`llm_agent.evaluate_ticker()`, `ai_context.summarize_ticker_context()`) producing valid, parsed output, plus a full Streamlit `AppTest` (0 exceptions) and a live dashboard restart.

    Not caught earlier because both `_call_gemini`/`_call_groq` and their callers are deliberately designed to degrade silently (never break a scan over a flaky LLM call) -- the right philosophy for resilience, but it means a PERMANENT provider failure looks identical to a transient one from inside the app, with no error surfaced anywhere short of directly bypassing the try/except. Worth remembering for any future "the LLM feature isn't responding" report: check the raw provider exception first, don't assume it's a rate limit or a flaky day.

53. [DONE -- 3 phases, per explicit user request] Position Review LLM overlay, richer qualitative LLM inputs, and an earnings-proximity filter investigation for squeeze_breakout/ma_crossover. Planned via EnterPlanMode, confirmed with the user via AskUserQuestion on scope/priority before building (all three phases wanted; multi-agent debate architecture explicitly deferred -- see below).

    PHASE 1 -- Position Review LLM overlay: `review_holding()` (swingtrade/levels.py) is purely mechanical -- stop breached or target hit both unconditionally recommend SELL, no "let a winner run" concept. Added `llm_agent.evaluate_holding(ticker, context)`, a sibling to `evaluate_ticker()` reusing the same Gemini-primary/Groq-fallback machinery (refactored `_call_gemini`/`_call_groq` to accept a pluggable `parse_fn` so both functions share the exact same provider-call code, just different JSON schemas -- `VALID_HOLD_ACTIONS = ("Hold For More", "Take Profit")` vs. `VALID_DECISIONS`). Deliberately a SEPARATE schema/function, not a parameter on `evaluate_ticker()`: the prompt explicitly defaults to "Take Profit" unless the evidence for continuation is real and specific, an intentional asymmetry a shared schema would blur. Only invoked for rows where the mechanical `Recommendation == "SELL (target hit)"` (not stop-breaches, not plain holds) -- same "second opinion on something mechanically flagged" philosophy as the LLM Agent tab, not a blind evaluation of every holding. Wired into `dip_buy_analyzer.py`'s Position Review section as a purely additive expander per hit-target position (`"{ticker} -- mechanical: SELL (target hit) | LLM: {action}"`) -- the existing mechanical `Recommendation` column and its color-coding are completely untouched. Never affects position sizing or any capital-allocation path.

    PHASE 2 -- richer qualitative LLM inputs, explicitly asked to go BEYOND the first-pass proposal (analyst targets/upgrades-downgrades/insider transactions alone). A second live `yfinance` probe (no new dependency, no repeat of the GDELT/OpenRouter dead ends already rejected earlier in this project) confirmed five genuinely distinct, free, working signal families: analyst consensus + 4-month recommendation TREND (not just a snapshot), insider buy/sell direction + institutional/insider ownership levels, short-interest % of float + month-over-month trend, recent SEC filings (8-Ks flagged as material, distinct from routine 10-Q/10-K/DEF14A), and nearest-expiry options put/call volume ratio. New `market_data.get_qualitative_snapshot(ticker)` -- each of the 5 sub-fetchers (`_get_analyst_data`/`_get_insider_data`/`_get_short_interest`/`_get_recent_filings`/`_get_options_sentiment`) degrades INDEPENDENTLY on its own failure (never raises, one bad yfinance call never blocks the other four), same resilience philosophy as the existing `get_macro_snapshot()`. `llm_agent._build_qualitative_block()` renders whichever sub-sections are actually present into one labeled paragraph, folded into both `_build_prompt()` (new-candidate evaluation) and `_build_holding_prompt()` (Phase 1's overlay) via a new optional `context["qualitative"]` key -- wired into both `dip_buy_analyzer.py` call sites. Verified via real calls: the LLM's own rationale text genuinely referenced the new signals (mentioned "insider selling," "weakening analyst trend" -- not generic filler) rather than just padding the prompt.

    PHASE 3 -- earnings-proximity filter investigation for squeeze_breakout (v39) and ma_crossover (v49), the two currently capital-eligible strategies.

    3a (foundational, kept regardless of 3c's result): discovered that `earnings_dates`/honest `Catalyst_Warning` computation was ONLY ever wired for the "rsi" strategy's backtest path (`simulate_signals`) -- every other strategy's backtest, including squeeze_breakout and ma_crossover, had `Catalyst_Warning` hardcoded to `False` regardless of real earnings proximity (confirmed by grep: zero `earnings_dates` references anywhere in `benchmark_random_entry.py`/`optimize.py`). This meant the live dashboard's `Catalyst_Warning` column had never actually matched what was backtested for 8 of 9 strategies -- a real, previously-undiscovered gap. Fixed for squeeze_breakout/ma_crossover specifically: threaded `earnings_dates` through `simulate_squeeze_breakout_signals`/`simulate_random_squeeze_breakout_entries`/`simulate_ma_crossover_signals`/`simulate_random_ma_crossover_entries` (mirroring `simulate_signals`' own `_next_earnings_date()` pattern exactly), `run_backtest()`'s dispatch, `benchmark_random_entry.py` (new `--with-catalyst` flag, matching `run_backtest.py`'s own convention), and `optimize.py` (same flag, threaded into `build_objective`/the baseline+holdout `run_walk_forward` calls -- scoped to `squeeze_breakout` only, since `optimize.py` has never supported `ma_crossover` as a `--strategy` choice, deliberately, matching this project's own "no Optuna for ma_crossover yet" precedent). Verified via a 20-check unit script using real AAPL data + real earnings history: `Catalyst_Warning` now genuinely varies (previously always `False`), the new gate fields default to `False` and are BYTE-IDENTICAL no-ops vs. the pre-existing behavior, enabling the gate only ever REMOVES trades (never adds), and the scoring layer (`add_*_trade_score`) and backtest layer agree.

    3b: new optional boolean fields `squeeze_breakout_earnings_gate`/`ma_crossover_earnings_gate` (default `False`) -- a boolean toggle, not a numeric threshold, since it reuses the existing shared `earnings_warning_days`/`Catalyst_Warning` machinery directly rather than inventing a second earnings-window concept. Wired as a hard gate in both `add_*_trade_score()` (scoring.py) and `simulate_*_signals()` (backtest.py), kept in sync per this codebase's own strict convention.

    3c: **REJECTED for both strategies after full validation.** Cheap 1-year WFO screen first: squeeze_breakout's gate made TUNE and HOLD both worse (sharpe -0.135->-0.18, 0.08->-0.022) -- clear reject, no further check needed. ma_crossover's gate showed a MIXED cheap-check signal (TUNE softened 0.075->0.049, HOLD improved 0.185->0.311 on a borderline-thin 30.0 effective trades) -- not the alarming "TUNE way up/HOLD craters" overfitting shape flagged repeatedly this session, so it earned the full check rather than being dismissed outright. **Full 5-year real-vs-random benchmark result: still not trustworthy.** Under `ma_crossover_earnings_gate=True`, TUNE actually FLIPS to a real loss (REAL -0.009 vs RANDOM +0.004, a genuine reversal from the baseline's clean win of -0.004 vs -0.034), even though HOLDOUT strengthens (REAL 0.104 vs RANDOM -0.103, wider than baseline's 0.088 vs -0.027 gap) -- and effective trade counts thin noticeably on both cuts under the gate (TUNE 1196->989, HOLDOUT 410->337). A filter that helps one cut while reversing another, on a shrinking sample, is exactly the kind of result this session's own validation-pipeline step 11 exists to catch, not trust. (Caught and fixed a real methodology bug in my own ad-hoc verification script during this check -- creating a fresh `random.Random(1)` inside the per-ticker loop instead of ONE shared instance reused across tickers, a deviation from `benchmark_random_entry.py`'s own established pattern; re-ran after fixing it before trusting any number.) **Both `squeeze_breakout_earnings_gate` and `ma_crossover_earnings_gate` ship disabled (the default) on v39/v49 -- no live config touched.** The 3a foundational fix stands on its own merit regardless (fixes a real, previously-broken field) and both boolean fields remain available for a future, more targeted attempt.

    Explicitly NOT built this round, per the user's own answers to AskUserQuestion: the TradingAgents-style multi-agent debate architecture. The LLM Agent tab had logged ZERO signals and ZERO settled trades ever (confirmed via a live Mongo query) because it was broken by the gemini-2.5-flash retirement (item 52) until just fixed -- building a bull/bear debate pipeline on top of a tab with no real usage history yet contradicts this project's own established trust-floor discipline. Recommended waiting until real signals accumulate now that the tab actually works; the user can revisit this once there's real prospective data. Also explicitly deferred: a full mechanical trailing-stop redesign of the exit rule itself (would touch the validated backtest engine for every strategy) -- Phase 1's LLM overlay is a lower-risk first step toward the same underlying "hold past target" question the user asked about.

    Verified via Streamlit AppTest (0 exceptions, full dashboard including the new Position Review overlay and richer LLM Agent tab context) and a live dashboard restart. `git status --porcelain` + stray-artifact cleanup before this writeup, matching this session's standing habit.

54. [DONE -- 2 real production bugs found + fixed, plus new automation] Diagnosed and fixed the GitHub Actions `daily_run.yml` failures the user reported, then automated the LLM Agent tab into `ingest.py`'s scheduled scan (per the user's "yes" to accelerating prospective LLM validation, since true backtesting of an LLM signal is not achievable here -- see the discussion below).

    BUG 1 -- stale duplicate unique index (root cause of the reported `DuplicateKeyError: ... index: ticker_1_signal_date_1 dup key: {ticker: "DXCM", signal_date: "2026-08-11"}`). `Trade_Signals`/`Trade_Outcomes` both had TWO unique indexes simultaneously: the correct, migrated `(ticker, signal_date, strategy)` index AND a stale `(ticker, signal_date)` index that was apparently never dropped when that migration happened (see the original migration note in [[trading-strategy-status]]). MongoDB enforces every unique index independently, so any two different strategies flagging the same ticker on the same day collided against the stale 2-field index even though the correct 3-field index would have allowed it -- confirmed by directly listing the live indexes via `db[...].list_indexes()`. Fixed by dropping `ticker_1_signal_date_1` from both collections (confirmed the underlying `ensure_indexes()` in `storage/signals.py`/`storage/outcomes.py` only ever creates the correct 3-field index, so this won't regress on the next scheduled run). Confirmed with the user before touching the live Atlas cluster (AskUserQuestion) since it's a production infrastructure change, even though safe (removes a redundant, strictly-subsumed index, no data touched).

    BUG 2 -- `ingest.py`'s own local `_score_for_strategy()` dispatch function was never updated when squeeze_breakout/ma_crossover were promoted into `config_loader.SECONDARY_STRATEGY_VERSIONS`. It's a separate, duplicated copy of `dip_buy_analyzer.py`'s own dispatch function (last touched in `f8f86de`, back when breakout_retest/week52_high were the only secondaries) that fell out of sync -- any day either strategy had a real signal, `run_secondary_strategy()` would crash with `KeyError: 'Oversold_Streak_Days'` (falling through to the RSI-only `add_trade_score()`, which expects a column neither strategy's levels dict carries) before ever reaching MongoDB. Reproduced directly (confirmed the exact crash via a real end-to-end call to `run_secondary_strategy()`), then fixed by mirroring `dip_buy_analyzer.py`'s complete 8-branch dispatch exactly. Verified fixed via the same reproduction (both strategies now score cleanly) and a full CLI dry run (`python ingest.py --watchlist <small test list>`, exit 0, no crashes).

    LLM AGENT AUTOMATION: new `ingest.py::run_llm_agent()`, a headless counterpart to the dashboard's "LLM Agent" tab -- same candidate-selection (tickers any mechanical strategy flagged above Ignore, capped at `MAX_LLM_CANDIDATES=10`, ranked by Trade_Score), same context-building (fundamentals, headlines, macro snapshot, the new qualitative snapshot from item 53), same `llm_agent.evaluate_ticker()` call, same Trade_Signals logging convention (`strategy="llm_agent"`, "Hold" mapped to "Watch", never capital-allocated). Called unconditionally at the end of `run()`, silently no-ops if `llm_agent.is_available()` is False (no keys configured) -- same "degrade quietly" convention as everything else optional in this pipeline, no new CLI flag needed. Verified via a real forced-candidate test (synthetic Buy-signal row -> real LLM call -> real MongoDB write, all succeeded) plus the full dry run above (correctly reported "nothing to evaluate" on a day with no real candidates in the small test watchlist).

    Also updated `.github/workflows/daily_run.yml` to pass `GEMINI_API_KEY`/`GROQ_API_KEY` through as optional secrets (same safe no-op pattern as the existing per-strategy `DISCORD_WEBHOOK_URL_*` secrets) -- **this automation will NOT actually run in GitHub Actions until the user adds these two secrets in the repo's Settings -> Secrets and variables -> Actions** (I can't do this myself, no access to GitHub's secrets UI). The code-level wiring is complete and correct either way; the secrets are the one remaining manual step.

    Context for WHY this automation was chosen over an actual LLM backtest: the user asked to find a way to backtest/validate the LLM side. Two independent, structural reasons rule that out with the current stack -- (1) training-data lookahead (a general-purpose LLM prompted with "historical" context may already know the outcome from its own training data, and this can't be fully engineered around), and (2) no point-in-time data (yfinance's news/analyst/insider/options/filings endpoints only return CURRENT state, so even the non-LLM parts of the qualitative context can't be reconstructed for a past date at all). Neither is a solvable engineering problem within a free-tier-only data stack. The one real lever is what's built here: the LLM Agent tab was dashboard-only, meaning its own prospective trust-floor clock (20-30 settled trades, 4-6 weeks, see [[llm-agent-status]]) was gated on someone remembering to open the dashboard, not real calendar time. Automating it into the daily scheduled scan removes that gap -- the fastest honest path to a trustworthy answer, given a real backtest isn't available.

55. [DONE -- 2 workstreams, per explicit user prioritization] Dual-provider LLM cross-validation (built, verified, shipped) and a trailing-stop exit redesign for the 3 active mechanical strategies (built, tested, honest negative result). Planned via EnterPlanMode after the user explicitly deprioritized new signal families in favor of enhancing what's already live, chosen between two options each via AskUserQuestion.

    WORKSTREAM B -- DUAL-PROVIDER LLM CROSS-VALIDATION (shipped): `llm_agent.py`'s `evaluate_ticker()`/`evaluate_holding()` now call BOTH Gemini and Groq whenever both are configured (not fallback-only like before), combined via a new shared `_resolve_dual()`: agreement -> use it with confidence averaged across both; disagreement -> default to the MORE CONSERVATIVE call (`Avoid`>`Hold`>`Buy`; `Take Profit`>`Hold For More`), extending `evaluate_holding()`'s own existing "default to caution" philosophy to also cover cross-provider disagreement. Both functions' return dicts gain 4 new, purely additive keys (`provider_agreement`, `secondary_provider`, `secondary_decision`, `secondary_confidence`) -- every existing key unchanged, nothing downstream broke. `storage/signals.py::_build_document()` gained the same 4 fields as optional (`None` for every non-LLM signal) -- the concrete mechanism that lets a future analysis of settled llm_agent trades check whether provider agreement correlated with better outcomes, not just a nicer prompt today. Wired into both dashboard call sites (LLM Agent tab + Position Review overlay, agreement surfaced in the UI text) and `ingest.py::run_llm_agent()`.

    Verified via a 25-check hand-computable unit test of `_resolve_dual()` in isolation (every branch: only-one-available x2, agree, disagree in both directions, for both schemas -- a real disagreement can't be reliably forced from live models on demand) plus real live calls. **Real finding during verification, not a bug**: `gemini-3.5-flash`'s free tier caps at 20 requests/MINUTE (separate from any daily quota) -- repeated rapid manual testing this session kept re-triggering it, and every time, the system correctly, silently degraded to Groq-only (`provider_agreement=None`), never crashing or hanging. Confirmed this matters more for bursty manual testing than real scheduled production use (`MAX_LLM_CANDIDATES=10`/day, naturally spaced by other work per candidate). Full Streamlit AppTest (0 exceptions) + a real forced-candidate test through `ingest.py::run_llm_agent()` confirming all 4 new fields land correctly in a real `Trade_Signals` Mongo document + live dashboard restart.

    WORKSTREAM A -- TRAILING-STOP EXIT (built, tested, honest negative result -- ships disabled): new `swingtrade/settlement.py::settle_trade_with_trailing()`, a hybrid design -- a trade behaves EXACTLY like the existing `settle_trade()` (fixed stop/target) until the target is FIRST reached, then switches to trailing a stop `trailing_stop_atr_multiplier x ATR` below the running post-target high (ratchets up only) instead of exiting automatically. Deliberately minimally-invasive: trades that never reach target (the majority) are untouched, isolating the change's effect cleanly. New `trailing_stop_enabled`/`trailing_stop_atr_multiplier` config fields (default `False`/`1.5`), wired via a new shared `_settle()` dispatch helper into the 6 relevant functions (breakout/squeeze_breakout/ma_crossover, real + random-entry counterparts each) -- the 4 dormant strategies untouched.

    **Caught and fixed a real bug during unit verification, not after**: the original implementation recomputed the trailing stop using the CURRENT bar's own high BEFORE checking whether that same bar's Open had gapped below the PREVIOUS bar's trailing level -- a look-ahead-within-the-bar sequencing error (you can't gap through a stop level that only exists because of a high you haven't seen yet that same session). Fixed by reordering: check the Open against yesterday's trailing level first, THEN fold in today's own high. Caught by a 20-check synthetic-price-path unit test (never-reaches-target byte-identical to `settle_trade()`, climbs-after-target captures more profit, reverses-hard still resolves sanely not stuck OPEN, gap-up-through-target starts trailing correctly, grinds-to-max-holding-days resolves honestly) -- all 20 passed after the fix. Real-data no-op regression also confirmed (`trailing_stop_enabled=False` produces byte-identical trades to before this change existed, on real AAPL data across all 3 strategies).

    Cheap 1-year WFO screen (trailing_stop_atr_multiplier 1.0/1.5/2.0/2.5 vs. baseline) across all 3 active strategies: **breakout and ma_crossover -- no multiplier beat baseline on both TUNE and HOLD, clean reject for both.** squeeze_breakout at mult=1.0 looked genuinely promising on the cheap screen (TUNE nearly flat -0.132->-0.129, HOLD meaningfully better 0.08->0.132, non-thin) -- NOT the alarming "TUNE way up/HOLD craters" shape flagged repeatedly this session, so it earned the full check rather than being dismissed. **Full 5-year check: the improvement evaporates into noise.** REAL-trailing vs. REAL-baseline over the full window: HOLDOUT sharpe 0.038 vs. 0.041 (statistically indistinguishable), TUNE 0.033 vs. 0.031 (also indistinguishable, if anything marginally worse) -- the promising 1-year HOLD number was a smaller-sample artifact, the exact pattern this session has hit repeatedly with entry-side filters, now confirmed to also apply to an exit-side change. REAL-trailing vs. RANDOM-trailing confirmed entry timing still holds under the new exit structure (ALL/HOLDOUT clear wins, TUNE an exact tie -- consistent with squeeze_breakout's already-known softer TUNE-cut regime, not a new concern). **`trailing_stop_enabled` ships disabled (default) for all 3 strategies -- no live config touched.** The underlying mechanism (a real, working, unit-tested trailing-exit implementation) remains available for a future, more targeted attempt (e.g. per-strategy multipliers, or applying from entry rather than only post-target) if ever revisited.

    Sequencing: Workstream B (smaller, contained) built and verified first, then Workstream A (larger, touches the validated backtest engine for 3 live strategies) -- matching the plan's own stated risk-ordering. `git status --porcelain` + stray-artifact cleanup, results written up honestly either way, nothing enabled on a live config without clearing full validation, matching this session's standing discipline throughout.

[DONE -- 2 parts, per explicit user request "accuracy and reliability enhancement"] Failure alerting fix + a lightweight CI regression test suite, directly motivated by this session's own two silent production bugs (item 54's stale Mongo index + `ingest.py` scoring-dispatch desync) -- both went unnoticed for "the last few" scheduled runs before the user happened to check manually.

    PART 1 -- FAILURE ALERTING: root cause of the silence, confirmed directly in the pasted failure log: `[notify] no webhook configured, message not sent` -- `daily_run.py`'s own always-fires run-status report already existed, but its ONLY channel (`notifications.notify_with_file`, Discord) depends on a `DISCORD_WEBHOOK_URL` secret that was never configured, so every run (including failures) was completely invisible outside the raw job log. Fixed with a SECOND, independent channel that needs zero setup: new `notifications.notify_github_summary()` writes to GitHub Actions' own `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY` (a native feature -- renders as markdown directly on the workflow run's summary page, no webhook/secret required). `daily_run.py` now calls this alongside the existing Discord attempt on every run, and on failure specifically also prints a `::error title=daily_run.py FAILED::...` annotation -- a recognized GitHub Actions syntax that renders a visible red error on the run page and is what feeds GitHub's own free "email me when a scheduled workflow fails" account setting (Settings -> Notifications -> Actions -- a zero-code toggle only the user can flip, flagged to them directly). Discord stays fully supported and unchanged if/when configured -- this is additive, not a replacement. Verified via a real local dry run: `daily_run.main()` with `run_step` mocked to simulate failure correctly wrote the GitHub step summary, printed the `::error::` line, and returned exit code 1, with Discord's own no-op message printing exactly as before (confirming both channels fire independently and neither depends on the other).

    PART 2 -- LIGHTWEIGHT CI REGRESSION SUITE: new `tests/` directory (`pytest`, not previously a dependency, added to `requirements.txt` + a `pytest.ini`), new `.github/workflows/tests.yml` running on every push/PR (separate from `daily_run.yml`'s daily schedule -- a signal within minutes of a bad push, not a day later). Every test targets a REAL bug class from this session, not hypothetical coverage:
    - `test_strategy_dispatch_parity.py` -- **the exact bug class that broke `ingest.py` silently.** For all 9 strategies, builds one REAL levels row (via the actual `swingtrade.compute_*_levels()` functions against a synthetic-but-realistic offline OHLCV fixture, not a hand-typed dict -- see `conftest.py`) and asserts `dip_buy_analyzer._score_for_strategy()` and `ingest._score_for_strategy()` agree. **Proven to actually catch the real bug**: temporarily reverted `ingest.py`'s dispatch to its old broken 4-branch version, re-ran the suite -- it failed with the exact real `KeyError: 'Oversold_Streak_Days'` for exactly the 4 strategies that were actually broken (momentum_burst/squeeze_breakout/adx_trend_entry/ma_crossover), then restored the fix and confirmed all 9 pass again.
    - `test_mongo_indexes.py` -- **the exact bug class behind the DuplicateKeyError.** Read-only: asserts `Trade_Signals`/`Trade_Outcomes` each have exactly the correct 3-field unique index and no stale 2-field leftover.
    - `test_imports.py` -- all 14 top-level scripts import cleanly (catches syntax/import regressions immediately).
    - `test_config_candidates_load.py` -- every version in `SECONDARY_STRATEGY_VERSIONS`/`EXPERIMENTAL_STRATEGY_VERSIONS` (plus the active config) loads into a valid `TradingConfig` from Mongo.
    - `test_llm_resolve_dual.py` / `test_settlement_trailing.py` -- this session's own ad-hoc verification scripts (item 55's `_resolve_dual()` 25-check and `settle_trade_with_trailing()` 20-check unit tests), promoted into permanent pytest coverage rather than left as one-off scratchpad scripts, plus a new real-data no-op-regression test for all 3 active strategies added directly in pytest form.

    All Mongo-touching tests are strictly read-only and skip gracefully (not fail) if `MONGODB_URI` isn't reachable, same "degrade, don't break the run" philosophy as the rest of this codebase. Full suite: 45 passed, 1 skipped (the `EXPERIMENTAL_STRATEGY_VERSIONS` parametrize is currently empty), in 1.3 seconds locally.

    Explicitly out of scope for this round, per the plan's own stated boundary: the dead-man's-switch gap `daily_run.py`'s own docstring already honestly flags ("if the scheduler itself doesn't fire, nothing here can detect that") -- still open, e.g. healthchecks.io's free tier, if the user wants it later.

[DONE -- 2 parts, per explicit user request "scope both"] Real ticker-universe expansion (`watchlist.txt` 266 -> 408) plus prompt-variant A/B testing for the LLM Agent, in response to the user pasting an external write-up ("The Category Error of LLM Backtesting") proposing 4 acceleration ideas. Of the 4: #2 (open the mechanical funnel to Watch-tier) was already shipped in item 53; #3 (a shadow intraday pipeline) was rejected as needing its own independent mechanical-layer validation on a different timeframe. Scoped and built #1 and #4 via `EnterPlanMode`.

    PART 1 -- TICKER-UNIVERSE EXPANSION: a live scan before this change found only 8 unique LLM Agent candidates across the prior 266-ticker watchlist on a given day (well under `MAX_LLM_CANDIDATES=10`) -- confirming candidate SCARCITY, not the cap, was the real bottleneck on how fast the LLM Agent's prospective trust-floor (20-30 settled trades, 4-6 weeks) accumulates. Used `sp500_historical_components.csv` (already in the repo for survivorship-bias-free backtesting) to find 277 real, current S&P 500 constituents not yet in the watchlist -- real data instead of hand-picking, since S&P 500 membership itself already implies a liquidity/market-cap floor. A one-time enrichment script (not part of the daily pipeline) fetched sector + market cap for all 277 via `yfinance.Ticker.info`, then selected 142 of them proportionally by sector share and ranked by market cap within each sector (dropped one obvious redundant dual-share-class pick, `FOX`, in favor of `CHTR`). Real gaps this surfaced in the prior hand-curated list: `TSLA` and `PYPL` were both missing entirely. `watchlist.txt` now has 408 tickers (verified: no duplicates, valid JSON, same compact one-line-per-entry format preserved rather than reformatting the whole file). `MAX_LLM_CANDIDATES` stays at 10 unchanged -- the goal is widening the funnel INTO the existing cap, not raising cost/rate-limit exposure. `daily_run.yml`'s `timeout-minutes` bumped 30 -> 45: a live-timed 20-ticker sample measured 2.93s/ticker average (fetch + `REQUEST_DELAY_SEC=0.5` + indicator compute), so 408 tickers extrapolates to roughly 20 minutes of fetch time alone, against the old 30-minute budget.

    PART 2 -- PROMPT-VARIANT A/B TESTING: since a real LLM backtest is structurally impossible (see item 55's write-up), the only way to compare prompt-framing choices is to run several concurrently against the SAME real candidates and let real settled trades decide. New `llm_agent.PROMPT_VARIANTS` dict maps a variant name to its prompt-builder: `"balanced"` (the original prompt, unchanged, keeps `strategy="llm_agent"` with no suffix so its already-accumulating trust-floor data isn't fragmented), `"qualitative_weighted"` (new `_build_prompt_qualitative_weighted()` -- tells the model to treat the mechanical Trade_Score as a starting filter only and weigh whether the qualitative/analyst/insider/macro picture confirms or contradicts it as the primary evidence), `"evidence_strict"` (new `_build_prompt_evidence_strict()` -- requires at least one specific, named data point to justify "Buy", defaulting harder to "Hold"/"Avoid" on generically-positive-but-vague setups). All three reuse the exact same context-rendering helpers (`_build_qualitative_block()`, headline/macro blocks) -- only the instructional framing differs, no duplicated data-plumbing. `evaluate_ticker()` gained a `variant: str = "balanced"` param. New shared `llm_agent.variant_strategy_name(variant)` maps a variant to its Trade_Signals `strategy` string (`"llm_agent"` for balanced, `"llm_agent_{variant}"` for challengers) -- deliberately a SINGLE function both `ingest.py` and `dip_buy_analyzer.py` call, not a duplicated mapping in each (exactly the kind of duplication that caused item 54's `ingest.py` dispatch-desync bug).

    Variants run in `ingest.py::run_llm_agent()`'s headless automation only, NOT the dashboard's live tab -- the dashboard is the user's actual daily-decision surface, and showing 3 differently-worded verdicts per ticker there would be confusing UX with no benefit today. `ingest.py` now builds each candidate's context ONCE and evaluates it under all `PROMPT_VARIANTS` (no extra fetch cost, only extra LLM calls -- worst case 10 candidates x 3 variants x 2 providers = 60 calls/day, far inside Gemini's 1,500/day and Groq's 250/day free tiers), logging each variant to its own strategy string so they settle completely independently (`settle_trades.py` needed zero changes -- confirmed it already walks every unsettled `Trade_Signals` doc using whatever `config_snapshot["strategy"]` says, no hardcoded strategy list). The dashboard's "Validation progress" section (previously hardcoded to `strategy="llm_agent"` only) generalized into a per-variant loop showing settled-trade count/days-elapsed/win-rate for all 3 variants side by side -- pure generalization of existing code, not new logic.

    Verified: full pytest suite (45 passed, 1 skipped, unchanged). Since no local `GEMINI_API_KEY`/`GROQ_API_KEY` are set in this dev environment (only configured as GitHub Actions secrets), live-network verification used a mocked `_call_gemini` returning a variant-derived response, confirming each of the 3 variants genuinely builds a distinct prompt (checked for each variant's unique marker text) and routes to its own strategy name. Real Mongo write-path test: logged all 3 variants' signals for a disposable `TESTXYZ` ticker, confirmed all 3 wrote as separate documents with zero `DuplicateKeyError`, then deleted the test documents. Confirmed the new dashboard query block runs cleanly against real data in both states -- `balanced` already had 5 real settled outcomes (60% win rate), the 2 new challenger variants correctly showed "0 settled trades yet". `py_compile` + fresh import checks on all 3 edited files; Streamlit server confirmed booting with zero exceptions (full interactive browser click-through wasn't done -- no browser tool available in this session; recommend the user does a quick visual check of the LLM Agent tab's new per-variant comparison view themselves). `git status --porcelain` + stray-artifact cleanup (6 zero-byte shell-escaping fragments removed, the intermediate `sp500_candidates_enriched.json` research file relocated out of the repo root to scratchpad).

59. [DONE -- infrastructure sound, 2 real candidates found, NOT promoted, pending user review] Re-ran Optuna for squeeze_breakout (v39) and ma_crossover (v49) -- the 2 strategies actually carrying real capital -- under the fully-corrected objective for the first time (RRR_FLOOR + recency-weighting + correlation-adjustment + ticker-holdout + multi-objective drawdown-awareness, all together). Per explicit user request ("accuracy and reliability" focus, then "let's go with the optuna rerun"), scoped via `EnterPlanMode` to these 2 strategies specifically (breakout/v43 excluded -- 6 already-failed re-tune attempts on record, $0 default pool).

    PART A -- FIXED A REAL GAP IN THE EXISTING MULTI-OBJECTIVE INFRASTRUCTURE (`optimize.py`): item 23's own multi-objective/drawdown-aware search (built earlier, NOT new this round) had a documented, still-open weakness -- a trial that clears `MIN_TRADES_FOR_SCORE=50` but is still much thinner than other trials (e.g. 55 vs. 300) reported its RAW `max_drawdown` with no tapering, since fewer trades mechanically means less time for a bad losing streak to compound. New `taper_drawdown_for_sample_size()` + `MIN_TRADES_FOR_TRUSTED_DRAWDOWN=100` (2x the floor): linearly tapers the reported drawdown toward the full `UNDER_SAMPLED_DRAWDOWN_PENALTY` as `effective_trade_count` approaches 50 from above, full trust only at 100+. Only affects `--multi-objective` mode; unit-tested (boundary + midpoint cases) and confirmed this actually mattered in the real run below (squeeze_breakout's winning trial landed at 51.2 effective trades -- right at the floor -- and its drawdown was correctly reported as 98.98%, heavily distrusted, not a falsely-reassuring low number).

    PART B -- WIRED `ma_crossover` INTO `optimize.py` FOR THE FIRST TIME: it had never been searched by Optuna at all (only manual one-at-a-time grid search on its 2 window params, tp/sl always held fixed -- item 50). New `MA_CROSSOVER_SHORT_WINDOW_RANGE`/`MA_CROSSOVER_LONG_WINDOW_RANGE` (same bounds item 50's grid search already validated as reasonable) + a new `elif strategy == "ma_crossover":` branch, `--strategy` choices list and docstring updated. `ma_crossover_entry_fill`/`ma_crossover_strength_cap_pct`/`ma_crossover_earnings_gate` deliberately excluded from the search space (structural/already-investigated-and-rejected choices, or scoring-display-only, verified by reading `add_ma_crossover_trade_score()` before wiring). Smoke-tested at `--trials 3` before committing to a full run.

    **REAL BUG FOUND AND FIXED, discovered because this was the first real use of `--pin-atr-take-profit-multiplier`/`--pin-stop-loss-atr-multiplier` through this exact CLI candidate-writing path**: `main()` built the written candidate config as `{**DEFAULT_CONFIG, **best.params}` -- but Optuna's own `trial.params` NEVER contains tp/sl when both pin args are given (pinned values are hardcoded constants inside `objective()`, never passed through `trial.suggest_*()`), so the candidate silently fell back to `DEFAULT_CONFIG`'s tp/sl (2.0/1.0) instead of the pinned values (3.0/1.0) every trial in the search was actually scored under. Caught by manually inspecting the written candidate (v50)'s stored params and noticing they didn't match the `--pin-*` flags just passed. Fixed by factoring candidate-construction into a new `build_candidate_config()` (independently unit-tested, including a regression test reproducing the exact bug) that explicitly re-applies the pinned values after the `best.params` merge. **v50's already-written Mongo document still has the wrong tp/sl (2.0/1.0) -- direct correction was attempted and blocked by the permission system as a production-data write (reasonable, even for a non-active candidate doc); flagged to the user rather than worked around. All verification below used the CORRECT config (3.0/1.0, reconstructed from the real trial params) instead of trusting the stored doc.**

    RESULTS -- both searches used the full 408-ticker watchlist, `--multi-objective`, 45 trials, seed=42, default 1-year TUNE/HOLDOUT window (this is optimize.py's own "cheap screen" stage, not the final answer -- see below):
    - squeeze_breakout: tp/sl PINNED at v39's own values (3.0/1.0) -- 2 of this project's 6 documented failed re-tunes were squeeze_breakout with tp/sl searched freely, so pinning was applied proactively this time rather than risking a 3rd failure. Winning trial (written as System_Config v50, filters only: `zscore_max=-1.826, lookback_days=14, gain_pct_min=3.263, rsi_overbought_threshold=99.69, relative_strength_min=-12.22, volume_ratio_min=0.527, adx_min=12.72, obv_zscore_min=-0.553`) had TUNE effective_trade_count=51.2 -- right at the floor, a real red flag per validation-pipeline step 10 -- and traded 58 times vs. baseline's 716 on the same TUNE tickers, a ~12x frequency collapse.
    - ma_crossover: all 4 dimensions (short/long window + tp/sl) searched freely for the first time -- much lower-dimensional, much healthier search than squeeze_breakout's 9D+2 space (no trial hit the under-sampled penalty at all, unlike squeeze_breakout where most did). Winning trial (written as System_Config v51: `short_window=17, long_window=81, atr_take_profit_multiplier=1.035, stop_loss_atr_multiplier=0.621`) had a genuinely healthy TUNE sample (181 effective trades) but REVERSED against baseline on the 1-year HOLDOUT cut (candidate sharpe_like=-0.022 vs. baseline's +0.037) -- the classic TUNE-wins/HOLDOUT-loses overfitting shape this project has been burned by before. v51's RRR (1.6675, just above the 1.6 floor) gives a best-case live Trade_Score of 61.1 -- clears `signal_buy_threshold=60`, but by only 1.1 points vs. v49's current 6.7-point buffer (66.7), meaning v51 would in practice fire far fewer LIVE Buy signals than the backtest's raw trade count suggests, since most real crossovers won't land at the absolute best-case Signal_Strength_Pct.

    MANDATORY VALIDATION (pipeline steps 3/5/9/10/11 -- the actual gate, not the WFO screen above), full 5-year `benchmark_random_entry.py` run against each candidate's EXACT params, same 408-ticker universe, same seed, monkeypatching `load_config_to_test()` to bypass its "active-config-only" design (new pattern -- reused verbatim otherwise, zero duplicated comparison logic) rather than the WFO numbers alone:
    - **v50 (squeeze_breakout) PASSES cleanly**: REAL beats RANDOM on ALL (0.126 vs -0.035), TUNE (0.092 vs -0.019), AND HOLDOUT (0.258 vs -0.172) -- healthy non-thin samples throughout (577/452/147 effective trades), a much better result than the 1-year WFO screen alone suggested (exactly why step 11 exists). Head-to-head against v39 on the IDENTICAL universe/window/seed: v50 beats v39 on all 3 cuts (ALL 0.126 vs 0.019, TUNE 0.092 vs 0.005, HOLDOUT 0.258 vs 0.038) -- a real, consistent margin. **But v50 fires ~11x less often than v39 (658 vs. 7,347 trades over the same 5-year window)** -- the same frequency-collapse red flag validation-pipeline step 8 already rejected once before (v33, pre-RRR-fix) for defeating squeeze_breakout's entire "fires often" reason for existing.
    - **v51 (ma_crossover) ALSO PASSES cleanly**: REAL beats RANDOM on ALL (0.029 vs -0.019), TUNE (0.029 vs -0.001), AND HOLDOUT (0.06 vs -0.065) -- healthy samples throughout (1940/1505/528 effective trades). The HOLDOUT reversal seen in the 1-year WFO screen (candidate vs. DEFAULT_CONFIG) did NOT reproduce in the proper 5-year real-vs-random check -- a real, concrete example of the WFO screen and the actual gate disagreeing, exactly the scenario step 11 exists to catch (the 5-year check is authoritative). Head-to-head against v49 on the IDENTICAL universe/window/seed: v51 beats v49 on all 3 cuts (ALL 0.029 vs 0.003, TUNE 0.029 vs 0.019, HOLDOUT 0.06 vs -0.038) -- and **only a ~21% trade-frequency reduction (2,206 vs. 2,823)**, a much healthier trade-off than squeeze_breakout's.

    **SEPARATE, ARGUABLY MORE IMPORTANT FINDING, independent of either candidate**: both CURRENTLY LIVE, real-capital-eligible secondary strategies have visibly weakened on today's expanded 408-ticker universe / rolled-forward 5-year window compared to their original validation on the smaller 253/266-ticker watchlist. **v49 (ma_crossover, live today) now LOSES to random-entry timing on ALL (0.003 vs 0.030) and TUNE (0.019 vs 0.042), and ties on HOLDOUT (-0.038 vs -0.038)** -- no longer a clean pass. **v39 (squeeze_breakout, live today) is near-tied on ALL (0.019 vs 0.017) and LOSES to random on TUNE (0.005 vs 0.039)**, winning only on HOLDOUT. Neither strategy was touched -- this is a measurement, not an action -- but it directly means the two strategies currently carrying real capital are not as clearly validated on TODAY's universe/window as they were when originally promoted.

    **NOTHING PROMOTED. v50 and v51 remain `status=candidate` in Mongo, not active, no live config touched, per this session's standing rule.** Both cleanly pass the mandatory random-entry gate and both beat their current live counterparts on a fair identical-universe comparison -- but v50's ~11x frequency collapse and v51's thin post-tapering TUNE sample (51.2, in v50's case) / razor-thin live-scoring margin (v51) are real, unresolved tradeoffs, not resolved by this write-up, that the user should weigh before deciding whether to promote either via `python promote_config.py --promote 50` / `--promote 51`. The weakening of v39/v49 on the current universe is flagged as its own open finding, not yet investigated further (candidate follow-up: is this a real regime change, a dilution effect from the wider/more-recent ticker universe, or something else -- not yet determined).

61. [DONE -- infrastructure sound, mixed candidate found, NOT promoted, pending user review] squeeze_breakout follow-up, scoped and built per explicit user request ("scope out a follow up for squeeze since it sounds like its worth looking into") after v50 (item 59) was rejected for collapsing trade frequency ~11x.

    ROOT CAUSE FIX: `optimize.py`'s squeeze_breakout search has 8 free filter/trigger dimensions searched simultaneously with nothing in the objective penalizing reduced firing frequency -- Optuna has a structural incentive to stack tight filters together, since a smaller, more selective sample naturally looks better on risk-adjusted metrics alone (the same fundamental selection-bias pattern already seen with thin-sample overfitting elsewhere, here manifesting via filter tightness). New `--min-frequency-fraction FLOAT` CLI flag (default 0.0, disabled): rejects any trial whose own TUNE-set `effective_trade_count` falls below this fraction of `DEFAULT_CONFIG`'s baseline `effective_trade_count` on the same tickers -- reuses the existing `UNDER_SAMPLED_PENALTY`/`UNDER_SAMPLED_DRAWDOWN_PENALTY` return path (new `is_below_frequency_floor()` helper) rather than a new penalty scale or a true 3rd Optuna objective. Applies to every strategy uniformly (general search-quality concern, not squeeze_breakout-specific code) but defaults to disabled so no other strategy's search behavior changes unless explicitly requested. Unit-tested (3 new tests: disabled-by-default, rejects-below-threshold, accepts-at-or-above). Byte-for-byte non-regression proof for the flag-omitted case (same stash/run/unstash/re-run/diff method used twice already this session) -- exact match.

    SEARCH RESULTS: `--min-frequency-fraction 0.4` (40% of baseline) found NOTHING -- all 45 trials fell below the 195.4-effective-trade floor, meaning no combination of the 8 filter dimensions this search explored could retain even 40% of baseline's firing frequency while also improving sharpe_like/drawdown. Retried at `--min-frequency-fraction 0.2` (97.5-effective-trade floor) -- found System_Config v52: `zscore_max=-0.471, lookback_days=3, gain_pct_min=5.546, rsi_overbought_threshold=87.82, relative_strength_min=-21.64, volume_ratio_min=0.928, adx_min=2.18, obv_zscore_min=-1.669` (tp/sl pinned 3.0/1.0, same as v39/v50). TUNE effective_trade_count=129.0 (healthy, above both floors), HOLDOUT=53.5 (thinner but clears MIN_TRADES_FOR_SCORE). Unlike v51's initial WFO screen, TUNE and HOLDOUT both improved over baseline in the SAME direction here (sharpe_like 0.046/0.065 vs baseline's -0.054/-0.004) -- no reversal shape.

    MANDATORY VALIDATION (5-year `benchmark_random_entry.py` against v52's exact stored params, same 408-ticker universe/seed as item 59): **v52 cleanly beats RANDOM on ALL (0.042 vs -0.002) and TUNE (0.05 vs -0.01), and narrowly beats RANDOM on HOLDOUT (0.016 vs 0.002)** -- passes the mandatory gate, non-thin samples throughout (1,028/769/336 effective trades). Fires 1,351 times over 5 years -- a ~5.4x reduction vs. v39's 7,347 (meaningfully better retention than v50's ~11x collapse, roughly 2x more trades retained). **But head-to-head against v39 on the identical universe/window/seed is genuinely mixed, not a clean sweep like v50 was**: v52 beats v39 on ALL (0.042 vs 0.019) and TUNE (0.05 vs 0.005), but **LOSES to v39 on HOLDOUT (0.016 vs 0.038)** -- the one cut that's most honestly out-of-sample (tickers Optuna never saw during tuning). v39 itself already has a weak/mixed record against random on today's universe (item 59) -- this isn't "a clearly better replacement vs. a clean baseline," it's "a mixed candidate vs. an already-mixed baseline."

    **NOT PROMOTED. v52 remains `status=candidate` in Mongo, squeeze_breakout stays on v39, per this session's standing rule.** The frequency-floor mechanism worked exactly as designed and is a genuinely useful, reusable addition to `optimize.py` regardless of this specific outcome -- but this particular result is a real tradeoff (much better frequency than v50, weaker/mixed edge on the most important validation cut), not a clean win, and is presented to the user as such rather than recommended outright.

    Verified: full pytest suite (56 passed, 1 skipped -- 3 new tests this item). `git status --porcelain` + stray-artifact cleanup (6 zero-byte shell-escaping fragments removed across this session's later half, temporary verification scripts placed in project root for import resolution then removed).

62. [DONE -- strong candidate found (v53), NOT promoted, pending user review; real production bug fixed along the way] Continued the squeeze_breakout investigation per explicit user request: "run the 0.3 floor just in case and in the meanwhile... monitor and look into other strategies that could have a better edge than v39 or if we can do anything to enhance v39s edge on the expanded universe." Three parallel threads.

    THREAD 1 -- REAL PRODUCTION BUG FOUND AND FIXED (discovered while running the 0.3-floor search, not something being looked for): `ta.adx()` returns `None` (not a NaN-filled DataFrame) when given too little history to compute even one window -- crashed `precompute_breakout_frame()` (`swingtrade/levels.py:291`, used by breakout/squeeze_breakout/adx_trend_entry alike) with `TypeError: 'NoneType' object is not subscriptable`. Root cause: `EA` (Electronic Arts), a pre-existing watchlist entry that predates this session's own ticker-expansion work, returns only ~4-6 rows of history via both `market_data.fetch_ticker_bundle()` and `run_backtest.fetch_history()` -- confirmed via `yf.Ticker('EA').info`: `regularMarketPrice=None` while other metadata (name, market cap, sector) is still present, the classic signature of a delisted/take-private stock (EA's real 2025 buyout). **The LIVE production path (`market_data.score_bundle_for_strategy()`, what `dip_buy_analyzer.py`/`ingest.py` actually use) already has a per-ticker try/except and was NEVER at crash risk** -- confirmed directly: `EA` silently failed to score every day with `score_errors=[('EA', "'NoneType' object is not subscriptable'")]`, degrading exactly as this codebase's philosophy demands, not crashing the scan. But `optimize.py`/`benchmark_random_entry.py`'s direct-simulation paths have no equivalent protection and crashed twice on real work this session (once mid-holdout-validation on an already-completed 45-trial search, wasting ~15 minutes; once during a fresh ticker-cohort diagnostic). Two-part fix: (1) removed `EA` from `watchlist.txt` (genuinely delisted, no reason to keep scanning it -- 408 -> 407 tickers, same precedent as item 24's DFS/MMC delisting handling), (2) fixed the ROOT CAUSE in `precompute_breakout_frame()` itself -- if `ta.adx()` returns `None`, fill `df["ADX"]` with `NaN` instead of crashing (matches the existing "missing/NaN values don't exclude a signal on their own" convention already used for OBV/squeeze z-scores), protecting every current AND future caller (not just EA, any ticker with genuinely insufficient history -- a new IPO, a future delisting, a transient data gap). Verified: 2 new regression tests (`tests/test_insufficient_history_adx.py` -- a 6-row synthetic frame no longer crashes and correctly gets an all-NaN ADX column; a normal 260-row frame still computes real ADX values, proving the fix doesn't silently disable ADX for legitimate tickers). Also discovered (not yet acted on): yfinance intermittently returns "possibly delisted" for real, liquid, actively-traded tickers (VLO/WMB/KMI/HAL/DVN/ENB.TO/CNQ.TO/SU.TO/BKR/OKE all failed to fetch in one run, succeeded when tested individually/in a matched re-run) -- a known, previously-documented (item 22) transient yfinance flakiness pattern, not a new bug, not fixed here.

    THREAD 2 -- SQUEEZE_BREAKOUT FOLLOW-UP, 0.3 FREQUENCY FLOOR: filled the gap between 0.2 (item 61's v52, mixed vs. v39) and 0.4 (found nothing). Found System_Config **v53**: `zscore_max=-0.526, lookback_days=5, gain_pct_min=4.214, rsi_overbought_threshold=76.31, relative_strength_min=-31.82, volume_ratio_min=0.652, adx_min=7.20, obv_zscore_min=-2.314` (tp/sl pinned 3.0/1.0, same as v39/v50/v52). TUNE effective_trade_count=234.9, HOLDOUT=102.3 -- both genuinely healthy (well above both MIN_TRADES_FOR_SCORE=50 and MIN_TRADES_FOR_TRUSTED_DRAWDOWN=100), and TUNE/HOLDOUT both improved over baseline in the SAME direction (no reversal). **Mandatory 5-year `benchmark_random_entry.py` validation is the cleanest result of the three squeeze_breakout candidates found this session**: REAL beats RANDOM on ALL (0.043 vs 0.018), TUNE (0.029 vs 0.018), AND HOLDOUT (0.064 vs 0.009) -- a genuine clean sweep, unlike v50 (11x frequency collapse) or v52 (lost to v39 on holdout). Head-to-head against v39 on the IDENTICAL matched ticker universe (397 tickers, 10 dropped by a transient yfinance fetch hiccup this specific run -- re-verified v39 on the exact same 397-ticker set for a fair comparison, not the full 407): **v53 beats v39 on ALL (0.043 vs 0.015) and TUNE (0.029 vs 0.001), and is essentially TIED on HOLDOUT (0.064 vs 0.063)** -- v53 does not lose anywhere, unlike v52's holdout loss. Frequency: v53 retained ~36% of v39's raw trade count on this same comparison (2,576 vs 7,168) -- a real reduction, but far healthier than v50's ~9% or v52's ~24% retention. **This is the strongest candidate found across the whole squeeze_breakout investigation (items 59/61/62) -- beats v39 outright on 2 of 3 cuts, ties on the 3rd, with the best frequency retention of any candidate tried.**

    THREAD 3 -- OTHER STRATEGIES SURVEYED: spot-checked `adx_trend_entry` (v40, the one previously-shelved strategy with a real, never-fully-disproven Phase-1 edge -- deprioritized only for sequencing reasons, not a negative result) on today's expanded universe via the same 5-year real-vs-random benchmark. **Result: same modest/thin pattern it always had, not improved, not a viable v39 replacement.** ALL (0.034 vs 0.029) and TUNE (0.035 vs 0.031) show only a razor-thin margin over random (differences of ~0.004-0.005 on a sample this size is within noise), and it actually LOSES to random on HOLDOUT (-0.003 vs 0.007). Not pursued further.

    THREAD 4 -- WHY v39 WEAKENED, DIAGNOSED (not just measured this time): split the identical 5-year real-vs-random benchmark by ticker cohort -- v39 on ONLY the original 265 pre-expansion tickers (the exact universe it was validated on) vs. ONLY the 142 newly-added tickers. **Both a real regime shift AND a real dilution effect are true simultaneously, not one or the other:** on the ORIGINAL 265 tickers alone, v39's edge has ALREADY decayed to essentially a tie with random (REAL sharpe=0.037 vs RANDOM=0.039) -- this is NOT an artifact of the ticker-universe expansion, the strategy's own edge on its own original validated universe has weakened over time (a real regime shift). On the 142 NEW tickers specifically, v39 clearly LOSES to random (REAL=-0.021 vs RANDOM=0.039) -- the expansion also introduced tickers this specific strategy handles worse (a real, additional dilution effect on top of the regime shift). Both explanations are real and additive, not competing.

    **NOT PROMOTED. v53 remains `status=candidate` in Mongo, squeeze_breakout stays on v39, per this session's standing rule -- but v53 is presented as a genuinely strong, recommended candidate given how clean this result is, unlike the more equivocal v50/v52.** `promote_config.py`-style promotion doesn't apply to secondary strategies (see item 60) -- the actual mechanism, if the user approves, is swapping `config_loader.SECONDARY_STRATEGY_VERSIONS["Squeeze Breakout"]` from 39 to 53, same one-line pattern used for ma_crossover's own v49->v51 promotion.

    Verified: full pytest suite (58 passed, 1 skipped -- 2 new tests this item for the ADX-None fix). `git status --porcelain` + stray-artifact cleanup throughout (temporary verification/diagnostic scripts placed in project root for import resolution, removed after use).

    **Result: v43 LOSES to random-entry timing on ALL (sharpe_like 0.031 vs 0.039) and, more importantly, on HOLDOUT (0.007 vs 0.095 -- real, decisive, ~13x worse) -- winning only on TUNE (0.037 vs 0.016).** This is a real reversal from v43's last-recorded check (item 44, 2026-08-10, on the smaller pre-expansion universe): back then TUNE lost and HOLDOUT nominally "won" only because random was negative; today the pattern has flipped -- TUNE wins, HOLDOUT (the most honestly out-of-sample cut) now loses decisively. Not investigated further this round (out of scope for the question asked) -- flagged as a real, current finding, not a stale concern being repeated.

64. [DONE] Annualized Sharpe + win_rate as an explicit selection gate, per explicit user request after questioning why no config's `sharpe_like` ever exceeded ~0.26 when they expected figures above 1.5-2.

    ROOT CAUSE, CONFIRMED BY READING THE ACTUAL CODE: `sharpe_like = mean(pnl_pct) / std(pnl_pct)` (`swingtrade/backtest.py`) is computed on the RAW PER-TRADE return distribution -- it was never annualized. The "Sharpe > 1.5" benchmark almost always refers to an ANNUALIZED Sharpe (scaled by `sqrt(periods per year)`), so comparing the two directly all session was apples-to-oranges, not necessarily evidence the strategies are weak.

    PART A -- ANNUALIZED SHARPE: new `_annualize_sharpe()` helper (`swingtrade/backtest.py`), shared by `summarize_trades()` and `summarize_trades_weighted()`, both now additionally return `trades_per_year` and `annualized_sharpe_like` (`sharpe_like * sqrt(trades_per_year)`, the standard trade-frequency annualization convention, same explicit "approximation, not a compounding portfolio simulation" caveat `compute_max_drawdown()` already carries). Computed from the pooled trades' own `entry_date` span -- purely additive, no signature changes, so all ~15 existing call sites (`optimize.py`, `benchmark_random_entry.py`, `run_backtest.py`, `settle_trades.py`, `evaluate_config.py`, `check_survivorship_bias.py`) get the new fields for free. `optimize.py`'s Pareto-front/best-trial print lines updated to show `annualized_sharpe_like`/`win_rate` inline (previously buried in the full metrics dict).

    **Real bug caught during verification, not by inspection**: `optimize.py::report_live_outcomes_context()` pools live `Trade_Outcomes` Mongo documents into a minimal `{"status", "pnl_pct"}` shape with no `entry_date` at all (those documents use a differently-shaped `signal_date` string instead) -- crashed with `KeyError: 'entry_date'` the moment this ran for real. Fixed by making `_annualize_sharpe()` degrade gracefully (return `None, None`) when trades don't carry the field, matching this codebase's established "missing data doesn't crash the summary" convention everywhere else. Caught by the same real-script verification run this session has used throughout, not just unit tests -- a good reminder that a plan's own "no signature changes needed" reasoning can still miss a real input-shape mismatch at an existing call site.

    PART B -- WIN_RATE AS AN EXPLICIT, OPT-IN SELECTION GATE: new `--min-win-rate FLOAT` CLI flag on `optimize.py` (default `0.0`, disabled), mirroring `--min-frequency-fraction`'s exact, already-proven pattern (item 61): rejects any trial whose own `win_rate` falls below the threshold, reusing the existing `UNDER_SAMPLED_PENALTY`/`UNDER_SAMPLED_DRAWDOWN_PENALTY` path via a new `is_below_win_rate_floor()` helper. Deliberately NOT a 3rd Optuna objective / not a true 3D Pareto front -- reuses the same minimal-risk "additional opt-in gate" architecture already validated this session, rather than the real complexity of a multi-objective selection heuristic.

    REAL VALIDATING EXAMPLE from a live smoke test: DEFAULT_CONFIG's own baseline on a 20-ticker sample showed `sharpe_like=0.259` (looks unremarkable on the raw scale everyone's been reading all session) -> `annualized_sharpe_like=2.164` (actually clears the user's own cited 1.5-2 bar) once read on the correct scale. Also confirmed `--min-win-rate` correctly rejects a flashy-but-thin trial (100% win rate, still caught by the existing under-sampled protection) -- the gates compose correctly together, not just independently.

    Verified: 18 new unit tests (10 in `tests/test_annualized_sharpe.py` -- known-rate calculation, all edge cases including the entry_date-missing regression; 4 new in `tests/test_optuna_objective_helpers.py` for `is_below_win_rate_floor()`), full pytest suite (72 passed, 1 skipped). Byte-for-byte confirmation that trial SELECTION (which params, which trial wins, whether a candidate gets written) is unchanged with the new flags at their defaults -- only the printed DISPLAY gained new fields, matching this being a measurement/reporting improvement, not a methodology change. Real end-to-end runs (both single- and multi-objective) with the new flags actually engaged, not just unit-tested in isolation.

    **Explicitly out of scope, per the user's own stated sequencing**: re-tuning any strategy using these new tools, and reevaluating which avenue reliably produces higher, more accurate Sharpe strategies -- both are the user's own next step, after this measurement/tooling work.

[DONE -- real tradeoff candidate found, NOT promoted, pending user review] Refined v51 (ma_crossover) using the new annualized-Sharpe/win-rate tooling (item 64), per explicit user request after the strategy-direction reevaluation identified v51 as the strongest capital-return candidate of the 4 live/candidate configs checked.

    APPROACH: fresh 100-trial free 4D search (`ma_crossover_short_window`/`long_window`/`atr_take_profit_multiplier`/`stop_loss_atr_multiplier`, same unconstrained space v51 itself was found in -- unlike squeeze_breakout's 9D+2, this space has never shown the overfit-via-filter-stacking failure mode, so tp/sl stayed unpinned), new seed (123, not v51's own 42), and -- first real use of item 64's new gate -- `--min-win-rate 40.0` (just under v51's own ~42.6% TUNE win rate), so any winning candidate had to be at least as consistent, not just chasing raw sharpe_like at consistency's expense.

    Found System_Config **v54**: `short_window=13, long_window=76, atr_take_profit_multiplier=1.103, stop_loss_atr_multiplier=0.652` (RRR=1.692, best-case Trade_Score=61.5 vs. the 60 floor -- barely better margin than v51's own 61.1, not a meaningful RRR improvement). Win rate held up well across all three cuts (44.4%/45.5%/40.7%), comparable to or better than v51's own. Head-to-head against v51 on the identical 407-ticker universe/window/seed: **v54 beats v51 by a wide margin on ALL (1.491 vs. 0.63) and TUNE (1.607 vs. 0.491), but v51 clearly beats v54 on HOLDOUT (0.754 vs. -0.011)** -- v51's own standout property was winning EVERY cut cleanly with random going negative everywhere; v54 partially breaks that (still ahead of random on HOLDOUT, but only barely, not robustly).
