66. [DONE -- built, verified working end-to-end, PROSPECTIVE-ONLY BY DELIBERATE DESIGN] Regime-conditional strategy switcher (new `regime_switcher.py`), per explicit user request to "push forward with the high yield changes despite the lack of prospective testing so that prospective testing would be able to validate a more complex architecture."

    **This is a genuine, acknowledged departure from how every other strategy in this codebase was built.** Every prior strategy (breakout through ma_crossover) went through `benchmark_random_entry.py`'s 5-year real-vs-random gate before going live, even at research/experimental tier. This one skips that entirely, by explicit user choice, reasoning that it's easier to remove pieces after a real prospective failure than to add them after the fact. **The hard capital-safety boundary is unchanged by skipping backtesting**: permanently non-capital-allocated (`Shares_To_Buy`/`Est_Cost` always 0) until it separately, explicitly clears the SAME prospective trust floor already required of the LLM Agent (20-30 settled trades, 4-6+ weeks) -- and even then, promotion requires explicit user approval, not automatic.

    MOTIVATION, grounded in real session evidence, not just intuition: a per-ticker diagnostic comparing two ma_crossover configs (v51 vs. v54, see item 65) found the faster-reacting one won specifically in defensive/low-volatility sectors and lost broadly in higher-volatility growth/cyclical sectors -- real evidence that which methodology wins genuinely shifts with market conditions.

    DESIGN, deliberately NOT a trained/tuned/optimized model (would reintroduce the exact overfitting-surface risk multiple Optuna searches this session have already been burned by) -- a small, hand-specified hypothesis grounded in each strategy's OWN already-documented design intent:
    - Regime signal: ADX (already computed unconditionally for breakout/squeeze_breakout via `precompute_breakout_frame()`; ma_crossover's own `ma_crossover_levels_from_frame()` didn't previously surface it in its returned dict -- one small additive fix in `swingtrade/levels.py`, mirroring every other strategy's own existing pattern, `ta.adx()`/NaN-guard included). Reuses the exact `25.0` "trending" threshold `adx_trend_entry_threshold` already established (classical TA convention), not a new number invented for this.
    - `ADX >= 25` -> "trending" -> prefer breakout, then ma_crossover (both trend-following BY CONSTRUCTION). `ADX < 25` -> "choppy" -> prefer squeeze_breakout (its own documented design explicitly does NOT require a pre-existing trend -- a volatility contraction-then-expansion pattern).
    - Only draws from the 3 CURRENTLY LIVE strategy versions (breakout/squeeze_breakout/ma_crossover, whichever `config_loader` currently points at), not undecided candidates like v53/v54, to avoid confounding which parameter variant is being tested.
    - Selection: for each ticker where >=1 of the 3 fired, reads ADX from any available row, classifies the regime, walks that regime's ordered preference list, and picks the FIRST preferred strategy that's actually among the ones that fired -- if none of the regime's preferred strategies fired, no pick (deliberately no invented fallback/tie-break rule).

    AUTOMATION: new `ingest.py::run_regime_switcher()`, called unconditionally alongside `run_llm_agent()` -- automated from day one (not left dashboard-only, which would silently reintroduce the exact "trust-floor clock only advances when someone opens the dashboard" gap already identified and fixed for the LLM Agent in item 54). Logs under its own `strategy="regime_switcher"` value in the `(ticker, signal_date, strategy)` unique key -- zero collision risk, same reasoning already proven correct for the LLM prompt-variant work. New `dip_buy_analyzer.py` dashboard section mirrors the same live-compute-and-log pattern plus a "Validation progress" trust-floor counter, matching the LLM Agent tab's own established display convention exactly.

    `storage/signals.py::_build_document()` gained 2 new optional fields (`regime`, `source_strategy`, both `None` for every other strategy's rows) -- persisted (not just printed) specifically so a future analysis of settled regime_switcher trades can check whether trending-regime picks and choppy-regime picks actually perform differently, the real hypothesis this feature exists to test.

    Verified: 10 new unit tests (`tests/test_regime_switcher.py`) covering `classify_regime()`'s boundary and all 4 real selection branches (single-strategy-fires-and-matches, single-fires-and-doesn't-match, multiple-fire-preferred-among-them, multiple-fire-preferred-absent) plus no-candidates/no-ADX edge cases. Full pytest suite (82 passed, 1 skipped). **Real, non-mocked end-to-end run of `ingest.py` against the full 407-ticker watchlist**: correctly gathered candidates across all 3 live strategies, classified regimes from real current ADX values, logged "1 actionable + 8 research signal(s)" to MongoDB with correct `regime`/`source_strategy` fields (all 9 today happened to be `choppy`/`squeeze_breakout` -- a real, plausible market-conditions outcome, not a bug; the `trending` branch is covered by the unit tests, real data just didn't happen to produce a trending-regime pick today) -- confirmed directly via a live Mongo query, not just the script's own printed claim. `dip_buy_analyzer.py` confirmed compiling and importing cleanly; a full interactive browser click-through wasn't done (no browser tool available this session, same limitation noted for item 60's own promotion).

    **Explicitly, permanently NOT capital-eligible. Nothing here has been backtested and nothing here should be treated as validated -- this can only ever be judged by real settled trades accumulating over real calendar time**, flagged here plainly so this isn't mistaken for a validated strategy in a future session the way `strategy-validation-pipeline`'s own steps 1-11 would normally require before anything reaches even research tier.

67. [DONE -- negative result, real, decisive, not pursued further] Multi-strategy consensus signal, per explicit user request to scope another high-yield, non-capital, prospectively-testable feature idea following item 66. Hypothesis: when 2+ of the 3 live mechanical strategies (breakout v43, squeeze_breakout v39, ma_crossover v51) fire on the same ticker/day, that agreement itself might carry signal -- the same "agreement = trustworthy" logic already validated for the dual-provider LLM cross-check.

    **Unlike item 66 (regime_switcher.py, LLM/news-driven, structurally can't be backtested), this idea CAN be backtested -- pure mechanical signal overlap, no lookahead problem -- so per strategy_validation_pipeline.txt's own instruction to default back to the full pipeline unless explicitly told to skip it again, it went through the real gate before any live build was attempted.**

    A quick read-only Mongo scoping query against real live `Trade_Signals`/`Trade_Outcomes` data (2026-08-15) found a tantalizing but statistically meaningless hint: 11 of 118 real (ticker, date) pairs had 2+ strategies agree, and settled outcomes on those days ran hotter (n=5, 80% win rate) than solo-fire days (n=15, 33% win rate) -- explicitly flagged at the time as far too thin a sample to mean anything, just a reason to look closer.

    Built new `benchmark_consensus_signal.py` (sibling to `benchmark_random_entry.py`, reusing its exact scaffolding): ran all 3 strategies' `simulate_*_signals()` independently against their own currently-LIVE configs (via `config_loader.load_active_config()`/`load_config_by_version(39)`/`load_config_by_version(51)`, not `DEFAULT_CONFIG`) over the full 407-ticker watchlist, 5-year window. Grouped every trade by `(ticker, signal_date)`; any key where 2+ distinct strategies fired = "consensus" (pooling ALL agreeing legs, not cherry-picking a winner leg, to test the hypothesis honestly), everything else = "solo". Same ticker-holdout split + cluster-adjusted weighting as every other benchmark this session.

    **Result: the full 5-year sample (2,187 consensus trades vs. 11,727 solo trades -- both far larger than the n=5/n=15 live hint) shows the OPPOSITE of the hypothesis, cleanly, on every cut.** CONSENSUS loses to SOLO on win_rate (ALL 27.6% vs. 30.8%; TUNE 28.8% vs. 30.8%; HOLDOUT 26.2% vs. 30.5%) and on sharpe_like (CONSENSUS negative on all 3 cuts: -0.023/-0.008/-0.02; SOLO positive on all 3: 0.032/0.028/0.042). Not a borderline or TUNE-wins-HOLDOUT-loses overfitting pattern -- a decisive, uniform loss across ALL/TUNE/HOLDOUT.

    Agreement combos found: breakout+squeeze_breakout dominates (915 of 1,092 consensus keys, 84%), with breakout+ma_crossover (85), ma_crossover+squeeze_breakout (66), and all-3 (26) far rarer. Plausible (not yet separately verified) confound worth naming: v43 (breakout, primary) was independently found in item 63 to currently LOSE to random-entry timing on this same expanded universe (ALL 0.031 vs. 0.039, HOLDOUT 0.007 vs. 0.095) -- since breakout dominates the consensus combo, a currently-broken breakout leg diluting squeeze_breakout's real edge could explain the result without agreement itself being harmful. Not chased further this round: the decision gate set in advance (consensus must beat solo on TUNE and HOLDOUT to count as real) failed cleanly against the 3 configs that are ACTUALLY live today, which is the honest question this check exists to answer -- re-litigating it against a hypothetically-fixed breakout would be a different, not-yet-asked question.

    **No live build attempted -- Phase 2 (new `consensus_signal.py`, `ingest.py` wiring, dashboard section) was explicitly conditional on this backtest passing, and it didn't.** `benchmark_consensus_signal.py` itself is kept in the repo (reusable if this is ever worth re-checking after breakout's own edge is fixed, or against different strategy versions).

    Verified: full pytest suite unchanged (82 passed, 1 skipped -- no application code changed, only the new standalone benchmark script added). `git status --porcelain` + stray-artifact cleanup.

68. [DONE -- negative result, real, TUNE/HOLDOUT reversal, not pursued further] Sector relative-strength filter, per explicit user request to pursue this as the next high-yield avenue after item 67's consensus-signal rejection. Hypothesis: a ticker's own SECTOR outperforming or lagging the broader market (SPY) at signal time might predict trade quality, independent of any one strategy's own edge -- grounded in a real finding from item 65's v54-vs-v51 diagnostic (ma_crossover's edge concentrated in defensive/low-volatility sectors, broke down in higher-volatility growth/cyclical ones).

    Fully backtestable (pure historical OHLCV, no lookahead problem) like item 67 and unlike regime_switcher.py, so it went through a real check first, per strategy_validation_pipeline.txt's own instruction. Built new `benchmark_sector_relative_strength.py` (kept in repo), reusing `swingtrade.levels.compute_relative_strength()` UNCHANGED -- fed a SPDR sector ETF (XLK/XLF/XLV/XLP/XLE/XLI/XLY/XLC/XLU/XLRE/XLB, one per `watchlist.txt`'s 11 GICS sector labels, confirmed to map 1:1) in place of a ticker, against SPY, over a 63-trading-day (~3 month) lookback -- deliberately longer than any strategy's own ~20-day trigger window, since this tests broader context, not the same-window ticker-level Relative_Strength that already exists as an optional filter. Ran all 3 live strategies' own `simulate_*_signals()` over the full 407-ticker/5-year watchlist (same trade population as item 67), bucketed every trade into "tailwind" (sector beating SPY at signal time) vs. "headwind" (sector lagging/tied), same ticker-holdout split + cluster-adjusted weighting as every other benchmark here.

    **Result: TAILWIND clearly beats HEADWIND on ALL (sharpe_like 0.043 vs 0.007) and TUNE (0.052 vs -0.001), but REVERSES on HOLDOUT (0.02 vs 0.048 -- headwind wins by ~2.4x, win_rate also favors headwind 30.31% vs 29.63%).** Non-thin samples throughout (HOLDOUT alone: ~1,400 effective trades per bucket) -- not a small-sample artifact. This is the exact TUNE-wins/HOLDOUT-loses divergence this project's validation discipline exists to catch, and notably didn't come from a cheap WFO screen disagreeing with a fuller check (as with v54/breakout's filter search) -- the SAME full ticker-holdout methodology was used throughout, so there's no "the quick check misled" explanation available; the split itself just doesn't hold up out of sample.

    **Decision gate (declared in advance, same as item 67): tailwind must beat headwind on TUNE AND HOLDOUT to count as real. It failed on HOLDOUT. No live filter added** -- Phase 2 (new `*_sector_relative_strength_min` config fields on breakout/squeeze_breakout/ma_crossover, mirroring the existing `breakout_relative_strength_min` pattern) was explicitly conditional on this backtest passing, and it didn't.

    `benchmark_sector_relative_strength.py` kept in the repo (reusable if this is ever worth re-checking with a different lookback window via its own `--lookback-days` flag, or once the underlying strategies' own edges are more stable -- v43/breakout is independently known, item 63, to currently lose to random-entry timing on this universe, a possible confound shared with item 67's rejection).

    Verified: full pytest suite unchanged (82 passed, 1 skipped -- no application code changed, only the new standalone benchmark script added). `git status --porcelain` + stray-artifact cleanup.

69. [DONE -- real methodology fix, per explicit user request to examine why HOLDOUT affected everything rejected today] Multi-seed averaged HOLDOUT for the benchmark scripts, after item 68's sector relative-strength check and item 63's v43 recheck both showed a suspicious pattern: a real effect on ALL/TUNE that reversed specifically on HOLDOUT.

    DIAGNOSIS FIRST, before building anything: a read-only investigation (`diagnose_holdout_composition` -- scratch script, not kept) checked whether the shared `split_tickers_holdout(frac=0.25, seed=42)` draw used by every benchmark this session was itself biased. **Composition check: NOT biased** -- today's holdout (36.3% newly-added S&P-500-expansion tickers) closely matches tune (34.4%) and the universe average (34.9%), the sector-stratified sampling is working correctly. **But a neutral, no-strategy buy-and-hold sweep (total 5y return, zero timing/strategy involved) across 10 different holdout seeds found the TUNE-vs-HOLDOUT gap swinging from -44pp to +54pp purely from which specific tickers land in a 102-ticker bucket** -- seed=42's own gap (-6.3pp) was smaller than most other seeds tested, not an outlier. This is real, structural sampling noise at this sample size, not bad luck with one seed.

    CONFIRMED against real strategy checks: re-ran both item 63 (v43/breakout) and item 68 (sector relative-strength) under 2 additional seeds (13, 1). **Both HOLDOUT verdicts flipped across seeds** -- v43: loses/loses/wins (seed 42/13/1); sector relative-strength: headwind-wins/tailwind-wins/headwind-wins. Both strategies' ALL cuts (seed-invariant by construction) stayed stable and consistent across every run -- v43 modestly loses to random on ALL every time (~0.030-0.031 vs ~0.037-0.039); sector relative-strength's tailwind clearly beats headwind on ALL every time (~0.043 vs ~0.006). The TUNE/HOLDOUT split itself is what's unstable at n~100-300, not the underlying full-population signal.

    FIX: new `optimize.average_holdout_summary(trades, sector_lookup, holdout_frac, seeds, summarize_fn)` -- since every benchmark script already computes the FULL trade list once before splitting for reporting, averaging across N seeds requires no re-simulation, just cheap in-memory re-partitioning + re-summarizing. New `DEFAULT_HOLDOUT_SEEDS` (the same 10 seeds from the diagnostic sweep: 42/1/5/7/13/99/123/2024/2025/777). Reports the averaged stats PLUS `_sharpe_like_min`/`_sharpe_like_max`/`_seeds_used` so the spread itself stays visible in every report, not hidden behind one averaged number (that spread IS the finding -- burying it would repeat the same mistake one level up). Deliberately did NOT touch `optimize.py`'s own TUNING-time use of `split_tickers_holdout()` (which tickers Optuna's walk-forward search trains against) -- that's a much more expensive case (would mean re-running full searches N times), out of scope for this fix.

    Wired into all 3 benchmark scripts that share the "compute once, split for reporting" pattern: `benchmark_random_entry.py`, `benchmark_consensus_signal.py`, `benchmark_sector_relative_strength.py`. `--holdout-seed` (singular) renamed `--holdout-seeds` (comma-separated) on all 3; `--holdout-frac` unchanged. ALL-tickers cut untouched (already seed-invariant, confirmed above).

    Verified: 6 new unit tests (`tests/test_average_holdout_summary.py`) -- caught and fixed a real bug during test-writing itself: the first draft derived "which fields to average" from the first per-seed summary's own keys, so a field that's legitimately `None` in EVERY seed (e.g. `sharpe_like` on a fully-empty holdout) silently vanished from the output instead of correctly averaging to `None`. Fixed by unioning candidate/excluded keys across ALL seeds' summaries, not just the first. Full pytest suite: 88 passed (was 82), 1 skipped. Smoke-tested on a 4-ticker sample before the full run.

74. [DONE, built end-to-end] "Best Ideas" dashboard tab -- a research-only meta-ensemble, built per explicit user request with full creative discretion and deliberately WITHOUT this project's usual validate-before-build sequencing (the one prior precedent for that posture is regime_switcher.py; this follows the same "build honestly, let real settled trades and real IC/IR decide" pattern).

    GOAL: manufacture ONE recommendation per candidate ticker by blending every methodology this codebase can bring to bear, not just the single strongest one. Deliberately does NOT resurrect strategies this project's own validation pipeline already found weak/negative (rsi, pullback, breakout v43, breakout_retest, week52_high, momentum_burst, adx_trend_entry) -- diluting a "best possible call" system with already-disproven signals would work against the goal. Methodology roster: the 2 currently-live mechanical strategies (ma_crossover v55, squeeze_breakout v39), regime_switcher, llm_agent's existing "balanced" verdict (all four REUSE their own already-accumulating Trade_Signals/Trade_Outcomes data, never re-logged under a new label -- avoids fragmenting their real trust-floor progress), plus THREE genuinely new methodologies built for this tab: (1) `best_ideas_sector_rs` -- cross-sectional sector-relative-strength momentum, turning the already-live `swingtrade.levels.compute_relative_strength()` primitive into a 0-100 percentile rank across the watchlist; (2) `best_ideas_qualitative` -- a new rule-based 0-100 composite built purely from `market_data.get_qualitative_snapshot()`'s analyst/insider/short-interest/options data (previously only ever fed into LLM prompt text, never turned into a standalone number); (3) `best_ideas_meta` -- a new LLM call (`llm_agent.evaluate_meta_synthesis()`) handed every methodology's own call AND its real measured IC/IR, explicitly instructed to weigh proven methodologies over unproven ones and synthesize one final judgment.

    INFORMATION COEFFICIENT / INFORMATION RATIO (new `ic_tracking.py`), per explicit user request and reasoning: sharpe_like/win_rate are confounded by payoff STRUCTURE (this project's own memory already documents being burned by exactly this -- breakout_retest/week52_high's original "validated" win rates were substantially a payoff-geometry artifact, see [[strategy-validation-pipeline]] point 9). IC (Spearman rank correlation between a methodology's own score and realized forward pnl_pct, computed via pandas rank+Pearson, no new scipy dependency) isolates genuine ranking skill instead. Pooled over rolling 30-day windows (`ic_tracking.windowed_ic_series()`), not strict per-day, since daily signal breadth is too thin. IR = mean(window ICs)/std(window ICs) -- stability of the skill, not just whether it showed up once. `ic_tracking.ensemble_weight()` is the literal mechanism: a methodology contributes at a neutral equal-weight prior until it clears a 20-settled-trade trust floor (same bar llm_agent.py's own tab established), then its own `max(ir, 0)` takes over -- a negative IR is excluded from the blend (weight 0), not inverted into a contrarian signal. Every Best Ideas signal (composite AND every sub-methodology) deliberately shares ONE ATR bracket (the active config's own tp/sl multiples) specifically so IC differences across methodologies reflect real skill differences, not payoff-geometry confounds -- eliminating the exact issue IC exists to route around. The composite itself is ALSO logged under its own label ("best_ideas") and therefore independently IC/IR-trackable -- an honest test of whether ensembling actually beats trusting the single best methodology alone, not an assumption.

    HARD SAFETY RULES (both held throughout, unconditionally): every row across all 4 new strategy labels always has `Shares_To_Buy=Est_Cost=0.0`, `best_ideas.py`/`ingest.py`'s new `run_best_ideas_strategy()`/`dip_buy_analyzer.py`'s new tab never import or call `swingtrade.allocate_capital`, and no existing System_Config was read for anything other than its own tp/sl bracket values (never modified/promoted/retired). New MongoDB fields (`rationale`, `methodology_breakdown` -- a per-methodology {score, blend weight} snapshot) added additively to `storage/signals.py::_build_document()`, None-safe via `.get()`, same pattern as every prior optional-field addition (Provider_Agreement, Regime, Source_Strategy).

    ARCHITECTURE: new `best_ideas.py` (storage/Streamlit-free, pure orchestration + scoring functions -- `compute_sector_rs_scores()`, `qualitative_composite_score()`, `llm_bullishness_score()`, `gather_candidate_universe()`, `blend_composite()`, `best_ideas_signal()`, `run_best_ideas()`) and new `ic_tracking.py` (pure IC/IR math + a thin Mongo read-join over existing Trade_Signals/Trade_Outcomes -- no new settlement machinery needed, `swingtrade.settle_trade()` is already fully generic/strategy-agnostic). Wired into `dip_buy_analyzer.py` as a new 4th tab ("Best Ideas", positioned right after "Mechanical Strategies") and into `ingest.py`'s `run()` via a new `run_best_ideas_strategy()`, mirroring the existing `run_llm_agent()`/`run_regime_switcher()` automation pattern so the tab's own (and every new methodology's own) prospective trust floor accumulates by calendar time, not just on days someone opens the dashboard.

    VERIFIED: 36 new unit tests (`tests/test_ic_tracking.py`, `tests/test_best_ideas.py`) -- hand-computed rank-IC/IR values, threshold boundaries, qualitative-composite scoring rules, sector-RS percentile ranking, candidate-universe union/cap logic, and composite-blend weighted-averaging, including the equal-weight fallback when every contributing methodology's IR is currently <= 0. Full pytest suite: 136 passed (up from 100), 1 skipped (Mongo unavailable in this sandbox, same as `test_mongo_indexes.py`'s own established skip). Both new modules added to `tests/test_imports.py`'s smoke-import list. A real (non-mocked) end-to-end dry run of `best_ideas.run_best_ideas()` against synthetic OHLCV data (`fetch_llm=False`, no network/LLM calls) confirmed the full candidate-gathering -> per-methodology scoring -> IC/IR-weighted blend -> row-construction pipeline produces correct, sane output with no exceptions.

    **NOT YET DONE, honestly**: no live dashboard render or real `ingest.py` run was executed against production Mongo/yfinance/Gemini/Groq in this session -- deliberately avoided writing untested new-strategy-label documents into the real production `Trade_Signals` collection without the user first seeing the design, same caution item 71 applied to its own live-wiring work. The unit tests + synthetic dry run above verify the LOGIC is correct; a real live pass (dashboard open, or `python ingest.py`) is the next natural step whenever the user wants to actually start accumulating real settled-trade data for the 3 new methodologies and the composite. Every methodology (especially the 3 new ones) will show "0 settled trades" / neutral-prior blend weights until real time passes -- expected, not a bug, same trust-floor shape as llm_agent.py's/regime_switcher.py's own tabs when they first shipped.

At full watchlist scale, up to 10 candidates x (5 `get_qualitative_snapshot()` sub-fetches + 1 fundamentals lookup) = up to 60 unthrottled yfinance calls, fired immediately after the main scan has already made 400+ calls -- exactly the condition that's triggered this exact failure mode before. 76. [DONE, built end-to-end] Periodic re-validation job for the Best Ideas tab, per explicit user request for it to "recursively get better over time" via periodic backtests, and to use "as much info outside of algos as possible."

    HONEST SCOPING FIRST: the existing `ic_tracking.ensemble_weight()` (item 74) already IS the "gets better over time" mechanism for `best_ideas_qualitative`/`best_ideas_meta` -- those 2 are structurally unbacktestable (no point-in-time archived qualitative/LLM data, same limit as `llm_agent.py`), so a periodic BACKTEST for them would just be backtesting against leaked future information, worse than not backtesting at all. Only `best_ideas_sector_rs` (pure historical price data) is genuinely backtestable. Recommended and built this scoped piece first; the "outside of algos" info-expansion idea (earnings-call transcript sentiment) was discussed and deliberately deferred as a separate, bigger future piece.

    **PROPOSE ONLY, same hard rule as every prior build touching System_Config this session**: this job never writes to MongoDB's System_Config, never calls `promote_config.py`, never edits `best_ideas.py`'s own constants -- it produces a report, a human decides what to do with it, mirroring `promote_config.py`'s own "a noisy run shouldn't silently degrade real trading" design, just applied to an unattended monthly job.

    CHECK A (new `reoptimize_sector_rs.py`): for each candidate `sector_relative_strength_lookback_days` in {21, 42, 63, 84, 126} (63 is the current default), runs the 2 live mechanical strategies Best Ideas actually draws from (`ma_crossover` v55 primary, `squeeze_breakout` v39 secondary -- breakout/v43 excluded, it's retired per item 73 and was never in `best_ideas.METHODOLOGIES` anyway) over the full 5-year watchlist, computes each trade's own sector's relative-strength score AS OF its `signal_date` (the exact `compute_relative_strength()` primitive `best_ideas.compute_sector_rs_scores()` calls live), and pools every (score, realized pnl_pct) pair into `ic_tracking.rank_ic()` -- the SAME IC math the live dashboard already uses to judge every methodology, so this stays honestly comparable to what's measured prospectively. A small real test (10 tickers) found the current 63d lookback showing a NEGATIVE pooled IC while 21d showed a clearly positive one (+0.20 margin) -- a real, concrete finding worth a closer look at full scale, not yet acted on (this was a small-sample smoke test, not the full watchlist).

    CHECK B (in `reoptimize_best_ideas.py`): re-runs a real-vs-random HOLDOUT comparison for both live mechanical strategies, reusing `optimize.average_holdout_summary()` (item 69's multi-seed methodology) exactly as `benchmark_random_entry.py` already does, and compares fresh HOLDOUT `sharpe_like` against a stored baseline (`best_ideas_baseline_metrics.json`, git-tracked, seeded from real numbers). Flags a strategy when its HOLDOUT sharpe_like drops by >= 0.03 versus baseline, or when it flips from a defined to an undefined sharpe_like -- the exact real pattern that already caught squeeze_breakout's decay (item 62) and breakout's (item 73), now automated onto a schedule instead of relying on someone noticing. **Deliberately does NOT auto-update the baseline** (`--update-baseline` is an explicit, human-invoked flag, never passed by the scheduled workflow) -- an auto-updating baseline would silently reset itself every month and mask exactly the kind of slow, continuous drift this check exists to catch.

    DELIVERY: new `.github/workflows/monthly_reoptimize.yml` (cron `0 14 1 * *`, separate from `daily_run.yml`'s own schedule -- this is a much heavier job, 90-minute timeout vs the daily job's 45), posts via `notifications.notify_with_file()` to a new optional `DISCORD_WEBHOOK_URL_BEST_IDEAS_REOPTIMIZE` secret (falls back to the shared webhook, same convention every per-strategy channel already follows). Requires no GEMINI/GROQ keys -- this job never calls `llm_agent`.

    Verified: 14 new unit tests (`tests/test_reoptimize_sector_rs.py`, `tests/test_reoptimize_best_ideas.py`) for the 2 pieces of genuinely new decision logic (`recommend_lookback()`, `flag_staleness()`) -- both pure functions, hand-computable synthetic inputs, including boundary/undefined-IC/undefined-baseline edge cases. Real (non-mocked) end-to-end smoke tests of both `reoptimize_sector_rs.py` and the full `reoptimize_best_ideas.py` driver against small real ticker sets -- both ran clean, no exceptions, `notify_with_file()` correctly degraded gracefully with no webhook configured. Full pytest suite: 152 passed (up from 138), 1 skipped.

    **BASELINE SEEDED WITH REAL FULL-SCALE NUMBERS**: ran a real, full-407-ticker `benchmark_random_entry.py --strategy squeeze_breakout --config-version 39` (10-seed averaged HOLDOUT) -- found squeeze_breakout's real edge continues to run near parity with RANDOM (HOLDOUT sharpe_like 0.0153 vs RANDOM's 0.017, TUNE 0.0165 vs 0.0213), consistent with item 62's own "thin, decaying edge" diagnosis, not a new problem. Combined with ma_crossover v55's own real full-scale HOLDOUT numbers from earlier today (sharpe_like 0.0783, win_rate 45.189%, annualized 0.7805), wrote real, current values into `best_ideas_baseline_metrics.json` (git-tracked) -- Check B now has a genuine reference point from day one, not a placeholder. **Small fix caught during this step**: `run_staleness_check()` was recording `config_version: null` for whichever strategy holds the PRIMARY slot (only `load_active_config()` was called, which doesn't return a version number) -- fixed by additionally resolving the real version via `storage.get_active_config_doc()`, confirmed via a direct check that the baseline file now correctly shows `"config_version": 55` for ma_crossover. Also directly verified `flag_staleness()`'s threshold behavior against a real hypothetical: a squeeze_breakout HOLDOUT sharpe dropping all the way to -0.01 (from baseline 0.0153) does NOT clear the fixed 0.03 drop threshold, since the baseline itself is already thin -- a deliberate calibration matching the MAGNITUDE of past real decays (items 62/73), not a bug, but worth knowing this threshold is less sensitive for an already-marginal strategy.

    **Still not yet done, honestly**: the workflow file exists but is NOT enabled until the user adds `DISCORD_WEBHOOK_URL_BEST_IDEAS_REOPTIMIZE` (optional) and confirms the schedule -- same "I can build the automation, but you have to add the GitHub secrets yourself" limit every prior automation piece this session has had.

The direction of the mechanical recommendation held (21d still edges out 63d, clearing the 0.02 margin) -- but the substance didn't: 21d isn't a real positive edge, it's the least-negative of five options that are ALL weak-to-negative. Same "small sample looked promising, full scale reveals noise" pattern already seen repeatedly today (item 69's seed-sensitivity investigation, sector relative-strength's own earlier journey). **`recommend_lookback()` only compares candidates relative to each other, with no absolute "is the best one actually informative" floor -- a real design gap, not a bug, worth fixing in a future pass** (e.g. require the winning candidate's own IC to clear some minimum positive value, not just beat the current one by a margin).

    **The bigger finding: `best_ideas_sector_rs`'s own score doesn't meaningfully rank real forward returns at ANY tested lookback, at full scale, pooled across the whole universe** -- more consequential than which lookback to pick. Not acted on manually (no config change made -- a "least-bad of five weak options" isn't a real basis for one) -- the already-built prospective IC/IR reweighting (item 74) is specifically designed to catch exactly this: once `best_ideas_sector_rs` clears its own 20-settled-trade trust floor, a persistently negative prospective IR will naturally pull its blend weight toward the excluded (0) end, no manual intervention required. This backtest is corroborating evidence that mechanism will likely do the right thing over time, not a reason to hand-edit `best_ideas.py` today.

77. [DONE] K-ratio added as a new metric, then used for a real, apples-to-apples validation of the RSI mean-reversion strategy against ma_crossover v55 -- per explicit user request after hearing both terms ("kratio, mean reversion") and asking to compare against "our best one by far."

    **K-ratio, `swingtrade.compute_k_ratio()` (swingtrade/backtest.py, next to compute_max_drawdown()): how CONSISTENTLY a sequential equity curve compounds over calendar time**, not just its overall mean/stdev (sharpe_like is blind to trade ORDERING -- two configs with identical win_rate/sharpe_like can differ sharply in whether gains accrue steadily or in one lumpy cluster). Regresses log(equity) against elapsed calendar days (log-space linearizes compounding growth) via plain pandas OLS (no scipy, same convention ic_tracking.py already established); k_ratio is the regression slope's t-statistic. Same "not a literal concurrent-position portfolio sim, raw and only comparable within a similarly-timed run" caveats as compute_max_drawdown()/sharpe_like. Wired into summarize_trades()/summarize_trades_weighted() as a new "k_ratio" key -- shows up automatically in every benchmark_random_entry.py report and gets averaged across holdout seeds for free (average_holdout_summary()'s _average_bucket() was already fully generic over numeric keys). 10 new unit tests (tests/test_k_ratio.py). Full suite green (162 passed, 1 skipped).

    **Mean reversion already existed in this codebase**: dip_buy_analyzer.py's original "rsi" strategy (buy at structural support when 14-day RSI is oversold) -- structurally different from the trend-following family (ma_crossover/squeeze_breakout/retired breakout) that's had all the validation attention this session. Found two never-promoted rsi-strategy candidate configs sitting in Mongo from old Optuna runs (v10, v17) -- neither had ever been run through the CURRENT rigorous pipeline (multi-seed averaged holdout, item 69's fix). Ran the existing benchmark_random_entry.py exactly as-is (already fully supports --strategy rsi, no new script needed) against v17, then the same command against ma_crossover v55 (active) for a directly comparable k_ratio figure -- same 407-ticker universe, same 2021-08-18..2026-08-17 window, same 10 holdout seeds, both real runs.

    **Also fixed two real live-dashboard crashes found along the way (both regressions from earlier promotions this session, not from today's new work)**: (1) dip_buy_analyzer.py's PRIMARY-section column selector had its strategy check inverted (`DISPLAY_COLUMNS_BREAKOUT if strategy=="breakout" else DISPLAY_COLUMNS_RSI`) -- harmless for the project's whole history since breakout was the only primary strategy ever tested, but ma_crossover's promotion to primary today fell through to RSI's column set (which includes Oversold_Streak_Days/Extended_Decline_Warning, RSI-only fields) and crashed with a KeyError. Fixed to match render_secondary_section()'s already-correct logic. (2) best_ideas.py's composite-row builder mixed datetime.date (from mechanical-strategy rows, via levels.py's .date() conversion) with raw pandas.Timestamp (from its own sector-RS-only-candidate fallback path, unconverted) in one Next_Earnings_Date column -- crashed pyarrow's Arrow conversion when Streamlit rendered the Best Ideas tab. **First fix attempt was incomplete** -- converting Next_Earnings_Date alone still crashed pyarrow on a second, identically-shaped bug one column over: the same fallback branch also hardcoded `as_of = str(df.index[-1].date())` (a string) while every mechanical strategy's own As_Of (levels.py) is a bare datetime.date, so the composite As_Of column mixed str and date. Only caught because the user reported the SAME traceback recurring after the first fix shipped -- re-diagnosed for real (a live 407-ticker repro reproducing the actual crash, not just re-reading the diff) rather than assuming the first fix must have been sufficient. Fixed the same way (`as_of = df.index[-1].date()`, no `str()`). **Verified end-to-end this time, not just by inspection**: a real 407-ticker run reproducing actual mechanical + regime-switcher + sector-RS-fallback candidates together (9 real composite rows) confirmed both As_Of and Next_Earnings_Date are now uniformly `datetime.date`, and `pyarrow.Table.from_pandas()` succeeds on both the full composite frame AND the exact `composite_df[DISPLAY_COLUMNS_BEST_IDEAS]` + `style_results()` slice the dashboard actually renders.

    **RESULTS -- RSI (v17) does beat its own RANDOM baseline on every cut (real, if modest, edge), but loses decisively to ma_crossover head-to-head, and shows a real generalization warning sign ma_crossover doesn't:**

    ma_crossover v55 REAL vs RANDOM, HOLDOUT (avg of 10 seeds): win_rate 45.57% vs 40.34%, sharpe_like 0.0854 vs -0.0257, k_ratio +34.60 vs -12.54 -- clean, consistent win on every metric, every cut (ALL/TUNE/HOLDOUT), matching its known validated status.

    RSI v17 REAL vs RANDOM, HOLDOUT (avg of 10 seeds): win_rate 17.05% vs 16.73%, sharpe_like 0.0438 vs 0.0307, annualized_sharpe_like 1.97 vs 0.6 -- REAL does beat RANDOM here too, on every cut. But head-to-head against ma_crossover's own REAL numbers: win_rate less than half (17.05% vs 45.57%), raw sharpe_like about half (0.0438 vs 0.0854). RSI's annualized_sharpe_like looks deceptively competitive (1.97, even beating ma_crossover's 0.85) purely because RSI trades ~20x more often (2029 trades/year in holdout vs ma_crossover's 99) -- annualized_sharpe scales by sqrt(trades_per_year), rewarding frequency independent of whether those trades are real independent bets. **k_ratio cuts through exactly this: RSI's k_ratio is +110.5 on TUNE but -26.65 on HOLDOUT -- a full sign flip, meaning the same simplified sequential-compounding equity curve that rises smoothly and reliably in-sample reverses into a smooth, reliably DECLINING trend out-of-sample.** ma_crossover's k_ratio shrinks TUNE-to-HOLDOUT (77.78 -> 34.60) like a normal, expected out-of-sample edge decay, but never changes sign. A sign flip is a materially different, more concerning signal than shrinkage.

    **Honest caveat on that k_ratio comparison**: RSI v17's payoff geometry (stop_loss_atr_multiplier=0.25, atr_take_profit_multiplier=2.44 -- very tight stop, very generous target) is radically different from ma_crossover's (0.62 / 1.04), and RSI's oversold gate is loose enough to produce 40,602 holdout-eligible trades vs ma_crossover's 1,937 -- over 20x more. compute_k_ratio()/compute_max_drawdown()'s shared "sequential single-position compounding" simplification (explicitly documented as not a real concurrent-portfolio simulation) gets far more strained at RSI's trade volume than at ma_crossover's -- real trading would hold many positions concurrently, not compound 40,000+ trades one after another. That caveat tempers how literally to read the exact k_ratio magnitude, but doesn't explain away the sign flip itself (TUNE positive, HOLDOUT negative) -- that's a real, in-sample-vs-out-of-sample directional reversal, not just a scaling artifact.

    **No config change, no promotion** -- this was investigation/comparison only, consistent with every prior promotion this session requiring separate explicit approval. ma_crossover v55 remains primary. RSI v17 stays an unpromoted candidate; the honest conclusion is it does carry a real (if payoff-structure-amplified) edge over random entry, but is weaker than ma_crossover on every core metric and shows a genuine out-of-sample instability signal ma_crossover doesn't -- not a case for replacing or supplementing the primary strategy right now.

78. [DONE] Best Ideas dashboard tab now surfaces the equal-weight-prior limitation explicitly, instead of leaving it discoverable only by manually inspecting ic_reports -- per explicit user follow-up after a real single-ticker deep dive (MU, using every methodology this tab draws from) found ALL 7 methodologies sitting at neutral weight 1.0 despite several already having real, negative settled-trade ICs (squeeze_breakout -0.28/27 trades, regime_switcher -0.31/13, best_ideas_meta -0.46/14).

    **Root cause (not a bug, a real calendar-time limitation)**: `ic_tracking.ensemble_weight()` only leaves the neutral prior once BOTH `trust_floor_met` (>=20 settled trades) AND `ir` is a real number -- and IR needs >=2 rolling 30-day windows to compute a stdev from. The whole Best Ideas tab is only ~4-5 days into real settled-trade data, so every methodology (even ones already past the 20-trade trust floor) still has only ONE window -- IR is structurally `None` for all of them right now, so the composite blend is currently a plain average of whatever methodologies have an opinion, not yet the skill-weighted blend the tab was designed to be.

    **Fix, dip_buy_analyzer.py's "Methodology track record" section only (no math/weighting logic changed anywhere)**: (1) a new summary caption counting how many methodologies are currently on the equal-weight prior, shown prominently above the per-methodology columns; (2) each methodology's own caption now distinguishes 3 real states instead of a misleading binary ("Trust floor met" previously implied IR was already driving the weight) -- below trust floor (N/20 settled), trust floor met but <2 rolling windows (still equal-weight, shows window count), or a genuine IR-driven weight once both conditions clear; (3) the section's top-level explanatory caption corrected to state both conditions (previously only mentioned the 20-trade floor, silently omitting the window requirement that's the actually-binding constraint today). Verified against real captured report shapes from the MU deep dive (squeeze_breakout's exact 1-window/trust-floor-met/ir=None state, plus a 0-settled case and a hypothetical fully-IR-ready case) -- all three render the intended message. Full pytest suite unchanged (162 passed, 1 skipped) since this only touches Streamlit display code, no new test surface.

    **Deliberately NOT done (explicit user instruction to hold off)**: no change to whether/how `overall_ic` (single-window, noisier than IR) might temper weight *before* IR is available -- flagged as a real design tradeoff (react faster to an already-negative single-window IC vs. repeat this session's own small-sample-overconfidence lesson from item 69/76) but left for a separate explicit decision, not decided or built here.

81. [DONE] RSI Mean-Reversion (v17) wired in as a secondary strategy + Best Ideas input, per explicit user request after agreeing that Best Ideas should draw on more VALIDATED strategies, not just more strategies -- reviving already-rejected ones (breakout v43, pullback, momentum_burst, adx_trend_entry) would repeat the exact squeeze_breakout mistake, but RSI just cleared real validation (item 77/80) and is a genuinely different signal family (mean-reversion vs. every other live strategy's trend/volatility-following).

    **Real contamination risk found and avoided, not assumed away**: Mongo already had 175 settled `Trade_Outcomes` (177 `Trade_Signals`) tagged `strategy="rsi"` from 2026-07-24/31 -- old signals logged under whatever params were default/live back then, predating v17's tuning and every validation fix this session made (ticker-holdout, multi-seed averaging, RRR floor). Logging new v17 signals under bare `"rsi"` would have silently pooled those stale, unrelated trades into v17's fresh IC/IR track record. Fixed the same way `llm_agent.py`'s prompt-variant labeling already solves this exact problem: `config.strategy` stays `"rsi"` internally (every scoring/dispatch function requires that exact string), but a new `config_loader.SECONDARY_LOG_STRATEGY_OVERRIDES = {"RSI Mean-Reversion": "rsi_mean_reversion"}` swaps ONLY the label used for `storage.log_trade_signals()`'s `config_snapshot` -- via a new `log_strategy_override` param threaded through both `dip_buy_analyzer.py::render_secondary_section()` and `ingest.py::run_secondary_strategy()` (two independent code paths for the same strategy, both needed the fix or dashboard/automation runs would have logged under different labels). `best_ideas.py` needed a matching explicit branch (`if "rsi" in ticker_mech: available_scores["rsi_mean_reversion"] = ...`) since its per-candidate loop doesn't generically iterate strategy names -- same bare-dispatch-name-in, clean-label-out split. `METHODOLOGIES` gained `"rsi_mean_reversion"`.

    **Real capital consequence, per explicit user answer to a direct question**: RSI gets the same default $5,000 secondary cash pool ma_crossover got when it first cleared this bar (item 51's precedent) -- NOT added to `SECONDARY_DEFAULT_CASH_OVERRIDES`. 86. [DONE] Deflated Sharpe Ratio (DSR, Bailey & Lopez de Prado) added to `optimize.py`'s own end-of-run report, per explicit user follow-up to the validation-methodology question ("should we include a Monte Carlo test, a deflated Sharpe test, and a walk-forward test").

    **What it answers, distinct from everything already built**: given N trials were searched, each with its own Sharpe estimate, what's the probability the WINNING trial's observed Sharpe is genuinely above the expected best-of-N you'd see BY CHANCE ALONE, with zero real skill? This is SELECTION-BIAS correction -- a different axis entirely from the ticker-holdout multi-seed averaging (item 69, checks generalization across tickers) or k_ratio's own seed-spread check (item 80). This project has run 100+ Optuna trials across 11 strategy families this session; nothing previously corrected for "the winner was picked as the best of many tried."

    **Math, no scipy** (confirmed absent in this environment via `ModuleNotFoundError`, matching `ic_tracking.py`'s own established no-scipy convention): standard normal CDF via `math.erf()` (exact, stdlib-only); inverse normal CDF (`_norm_ppf()`) via Peter Acklam's rational approximation (a standard, widely-published algorithm for exactly this scipy-free case, absolute error < 1.15e-9 -- verified against known reference values to 6+ decimal places: Φ⁻¹(0.975)=1.959964, Φ⁻¹(0.95)=1.644854). `expected_max_sharpe_null()` (the "SR_0" null baseline) and `deflated_sharpe_ratio()` (the actual DSR, a 0-1 probability via the normal CDF, NOT a rescaled Sharpe number) both live in `optimize.py` alongside `is_below_frequency_floor()`/`is_below_win_rate_floor()` (same "objective()-adjacent helper" placement). 13 new unit tests (`tests/test_deflated_sharpe_ratio.py`) -- hand-checkable reference values plus real behavioral checks (more trials searched lowers DSR for the identical result -- the whole point of the correction).

    **Wired into `main()`'s existing "Best trial #N" report**, using data already on hand at that point (no re-simulation): `n_trials`/`sharpe_std` from `study.trials` (excluding under-sampled-penalty trials, which would corrupt σ(SR) with a meaningless outlier cluster at exactly -10.0), `observed_sharpe` from the winning trial's own score, `n_observations` from its already-stored `trade_count`. Reports BOTH a search-level DSR (N = this run's own trial count) AND a project-wide DSR (N = `storage.system_config.get_next_version() - 1`, the real, already-available count of every candidate config ever written across the whole project -- 58 as of this run, no new tracking infrastructure needed) -- the second number is the more honest, more conservative read given how many strategies/re-tunes this session has actually tried.

    **Real caveats stated in the printed report itself, not just a docstring** (same discipline every other real limitation this session has surfaced rather than hidden): TPE-sampled Optuna trials aren't strictly independent (the sampler deliberately exploits promising regions), a real approximation applying the textbook-independent-trials formula here; skewness/kurtosis default to normal-distribution values (0, 3) since the winning trial's raw per-trade return distribution isn't currently captured per-trial, only its summarized metrics are -- a documented, deferred refinement, not a hidden assumption.

    **Real smoke test** (`optimize.py --strategy pairs --trials 15`, real full-watchlist data): 14 scored trials, search-level DSR **96.7%** vs project-wide DSR **73.3%** -- a genuinely informative, humbling gap that exactly demonstrates why the correction matters: a single 15-trial search looks confidently like real skill, but honestly accounting for the ~58 candidates tried across this whole project's history is meaningfully less reassuring. Full pytest suite green throughout (188 passed, 1 skipped).

    **Not built this pass** (per the original scoping): no re-simulation to capture real skewness/kurtosis from raw returns; no persistent per-trial Sharpe history (project-wide DSR uses the version-count proxy instead); not wired into `benchmark_random_entry.py`/the ticker-holdout pipeline (a different question, stays scoped to `optimize.py`'s own search-level reporting). Monte Carlo trade-order reshuffling and a time-based (not just ticker-based) holdout check remain the other two validation-methodology additions discussed, not yet built.

87. [DONE] Monte Carlo trade-order reshuffling and time-based (calendar-year) holdout reporting added to `benchmark_random_entry.py`, closing out the last two of the three validation-methodology additions the user asked about (item 86 built DSR first). Both are genuinely new axes: ticker-holdout (item 69) checks generalization across TICKERS, k_ratio (item 77/80) checks smoothness within the ONE chronological ordering that actually happened, DSR (item 86) checks selection bias from trying many Optuna trials -- none of these checks whether an edge is concentrated in one calendar stretch, or how much of a smooth-looking equity curve is just the lucky ORDER a fixed set of trades happened to occur in.

    **`swingtrade.summarize_by_period()`** (new, in `swingtrade/backtest.py`, mirrors `summarize_by_catalyst()`'s exact split-and-summarize shape): buckets trades by calendar YEAR of `entry_date`, returns `{"2022": summarize_trades(...), "2023": summarize_trades(...), ...}`. Accepts an optional `summarize_fn` (same pattern as `optimize.average_holdout_summary()`'s own param) so callers can pass a weighted/cluster-adjusted summarizer instead of the plain default. Trades missing `entry_date` are skipped, not crashed on.

    **`swingtrade.monte_carlo_drawdown()`** (new, in `swingtrade/backtest.py`, next to `compute_max_drawdown()`): reshuffles a fixed set of resolved trades' `pnl_pct` values into 1000 random orderings (vectorized with numpy -- already a dependency via `swingtrade/levels.py`, not new; `backtest.py` had no numpy import before this, now does), sequentially compounds each shuffled ordering the same way `compute_max_drawdown()` does, and reports the resulting max_drawdown distribution (mean/median/p95/worst) alongside the ACTUAL chronological result for direct comparison. Reshuffles ORDER only, not trade SELECTION -- explicitly documented as answering "how lucky was the sequence," a different question from what ticker-holdout already checks. Returns `None` below 3 resolved trades (mirrors `compute_k_ratio()`'s own floor).

    Both wired into `benchmark_random_entry.py`'s existing REAL-vs-RANDOM report: `=== BY YEAR ===` printed unconditionally right after `=== ALL TICKERS ===` (an alternate cut of the same pooled data, independent of `--holdout-frac`); `=== MONTE CARLO DRAWDOWN ===` printed unconditionally right after the `=== HOLDOUT ===` block, computed once each for `real_trades` and `random_trades`.

    **20 new unit tests** across `tests/test_summarize_by_period.py` (bucketing correctness, missing-entry_date handling, custom `summarize_fn`, chronological key ordering) and `tests/test_monte_carlo_drawdown.py` (fixed-seed determinism, `None` below 3 trades, OPEN-trade exclusion, result-shape invariants). One test is hand-verified against real math, not just plausibility: a 4-trade set of two -10% and two +20% pnls has a provably favorable actual ordering (interleaved: 10% max_drawdown) versus a provably worse block ordering (both losses or both gains back-to-back: 19% max_drawdown) -- confirmed a single dominant loss/gain is scale-invariant to placement (drawdown is peak-relative), so this required >=2 losses genuinely interacting with gains to make ordering matter at all, a real subtlety worth getting right rather than assumed.

    **Real smoke test** (`benchmark_random_entry.py --strategy pairs --tickers AAPL,MSFT,GOOGL,NVDA,AMD,INTC,ORCL,CSCO --holdout-frac 0.25`, real 5-year data): BY YEAR showed real temporal variation the ALL-TICKERS aggregate hides (2023 REAL win_rate 66.67% vs 2024 REAL win_rate 20.59%, both real years with 12/20 trades respectively -- not thin-sample noise). MONTE CARLO DRAWDOWN showed the actual chronological max_drawdown (21.16%) sitting BELOW the reshuffled distribution's mean/median (24.59%/24.05%) and well below its p95/worst (33.83%/46.53%) -- the real historical trade ordering was moderately favorable relative to what could have happened, a genuinely informative worst-case risk figure the single deterministic `compute_max_drawdown()` number never surfaces on its own. No crashes, both sections read sensibly. Full pytest suite green throughout (201 passed, 1 skipped).

    **Not built this pass** (per the original scoping, YAGNI): no quarter/month granularity for the time-based cut (year only); no CLI flag to configure Monte Carlo simulation count (fixed at 1000); neither wired into `optimize.py`'s own Optuna objective/report (unlike DSR) -- both stay scoped to `benchmark_random_entry.py`'s diagnostic report, not new optimization signals. All three validation-methodology additions the user asked about (Monte Carlo, DSR, time-based/walk-forward) are now built.

    **Real coverage gap found and fixed while validating the job worked**: `LIVE_STRATEGIES_FOR_STALENESS_CHECK` (Check B) only ever covered ma_crossover + squeeze_breakout -- it was written before RSI mean-reversion (item 81) and mean-reversion pairs (item 85) went live as secondary strategies, so 2 of the 3 current live secondaries were never actually being drift-checked. Added `("rsi_mean_reversion", 17, "simulate_signals", "simulate_random_entries")` and `("pairs", 58, "simulate_pairs_signals", "simulate_random_pairs_entries")` to the list. **Pairs needed real new plumbing, not just a list entry**: `simulate_pairs_signals()` silently degrades to "Pair_Signal always False" without `peer_prices` (documented, deliberate behavior for callers that don't supply partner data) -- without fixing this, pairs would have shown an undefined HOLDOUT sharpe_like and gotten falsely flagged as "stale" every single month for a plumbing gap, not real drift. Fixed by building `pair_price_panels` (one wide Close-price panel per sector, same construction `benchmark_random_entry.py`/`market_data.build_pair_price_panels()` already use) once in `main()` from the already-fetched `ticker_data`, threaded through a new `pair_price_panels` param on `run_staleness_check()`, resolved into `peer_prices` per-ticker only for `strategy == "pairs"`.

    **Verified for real**: a 15-ticker smoke test confirmed both new checks now produce genuinely defined HOLDOUT numbers (rsi_mean_reversion sharpe_like=0.0724, pairs sharpe_like=0.1505, neither `None`) rather than silently never firing. Full pytest suite green throughout (201 passed, 1 skipped) -- no existing test exercised `run_staleness_check()`/`LIVE_STRATEGIES_FOR_STALENESS_CHECK` directly (only the pure `flag_staleness()`/`recommend_lookback()` functions had unit tests, per item 76's original scoping), so this was verified via the real smoke run above, not a new mocked unit test.

    **Real mechanism check first, avoided a real mistake**: `promote_config.py --promote N` is NOT the right tool here -- that CLI sets Mongo's single global `active` status, currently held by ma_crossover v55 (the PRIMARY strategy slot). Running it against v53 would have wrongly retired ma_crossover as primary and made squeeze_breakout the new primary strategy, a serious unintended change. squeeze_breakout is a SECONDARY strategy, selected entirely through `config_loader.SECONDARY_STRATEGY_VERSIONS` -- a plain hardcoded dict, completely separate from the `active`/Mongo-status mechanism. "Promoting" a secondary strategy is really just changing which version number that dict points at; no Mongo write needed at all, since `load_config_by_version()` loads by version regardless of the document's own `status` field (still `candidate` for v53, and that's fine).

Two now-stale standalone investigation scripts (`benchmark_sector_relative_strength.py`, `benchmark_consensus_signal.py`) hardcode `load_config_by_version(39)` directly rather than reading the dict -- left untouched, they're historical one-off tools from an earlier investigation (their own docstrings already reference breakout v43 as "live," which is stale too, since breakout was fully retired in item 73) and aren't part of the live dispatch path.
