90. [DONE] Tier-weighted IC/trust-floor math in `ic_tracking.py`, per explicit user request after chasing down RSI mean-reversion's suspicious real-trade data (see the "chase RSI" investigation, same day): real Strong Buy/Buy ("actionable", real capital-eligible) signals now count more toward a methodology's demonstrated track record than Watch-only ("research", logged purely to accumulate data faster, never real capital -- see storage/signals.py's own tier docstring) or "research_loosened" signals.

    **What the RSI investigation actually found first, motivating this**: NOT a settlement bug -- independently re-fetched real market OHLCV confirmed every stored entry/exit price was genuine (RSI's `Buy_Price` = the real trailing-20-day swing low, e.g. MRVL's $162.90 trigger matched the real Low from 2026-07-29 exactly). The real story: 2026-08-20 was RSI's first-ever live day, a broad tech-sector pullback fired 38 highly correlated signals in one day (31 research-tier, 7 actionable), and the next day's broad recovery + RSI v17's lopsided RRR=9.75 bracket (0.25 ATR stop, 2.44 ATR target) turned nearly every position into a `gap_up_target` win. Even the 7 actionable-only trades are ALL from that same single correlated day -- effectively one market event, not 7 (or 38) independent trials, yet `ic_tracking.fetch_score_pnl_pairs()` pooled every one as a full, equal-weight, independent data point toward the trust floor, with zero distinction between real actionable signals and much looser Watch-tier ones. Re-running the actionable/research split by hand found this wasn't RSI-specific: llm_agent's earlier-reported "+0.18 IC, best real result in the system" was 31 of 35 trades research-tier -- the real actionable-only sample is just 4 trades, IC=+0.40 but avg pnl_pct=-0.15% (net negative), nowhere near enough to call anything.

    **New tier-weighted mechanism in `ic_tracking.py`**: `TIER_WEIGHTS = {"actionable": 1.0, "research": 0.5, "research_loosened": 0.25}` (missing tier -> 1.0/actionable, matching storage/signals.py's own convention for pre-existing documents). `_effective_count(weights)` -- Kish's effective sample size, the SAME formula `swingtrade.backtest.summarize_trades_weighted()` already uses for the identical reason (a pile of low-weight trades can't fake a trustworthy raw count). `rank_ic(scores, pnls, weights=None)` grew an optional `weights` param -- when given, computes a WEIGHTED Pearson correlation on the (still positionally unweighted) rank sequences via new `_weighted_corr()`; `weights=None` reproduces the original unweighted behavior exactly, so `reoptimize_sector_rs.py`'s own direct `rank_ic()` call (backtest-driven, no tier concept) is unaffected. `windowed_ic_series()` threads an optional per-record "weight" key through to each window's own weighted IC and now checks the window's EFFECTIVE count (not raw count) against `MIN_TRADES_PER_WINDOW`, so a window padded mostly with research-tier signals can't clear the floor on volume alone. `fetch_score_pnl_pairs()` now returns each pair's `tier`/`weight` alongside score/pnl_pct. `methodology_report()` now returns a new `effective_n_settled` field, and `trust_floor_met` is gated on `effective_n_settled >= TRUST_FLOOR_TRADES` instead of the raw count.

    **Dashboard updated to match**: `dip_buy_analyzer.py`'s "Methodology track record" section gained an "Effective (tier-weighted)" metric alongside raw "Settled trades", and the "Below trust floor" caption now shows both numbers -- the explanatory caption above the grid was also updated to state the weighting scheme plainly.

    **19 new unit tests** (`tests/test_ic_tracking.py`, following this file's own established "pure functions get hand-computable unit tests, DB-touching `fetch_score_pnl_pairs()`/`methodology_report()` get a real smoke test, never mocked Mongo" convention): `_effective_count()` hand-computed (including the "equal weights collapse to raw count" identity), `rank_ic(weights=...)` hand-computed on two cases -- one showing zero-weight points get fully ignored (reduces exactly to the weight>0 subset's own correlation), one showing the weighted result genuinely DIFFERS from the unweighted one on the same input (proving this isn't a no-op), plus `windowed_ic_series()` cases showing a window with 5 raw records but skewed-low weights (effective_n~=1.88) now gets excluded where it would have cleared the old unweighted floor.

    **Real smoke test against live Mongo data** confirmed the mechanism works as intended: squeeze_breakout (even actionable/research split, 14/14) went from n_settled=28 to effective_n_settled=25.2 (exact Kish-ESS match by hand); RSI mean-reversion 38->34.3; llm_agent 35->32.4; best_ideas_sector_rs (33/34 actionable-heavy) barely moved, 34->33.8 -- exactly the expected pattern (research-heavy methodologies shrink more than actionable-heavy ones). No methodology's `trust_floor_met` flag flipped in this particular snapshot (every effective_n stayed on the same side of the 20 threshold it was already on), but the mechanism is now correctly primed to matter as more research-tier-heavy correlated batches accumulate. Full pytest suite green (210 passed, 1 skipped).

    **Not built this pass**: no change to `ensemble_weight()` itself (it already consumes `trust_floor_met`/`ir` generically, needed no changes); no attempt to also correlation-weight same-day/same-sector clustering the way `compute_cluster_weights()` does for backtests -- tier-weighting addresses "how real is this signal," a related but distinct question from "how independent is this signal from its neighbors," which remains a real, separate gap worth a future pass if a single correlated day keeps dominating a methodology's live track record even after tier-weighting.

91. [DONE, built + smoke-tested; REJECTED on full-scale validation, see addendum below] Insider-buying strategy (12th signal family) -- built from an idea in an external document the user found ("How to Create a Stock Research Analyst with Claude Fable," a generic course tutorial, not a validated strategy -- the user dragged the .docx into the project directory and asked for a read + honest assessment first). Scoped and adapted for this project's own discipline, not built as the document describes (the document's own scoring weights are picked by feel with zero backtesting -- the opposite of this project's whole approach).

    **Real feasibility check FIRST, before any code**: sampled ~41 real watchlist tickers against yfinance's `insider_transactions`. Found real, concrete constraints the document doesn't mention: (a) historical depth caps at ~150 rows/ticker, roughly 12-22 months of real history -- NOT this project's usual 5-year backtest window, a genuine data-source limitation, not something fixable; (b) purchase-event frequency is workable (118 events/41 tickers, ~1,100+ extrapolated full-scale) but lumpy -- some tickers (SPG: 41/96 rows) show one or two insiders repeatedly buying, not broad conviction; (c) yfinance's own "Transaction" column returns empty in the installed version (1.5.1), so classification needs free-text parsing of the "Text" field; (d) a real look-ahead-bias risk -- the only date field ("Start Date") is ambiguous between transaction date and SEC filing date, and Form 4 filings can legitimately lag by a few business days.

    **Design decisions made to address each real constraint**, not glossed over: `run_backtest.fetch_insider_purchases()` (mirrors `fetch_earnings_dates()`'s exact pattern -- a sparse per-ticker event list fetched once, not a daily frame) classifies purchases via new `swingtrade.classify_insider_transaction()` (hand-tested against the real strings from the probe: "Purchase at price X per share." -> purchase, everything else -> other), drops rows with no real $ Value (mostly option exercises), and applies a conservative, EXPLICIT `insider_reporting_lag_days` (default 3) to the raw Start Date before treating a purchase as "known" -- an honest assumption, not a silent one. `swingtrade.levels.precompute_insider_buying_frame()` (mirrors `precompute_pairs_frame()`'s pattern of merging an external per-ticker signal onto the base OHLCV frame) is fully vectorized via a (days x events) boolean window matrix, tracking both summed $ value AND distinct-insider count within `insider_lookback_days` (default 14, matching the source idea's own convention) -- distinct buyers, not raw dollar value, drives the scoring "strength" term (`insider_strength_cap_buyers`), since real purchase sizes ($1M-$10M+ seen in the probe: INTC's CEO bought $10M, PFE's CEO $1M) would blow past any dollar-based cap almost immediately and stop differentiating anything; several different insiders buying independently is also the better-regarded signal in the insider-trading literature over one large purchase.

    **Full strategy-family pattern built**, mirroring pairs' own precedent exactly: `insider_lookback_days`/`insider_min_purchase_value`/`insider_min_distinct_buyers`/`insider_strength_cap_buyers`/`insider_reporting_lag_days`/`insider_entry_fill` config fields; `insider_buying_levels_from_frame()`/`compute_insider_buying_levels()` (Buy_Price = today's Close, same "already happening" convention as squeeze_breakout/pairs); `add_insider_buying_trade_score()` (hard-gated on `Insider_Buy_Signal`, same "not eligible, not just low score" semantics every strategy uses); `simulate_insider_buying_signals()`/`simulate_random_insider_buying_entries()` in backtest.py, new `elif strategy == "insider_buying":` dispatch in `run_backtest()`; Optuna parameter-suggestion branch pre-wired in `optimize.py` (`INSIDER_*_RANGE` tuples) but **deliberately NOT added to `optimize.py --strategy`'s CLI choices yet** -- `insider_data` isn't threaded through `run_walk_forward()`'s multiprocessing path (unlike `pair_price_panels` for pairs), so every Optuna worker would silently see `insider_purchases=None` and every trial would hit `UNDER_SAMPLED_PENALTY` with zero real signals; enabling the CLI choice before that threading exists would be a silent trap, not a convenience. Fully wired into `benchmark_random_entry.py` instead (fully sequential, no multiprocessing gap) -- new `INSIDER_AWARE_STRATEGIES` tuple, insider-data fetch block, dispatch branch, `--strategy insider_buying` CLI choice, module docstring paragraph stating the ~12-22mo depth caveat plainly.

    **Verified for real, not just imports**: `classify_insider_transaction("Purchase at price 95.00 per share.")` etc. tested against the real strings observed in the probe. Best-case `Trade_Score` checked against `signal_buy_threshold` BEFORE declaring success (validation-pipeline point 9's mandatory RRR-vs-scoring-ceiling check) -- 66.7 vs 60, clears it, so this strategy isn't structurally unable to ever log a real Buy the way breakout_retest/week52_high originally were. Real end-to-end smoke test against INTC's actual OHLCV + actual insider purchases (no mocking): 15 real signals fired, correctly clustered around each purchase's effective_date + lookback window, correct entry/exit prices, an honest (mostly losing) result (13 losses, 1 small win, 2 still open) -- not spun positively. Full CLI smoke test (`benchmark_random_entry.py --strategy insider_buying`, 15 real tickers with confirmed purchase history): 129 real signals, and on this SMALL sample REAL currently loses to RANDOM (sharpe_like -0.156 vs +0.082, max_drawdown 70.27% vs 27.12%) -- a real, small-sample, NOT YET a validated result (only 15 of 407 tickers, and the inherent ~12-22mo depth limit), but an honest one, consistent with the document's own un-tested weights not obviously working. 12 new unit tests (`tests/test_insider_buying_strategy.py`). Full pytest suite green (222 passed, 1 skipped).

    **Explicitly not done this pass, per this project's own build sequencing** (build+smoke-test first, full validation/tuning/live-wiring are separate later decisions, same as every prior strategy): no Optuna tuning run; no live dashboard/Best Ideas wiring; no Mongo caching layer for insider transactions (only relevant once/if this clears validation and gets live-wired).

    **ADDENDUM (2026-08-21/22): full 407-ticker `benchmark_random_entry.py --strategy insider_buying` validation run completed -- REJECTED, not a clean pass.** Real, full-scale numbers: 708 real purchase events found across 206/407 tickers, 1,281 real signals fired. ALL/TUNE/HOLDOUT cuts each show REAL modestly beating RANDOM on sharpe_like/win_rate (e.g. HOLDOUT avg-of-10-seeds: REAL sharpe_like=0.0044 vs RANDOM=-0.0301) -- but the **BY YEAR breakdown (item 87's own addition, built specifically to catch exactly this) shows the apparent edge is NOT stable over time**: no signals at all 2021-2023 (the known ~12-22mo yfinance depth limit), a real, strong edge in 2024 (REAL sharpe_like=0.264 vs RANDOM=0.013, annualized 7.39 vs 0.24), then REAL **reverses and underperforms RANDOM in both 2025 (sharpe -0.01 vs +0.007) and 2026 (sharpe -0.056 vs +0.037)**. HOLDOUT's own multi-seed spread crosses zero (`_sharpe_like_min`/`_max`: -0.142 to +0.191), meaning the small aggregate "win" isn't robust seed-to-seed either -- the same seed-sensitivity discipline validation-pipeline point 14 exists to catch. k_ratio reads deeply negative for both REAL and RANDOM (-15 to -40 range) at this trade frequency, but per item 80's own established caution this metric is known-unreliable at high frequency without checking its own seed spread, which here is also extremely wide (-62 to +6) -- not treated as the deciding evidence either way. Monte Carlo drawdown: both REAL (95.1%) and RANDOM (91.4%) show comparably severe worst-case drawdowns at this pooled trade volume.

    **Honest verdict: the document's un-tested default weights (14-day lookback, $50k minimum, 1 distinct buyer) do NOT show a real, temporally-stable edge.** The aggregate numbers alone would have looked like a marginal pass; the temporal-stability check this project built earlier the same day is exactly what caught that the "edge" is really one good year (2024) surrounded by two years of underperformance, plus wide seed-sensitivity on top. Not tuned via Optuna (per the strategy's own scoping decision above) since a genuinely thin/unstable untuned baseline is the wrong foundation to invest tuning-infrastructure effort into -- same "confirm the untuned signal carries real information first" sequencing every other strategy in this project has followed. **Status: built, working, honestly evaluated, not promoted, not live-wired -- available for future reconsideration if a longer real insider-transaction data source ever becomes available to retest against a real 5-year window.**

92. [DONE] The other two ideas from the same external document, scoped and built to fit this project's own conventions (not as the document describes) -- (a) an adversarial "checker" second-pass review for `llm_agent.py`, and (b) decision/preference tracking (what a user actually acted on vs. passed on, and why).

    **(a) `llm_agent.audit_verdict(ticker, context, verdict)`** -- a genuinely different check from `_resolve_dual()`'s existing dual-provider consensus (which asks Gemini+Groq the SAME fresh question in PARALLEL and reconciles disagreement, see llm_agent.py:651). This instead takes the WINNING verdict as already-decided and runs a fresh, SEQUENTIAL call that assumes it's wrong and hunts for unsupported claims -- "does every specific claim in the rationale (a number, a named catalyst, a sentiment characterization) actually appear supported by the data given," "is the decision consistent with the rationale's own tone," "is the stated confidence plausible." **Never rewrites the verdict, only reports PASS/FAIL + notes** -- the source idea's own core discipline, and consistent with this project's existing champion/challenger philosophy (`promote_config.py` never lets a search self-promote). Only called for `verdict["decision"] == "Buy"` (the only ones a human might act on) -- auditing Hold/Avoid verdicts has no practical value and would double API cost for nothing. Single-provider (Gemini if available, else Groq), not dual -- a bounded-cost sanity pass on top of an already-dual-provider-reconciled verdict, not the primary judgment itself. New `_build_audit_prompt()`/`_parse_audit_response()` reuse `_call_gemini()`/`_call_groq()`'s existing pluggable `parse_fn` machinery (llm_agent.py:593/624) -- no new low-level API code needed. Wired into both existing `evaluate_ticker()` call sites (`ingest.py`'s headless loop, `dip_buy_analyzer.py`'s live LLM Agent tab), with a visible "Audit: PASS/FAIL -- {notes}" caption in the dashboard. New `audit_result`/`audit_notes` fields on `strategy="llm_agent"` Trade_Signals documents, mirroring `provider_agreement`/`secondary_*`'s exact precedent (storage/signals.py). 12 new unit tests (`tests/test_llm_audit_verdict.py`) covering `_parse_audit_response()`'s shape validation and `_build_audit_prompt()`'s content. **No real end-to-end LLM smoke test possible** -- neither GEMINI_API_KEY nor GROQ_API_KEY is configured in this local environment (same limitation `test_llm_resolve_dual.py`'s own docstring already documents for `_resolve_dual()` -- a real disagreement/API response can't be forced locally either); verified instead that `audit_verdict()` degrades gracefully to `None` with no credentials configured, a real executed call, not just imports.

    **(b) Decision/preference tracking** -- confirmed via a full-codebase search first that nothing like this existed: no field, collection, or CLI anywhere recorded *why* a user passed on a signal, or calibrated future output to revealed preference. `confirm_fill.py` (CLI-only, never wired into the dashboard) only tracks the financial-truth question (did a real fill happen, at what price) -- genuinely a different concept from a preference/journal question. New `storage.record_user_decision(ticker, signal_date, strategy, decision, reason=None)` (`decision` in `{"acted_on", "passed"}`) sets `user_decision`/`user_decision_reason`/`user_decision_at`, deliberately kept OUT of `_build_document()`'s `$set` -- same discipline `confirmed_filled` already established (storage/signals.py), so a routine re-scan can never silently wipe a recorded decision. `confirm_fill.py` extended (not a new script) with a new `--pass "reason text"` mode, mutually exclusive with confirming a fill; confirming a fill now also auto-records `user_decision="acted_on"` (the common case needs one command, not two), while `--pass` is the only path to a "passed" decision (a pass never touches `confirmed_filled`/settlement at all). New `user_preferences.py` module (mirrors `ic_tracking.py`'s own "one focused analysis module" scope): `summarize_decisions(strategy=None)` pools acted_on/passed counts and a reverse-chronological digest of passed reasons, split into a pure, directly-testable `_summarize_decision_docs()` plus a thin Mongo-read wrapper -- same test-shape convention `ic_tracking.py` itself established. **Deliberately does NOT attempt automated pattern-mining/clustering of passed reasons** (e.g. auto-detecting "you tend to pass on high-RSI names") -- with realistically sparse early data, that risks fabricating a false pattern from noise, the exact lesson item 69/76 already re-taught this project twice this session; v1 surfaces the raw digest for a human to notice their own patterns, automated correlation is explicit future work once real decisions accumulate. 7 new unit tests (`tests/test_user_preferences.py`), including `record_user_decision()`'s one DB-free validation check (rejects an invalid `decision` value before ever touching Mongo).

    **Real smoke test, not just imports**: ran the actual `confirm_fill.py --ticker NVDA --date 2026-08-21 --strategy rsi_mean_reversion --pass "..."` CLI against a real logged signal, confirmed `user_preferences.summarize_decisions()` picked it up correctly (`passed_count=1`, the reason text intact) -- then cleaned up the test fields from that real document afterward so a real future decision on that signal isn't confused by leftover test data. Full pytest suite green (241 passed, 1 skipped).

    **No new Streamlit capture UI for (b)** -- CLI-only via `confirm_fill.py`, matching today's actual (CLI-only) workflow `confirmed_filled` has always had; a dashboard capture UI is a separate, bigger project not needed for v1's value. No retroactive backfill of `user_decision` for historical signals -- starts capturing forward from whenever this ships. Both ideas from the original document are now scoped and built (idea 1: item 91, rejected on validation; ideas 2/3: this item).

93. [DONE, Phase 1 -- core mechanism, not yet run with real LLM credentials] LLM-invented strategy research loop, per explicit separate user request (not from the external document -- a new idea): an LLM that composes genuinely novel mechanical trading logic, not just tunes/weighs existing strategies, backed by a persistent knowledge base, recursively proposing and refining ideas day by day. Confirmed with the user up front: isolated, non-capital-eligible, all details dashboard-viewable, capital decision stays explicitly human -- same status every experimental methodology in this project already has (regime_switcher.py, llm_agent.py, every Best Ideas methodology).

    **The critical safety decision, made explicit and confirmed before any code**: the LLM does NOT generate or execute Python code. It composes a structured JSON rule -- which of this project's already-computed technical indicators to condition on, and which of this project's already-built, already-tested exit mechanics to use -- interpreted by one generic, human-written engine. This avoids the real security/correctness risk of running LLM-generated code against production data entirely, while still delivering genuinely novel strategy logic (which signals matter, how to combine them, which exit mechanic).

    **Rule DSL**: `{"conditions": [{"field":, "op":, "value":}, ...], "logic": "AND"|"OR", "exit": {"type": "atr_bracket"|"trailing_stop"|"time_based", ...}}`. `field` is whitelisted against `swingtrade.KNOWN_INDICATOR_FIELDS` -- every column `precompute_breakout_frame()` actually guarantees (RSI, ATR, ADX, SMA_TREND, AvgVolume, AvgVolume_Prior, Highest_High, OBV_Zscore, Squeeze_Zscore, Relative_Strength, raw OHLCV) -- deliberately NOT the full set every OTHER strategy's own extension adds (e.g. Recent_Min_Squeeze_Zscore is squeeze_breakout-specific), since this generic engine only calls the shared base. The shared macro-uptrend/liquidity gate every strategy respects is NOT rule-configurable -- enforced identically and unconditionally regardless of what the LLM proposes.

    **The user explicitly chose to let the rule select exit mechanics too** (not just entry conditions) -- made safe by a real architectural discovery: `swingtrade/backtest.py`'s own `_settle()` helper ALREADY dispatches between `settle_trade()` and `settle_trade_with_trailing()` based on `config.trailing_stop_enabled`, and `max_holding_days` is already a plain config field both read. New `swingtrade.rule_exit_to_config(rule, config)` just builds a per-rule effective `TradingConfig` from the rule's own exit params using these EXISTING fields -- zero new settlement code, the LLM only selects and parameterizes among mechanics that already exist and are already tested.

    **New interpreter** (`swingtrade/levels.py`): `evaluate_llm_rule_conditions(frame, rule)` (pure, vectorized, raises on any unknown field/op -- never silently coerces), `precompute_llm_strategy_frame()`, `llm_strategy_levels_from_frame()`, `compute_llm_strategy_levels()` -- all mirror every other strategy family's own exact pattern (same macro-uptrend/liquidity raise, same Buy_Price=today's-Close convention). `swingtrade/backtest.py`: `simulate_llm_strategy_signals()`/`simulate_random_llm_strategy_entries()` mirror `simulate_insider_buying_signals()`'s day-walk exactly, settling via the SAME `_settle()` every other strategy already uses.

    **Knowledge base**: new `Strategy_Research_Journal` Mongo collection (`storage/research_journal.py`, mirrors `storage/outcomes.py`'s per-collection module pattern) -- one document per cycle: the rule, the LLM's own rationale, an optional `parent_cycle_id` linking a refinement back to the idea it refined (so "recursive" is a real traceable chain, not just a fresh idea every day), the small-sample backtest result, whether it escalated to full validation, the full-watchlist result if so, and the LLM's own reflection notes for next time. Append-only by design, same "never delete history" discipline the source idea's own state-file concept called for. **Never read by `allocate_capital()`/anything capital-adjacent.**

    **Orchestration** (`llm_strategy_research.py`, mirrors `reoptimize_best_ideas.py`'s own structure): `validate_rule()` -- the safety-critical gate, checks field/op whitelist AND clamps every numeric param to a sane bound (ATR multipliers 0.1-8.0, max_holding_days 1-90, condition values within a generic sanity net) so a malformed or adversarial proposal can't produce a nonsensical backtest even if the field names were technically valid. `propose_rule()` reads recent journal history, builds a prompt, calls `llm_agent.call_gemini()`/`call_groq()` (promoted from module-private to public this item -- same "promote when a second module needs it" precedent `classify_insider_transaction` set earlier this session -- avoiding a duplicated ~15-line provider-call wrapper). `run_daily_cycle()` orchestrates: read history -> propose -> validate -> small-sample backtest (default 40 tickers) -> escalate to full 407-ticker validation ONLY if REAL clears RANDOM on sharpe_like AND win_rate (same bar every other strategy's own validation uses) -> write the cycle. **Never writes to Trade_Signals, never touches System_Config, never promotes anything** -- a human reviewing the journal decides whether any full-validation-passed rule is worth building into a real strategy later, same deliberate step every other strategy's promotion has required.

    **User's own mid-scoping addition**: offered to add a third free-tier cloud provider as a cost/rate-limit safety net. Not built this pass -- at 1 research cycle/day this stays comfortably inside Gemini's existing free tier alone (same conclusion as the earlier multi-key-load-balancing check, improvements.txt LLM Agent section) -- flagged as a cheap, easy future addition if real usage ever approaches a limit, not needed to ship Phase 1. Ollama (the user has it downloaded) stays available for the same reason -- not needed yet, not rejected either.

    **Verified for real, not just imports**: real hand-constructed rules (not LLM-proposed -- no GEMINI_API_KEY/GROQ_API_KEY configured in this local environment, same limitation `audit_verdict()` hit earlier the same day) run through all three exit types against real NVDA/AMD/MSFT OHLCV. Confirmed `trailing_stop` and `atr_bracket` produce genuinely DIFFERENT exit reasons and P&L when a trade actually reaches target (NVDA: $236.68 total pnl_pct vs $98.43 across 276 real trades; AMD/MSFT similarly diverged) -- proof the exit-mechanic dispatch is real, not a no-op. Full orchestration smoke test: `run_daily_cycle()` with a hand-built `propose_fn` (dependency-injected, since the real LLM path can't be exercised locally) ran a REAL small-sample backtest (5 tickers, 1,497 real trades), correctly computed `escalate=False` (REAL's sharpe_like 0.034 didn't clear RANDOM's 0.045), and wrote/read back a real cycle via `Strategy_Research_Journal` with full fidelity -- then the test cycle was deleted since it was clearly-labeled synthetic data in a brand-new, otherwise-empty collection. 61 new unit tests across `tests/test_llm_strategy_engine.py` (interpreter) and `tests/test_llm_strategy_research.py` (`validate_rule()`/`_parse_proposal_response()`). Full pytest suite green (278 passed, 1 skipped).

    **Explicitly not built this pass** (matches every prior strategy's own build sequencing -- core mechanism first, automation/UI/promotion are separate later decisions): no GitHub Actions daily cron (`run_daily_cycle()` is manually callable, `python llm_strategy_research.py --run-once`); no dashboard tab; no path from a research result to a real live-tracked strategy or System_Config candidate; no third LLM provider or Ollama integration; no nested/OR-of-AND rule logic (flat AND/OR only, matching every strategy's own lean-v1 precedent); no entry-fill-model selection in the rule DSL (always the resting-limit-order model).

    **ADDENDUM (2026-08-22, same day): the user asked directly whether the API keys were actually wired in -- this surfaced two real, pre-existing bugs in `llm_agent.py` unrelated to this item's own new code, both now fixed, plus a genuine first successful real proposal.**

    **Bug 1 (found, fixed): `llm_agent.py` never loaded its own `.env`.** Every real caller so far (`ingest.py` imports `notifications`, `dip_buy_analyzer.py`/`llm_strategy_research.py` import `storage`) happened to trigger `.env` loading as a side effect of some OTHER module's own `load_dotenv()` call (`storage/mongo.py`, `notifications.py`) -- `llm_agent.py` itself never called it. A bare `import llm_agent` with nothing else imported first genuinely saw `_gemini_available()`/`_groq_available()` as False even with real, valid keys configured -- exactly what happened during this item's own earlier verification, producing a false "no credentials available locally" conclusion that had to be corrected. Fixed by adding `load_dotenv()` directly to `llm_agent.py`'s own module top, matching `storage/mongo.py`'s identical pattern -- verified in true isolation afterward (`_gemini_available()`/`_groq_available()` both True with nothing else imported first).

    **Bug 2 (found, fixed): Groq's model was retired.** `GROQ_MODEL = "llama-3.3-70b-versatile"` now 404s ("does not exist or you do not have access to it") -- the exact same failure mode `GEMINI_MODEL` itself hit earlier this project (see that constant's own comment, improvements.txt item 52) and was fixed the same way then. This was silently broken for EVERY existing llm_agent.py caller (`evaluate_ticker`, `evaluate_holding`, `audit_verdict`, now `propose_rule`), not just this item's own new code -- Gemini being PRIMARY had been masking it (Groq only gets tried as fallback/second-opinion). Confirmed against this account's real available model list (`client.models.list()`), live-tested 3 real candidates with an actual JSON-mode call before choosing -- switched to `openai/gpt-oss-120b` (largest general-purpose option available, verified working).

    **Bug 3 (found, fixed): `MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS=400` silently truncated `propose_rule()`'s own larger schema.** Every existing llm_agent.py caller's JSON shape (decision/confidence/rationale/news_sentiment) fits comfortably in 400 tokens; the proposal schema (a nested rule object plus rationale plus notes_for_next_time) doesn't -- the first real diagnostic call returned a genuinely truncated, incomplete JSON object. Fixed by adding an optional `max_output_tokens` parameter to `call_gemini()`/`call_groq()` (default unchanged at `MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS`, so every existing call site is unaffected), and `llm_strategy_research.PROPOSAL_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS = 1200` for this specific schema.

    **Real success, after all three fixes**: `propose_rule([])` returned a genuine, coherent, valid first proposal -- `RSI < 30 AND ADX > 25 AND OBV_Zscore > 1.0`, ATR bracket exit (3.0x target / 1.2x stop), with real, sound rationale ("RSI <30 targets oversold conditions, ADX >25 ensures a strong trending environment, and OBV_Zscore >1 suggests bullish volume pressure... the ATR-based bracket provides a balanced risk-reward profile") and a genuinely useful forward-looking note ("If performance stalls, consider tightening RSI to <25 or raising ADX threshold, and experiment with trailing_stop exit"). Passed `validate_rule()` cleanly. This is real evidence the "recursive... propose, reflect, refine" framing works as intended, not just the mechanism firing.

    **Real rate limits hit during this same verification, both genuine and informative**: Gemini's free tier is 20 requests/day/model (`gemini-3.5-flash`) -- already exhausted partway through this session's own testing, confirmed via the API's own 429 RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED response, not guessed. A subsequent full `run_daily_cycle()` CLI run then hit Groq's own rate limit too (several real Groq calls made back-to-back during this same debugging session) and correctly, gracefully logged a "no usable proposal this cycle" journal entry instead of crashing -- real proof the degrade-gracefully path works end to end too. Both test cycles (the successful proposal's own run, and the rate-limited placeholder) were deleted from `Strategy_Research_Journal` afterward since they were testing artifacts in a brand-new, otherwise-empty collection, not genuine research -- the journal is clean and ready for a real first cycle whenever the user runs one (mindful of today's now-exhausted Gemini quota; Groq's own limit should reset on a much shorter window).

    **User's own follow-up offer (a third free-tier provider, or Ollama) is now more clearly motivated than it looked earlier**: Gemini's 20-requests/DAY free-tier limit (not per-minute, as this project's own memory had previously assumed -- corrected via this real 429 response) is a real, tighter constraint than assumed, especially once local testing/debugging is added on top of any real daily cycle volume. Still not built this pass, but worth revisiting sooner than "not needed" -- a cheap, easy addition whenever the user wants it.

94. [DONE] OpenRouter as a third free-tier LLM provider (`llm_agent.py`), acting on item 93's own follow-up offer above once a subagent capacity audit confirmed the real need: this project's EXISTING daily automation alone (`ingest.py`'s LLM Agent -- up to 10 candidates x 3 prompt variants -- plus Buy-gated audits, plus Best Ideas' own `evaluate_ticker()`/`evaluate_meta_synthesis()`) already reaches 50-80 real Gemini calls on a normal trading day, against the real, confirmed 20-requests/day/model cap (item 93's own 429-response finding) -- independent of the new research loop's own negligible ~1 call/day.

    **A real backtrack, caught and corrected mid-build**: first built this with Mistral (a dedicated new API key) after I surfaced `llm_agent.py`'s own pre-existing, already-documented rejection of OpenRouter ("paid top-up required for sustained use") and the user chose Mistral over it. The user then found and shared OpenRouter's actual published terms, which directly contradict that claim -- free ":free"-suffixed models need no credit card or top-up at all. Verified independently (not just taken on the user's word or my own prior claim) via OpenRouter's own public `/api/v1/models` endpoint (no auth required) before reversing course -- confirmed real, live, genuinely free (`pricing.prompt`/`pricing.completion` both `"0"`) models exist right now. Swapped the whole Mistral integration for OpenRouter before anything was committed, so no half-finished Mistral code was left behind. **Lesson**: the original OpenRouter rejection in `llm_agent.py`'s own docstring was itself never verified against OpenRouter's real terms when first written -- stated as fact, carried forward unchallenged for months, and only caught because the user happened to look it up. Worth remembering next time this project's own prior notes get cited as a reason to reject something, rather than re-checked.

    **The real, narrower constraint** (found via the same live `/api/v1/models` check plus OpenRouter's own rate-limit docs): free models share one account-wide cap of 20 requests/minute and 50 requests/day if you've never added credit (rising to 1000/day if you ever add as little as $10 one-time, credits don't expire) -- not "needs payment," but real and worth knowing plainly. The free ":free" model roster itself also rotates/gets retired often (confirmed live: Meta's Llama 3.3 70B free variant was delisted since first researched this same day) -- deliberately did NOT use OpenRouter's own `openrouter/free` auto-router shortcut despite it sidestepping the "which model do I hardcode" problem, because it carries an even TIGHTER limit (5 requests/day with no credit, 200/day with credit) than calling a specific `:free` model directly -- confirmed via OpenRouter's own docs before ruling it out, not assumed.

    **Design: fallback POOL for the secondary slot, not a third simultaneous call.** The real problem is capacity, not decision quality -- tripling every call site's volume would make the actual problem worse. New `call_secondary(prompt, parse_fn, max_output_tokens) -> (result, provider_name)`: tries Groq first, OpenRouter only if Groq itself is unavailable or its call fails. Gemini's own primary role and fallback-through-here behavior are completely unchanged -- only the secondary slot gained resilience.

    **`_resolve_dual()` signature change**: previously hardcoded the literal string `"groq"` for `secondary_provider` in two places (the agree branch, and the disagree branch's swap case) -- both replaced with an explicit `secondary_provider_name` parameter so the returned dict honestly reports "openrouter" when that's the provider that actually answered, instead of always claiming "groq" (also fixed the disagreement rationale's provider label to use an explicit display-name map rather than `str.title()`, which would have rendered "Openrouter" instead of "OpenRouter"). All 3 real dual-provider call sites (`evaluate_ticker`, `evaluate_holding`, `evaluate_meta_synthesis`) now call `call_secondary()` and pass its returned provider name through. `audit_verdict()`'s own sequential (single-provider, not dual) fallback and `llm_strategy_research.propose_rule()`'s Gemini-then-fallback sequence both updated the same way.

    **New primitives**: `OPENROUTER_MODEL = "dots-studio/dots-3-note-preview:free"` (chosen by querying OpenRouter's own live `/api/v1/models` catalog for a genuinely free, `:free`-suffixed, `response_format`-capable, large-context model -- not guessed from training-data familiarity with older model names, several of which turned out to already be delisted), `OPENROUTER_BASE_URL = "https://openrouter.ai/api/v1"`, `_openrouter_available()`, `call_openrouter()` -- all mirror `call_groq()`'s exact shape (same `openai` SDK pointed at an OpenAI-compatible endpoint, no new dependency). `is_available()` now checks all three providers.

    **Verification**: `tests/test_llm_resolve_dual.py`'s existing 8 tests updated to the new explicit-`secondary_provider_name` signature (each passes `"groq"`, preserving original intent) plus 2 new tests confirming the "openrouter" label (and correct "OpenRouter:" display capitalization) surfaces correctly on both the agreement and disagreement branches, plus 5 new tests directly covering `call_secondary()`'s own fallback sequencing (Groq succeeds; Groq unavailable + OpenRouter succeeds; Groq call fails + OpenRouter succeeds; both unavailable; both fail) via dependency injection (`monkeypatch`), no live API calls. Full pytest suite green (286 collected, 285 passed, 1 pre-existing skip). Confirmed via a real (non-mocked) module load that `is_available()`/`_gemini_available()`/`_groq_available()` still correctly reflect this machine's real currently-configured keys (Gemini/Groq both True) and `_openrouter_available()` correctly reports False (no `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` configured yet -- the user adds their own real key from openrouter.ai/keys to `.env` plus the `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` GitHub Actions repo secret whenever ready; both are manual steps only the user can do, consistent with how `GEMINI_API_KEY`/`GROQ_API_KEY` were originally added). `.github/workflows/daily_run.yml` updated to pass the new secret through. Module docstring rewritten to describe three providers, the real capacity finding that motivated this, and the OpenRouter-rejection correction, in place of both the old (and since-corrected) "1,500 requests/day" Gemini claim and the old (and since-corrected) OpenRouter-needs-payment claim.

    **Explicitly not built this pass**: no Ollama wiring (still deferred, per the user's own explicit "if free tier isn't enough then I'll wire Ollama all at once" framing) -- OpenRouter is meant to be tried first before reaching for a local model. `OPENROUTER_MODEL`'s free-model roster is known to rotate/retire (see above) -- re-verify against `/api/v1/models` if it ever starts silently 404ing, same lesson `GROQ_MODEL`'s own retirement already taught.

    **UPDATE, same day, after the user added a real `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`: the live smoke test found a real bug, fixed immediately.** `call_openrouter()` returned `None` for every realistically-sized prompt (worked for a trivial one-liner, silently failed for evaluate_ticker()'s actual schema) -- bypassed the deliberate exception-swallowing to see the raw response (same diagnostic approach that caught the Groq 404 and the earlier `MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` truncation bug) and found `finish_reason="length"`, `content=None`: `OPENROUTER_MODEL` (`dots-studio/dots-3-note-preview:free`) is a "thinking" model by default, and the raw `reasoning` field on the response showed it had fully worked out the correct JSON answer internally but never emitted it to `content` before its `max_output_tokens` budget ran out -- the EXACT SAME failure mode `GEMINI_MODEL` hit earlier (`gemini-3.5-flash`'s own thinking-budget issue, fixed with `thinking_config=types.ThinkingConfig(thinking_budget=0)`). Looked up OpenRouter's real documented fix (not guessed) -- `extra_body={"reasoning": {"effort": "none"}}` on the chat-completions call -- and verified live: same prompt now returns `finish_reason="stop"`, valid JSON content, `reasoning_tokens=0`. Re-verified the full real chain end to end: `call_openrouter()` directly (real success), `call_secondary()` with Groq available (correctly prefers Groq, returns `provider="groq"`), and `call_secondary()` with Groq forced unavailable (correctly falls through to a genuinely working OpenRouter call, `provider="openrouter"`). Full pytest suite still green (290 passed, 1 pre-existing skip) after the fix.

96. [DONE] Correlation-weighting for `ic_tracking.py`'s trust floor, closing a real gap a subagent audit surfaced (2026-08-22): tier-weighting (item 90) already down-weighted Watch-tier signals, but had NO notion of same-day/same-sector CORRELATION -- `rsi_mean_reversion`'s first 39 settled signals ALL fired on one calendar day (2026-08-20, one broad tech pullback), yet tier-weighting alone still read this as ~35 effective trades, comfortably clearing the 20-trade trust floor on what's really one event, not 20+ independent trials. `squeeze_breakout`'s 28 settled signals cluster nearly as badly (57% one sector, several same-day).

    **Reused, not reinvented**: `swingtrade.compute_cluster_weights(trades)` already existed for this exact correction on backtest trades (used by `optimize.py`/`reoptimize_best_ideas.py`) -- splits a combined weight of 1.0 evenly across trades sharing `(entry_date, sector)`. `ic_tracking.py` now has a thin, pure, directly-testable wrapper `_cluster_weights(pairs, sector_lookup)` that adapts its own record shape (signal_date + ticker) into that function's expected shape, using `watchlist.read_ticker_sectors()` (already used elsewhere -- `best_ideas.py`, `optimize.py`) for the sector lookup, lazily cached at module level (`_get_sector_lookup()`) to avoid re-parsing `watchlist.txt` on every one of `ingest.py`'s ~7-8 per-run `methodology_report()` calls. Combined with `TIER_WEIGHTS` by multiplying the two per pair -- the exact same "combine independent weight sources by multiplying them" pattern `optimize.py` already uses for cluster x recency-fold weights, not a new interaction term invented for this.

    **`fetch_score_pnl_pairs()`** gained a `ticker` field on each returned pair (needed for the sector lookup, purely additive). **`methodology_report()`** now computes `combined_weights = tier_weight x cluster_weight` per pair, uses them for `effective_n_settled` (same key name, but now the FULLY corrected number, not tier-only) and for `overall_ic`/`windowed_ic_series()` (so per-window IC also reflects the correction, not just the aggregate -- clusters never span multiple windows since they require an identical `entry_date`). New `tier_only_effective_n_settled` field keeps the OLD tier-only number visible for comparison, so the size of the correction isn't hidden. Confirmed the blast radius first (nothing outside `ic_tracking.py` calls `_effective_count()`/the new cluster helper; every downstream reader of `methodology_report()`'s dict -- `dip_buy_analyzer.py`, `best_ideas.py`, `ingest.py`, `llm_agent.py`'s meta-synthesis prompt -- reads fields defensively, so this was a safe value-changing fix, not a breaking one).

    **Real result, confirmed against live Mongo data, reported plainly (not softened)**: `rsi_mean_reversion` -- tier-only effective_n was 35.1, combined (tier x cluster) is **4.1**, `trust_floor_met` correctly flips to **False**. `squeeze_breakout` -- tier-only was 25.2, combined is **17.2**, also correctly flips to **False**. Both matched this session's own pre-fix simulated estimates (~4.1, ~17.2) almost exactly, confirming the fix works as designed rather than producing a surprising number. **A further, unplanned finding from the same verification pass**: `ma_crossover` -- the PRIMARY, real-capital-eligible strategy -- has only **2 settled trades total**, nowhere near any trust floor, tier or cluster-adjusted. `pairs` has 0 (one day old, expected). Net effect: with this fix, ALL FOUR currently-live strategies now correctly read `trust_floor_met=False` -- `ensemble_weight()` gives every one of them the neutral equal-weight prior in the Best Ideas blend, none are yet IR-driven. This is the honest current state, not a regression this fix caused -- the floor was previously reporting false confidence.

    **`dip_buy_analyzer.py`**: "Effective (tier-weighted)" metric label updated to "Effective (tier + cluster)"; added a caption showing the tier-only number alongside whenever it differs meaningfully (>=0.5) from the combined value, so a user can see how much the correlation correction mattered for that methodology at a glance.

    **Verification**: 4 new hand-computed pure-function tests for `_cluster_weights()` in `tests/test_ic_tracking.py` (all-same-day-same-sector splits evenly; unknown-sector gets full weight; different day/sector stays independent; mixed cluster sizes) -- `fetch_score_pnl_pairs()`/`methodology_report()` stay outside unit-test coverage per this file's own established DB-dependent-function convention. Full pytest suite green (295 collected, 294 passed, 1 pre-existing skip). No circular-import risk confirmed (`swingtrade` never imports `ic_tracking`).

97. [DONE] Fixed ma_crossover's Buy/Strong Buy tier being STRUCTURALLY UNREACHABLE, found while investigating item 96's own "ma_crossover has only 2 settled trades" observation -- the real root cause was a scoring bug, not just needing more time. `allocate_capital()` only ever sizes against `Signal in ("Strong Buy", "Buy")`; `add_ma_crossover_trade_score()`'s formula required `Signal_Strength_Pct` ((MA_Short - MA_Long) / Close * 100) to reach ~97% of `ma_crossover_strength_cap_pct` (2.0%) to cross the Buy threshold (60) -- but that field is measured ON THE CROSSOVER DAY ITSELF, the moment the gap just turned positive, by construction near zero. **Confirmed against real data, not assumed**: a 70-ticker, 5-year sample (584 real crossovers) found the actual distribution never gets remotely close -- median 0.14%, p90 0.52%, p99 1.08%, max (of 584) 1.24%, against a 2.0% cap. Net effect: ma_crossover -- the PRIMARY, real-capital strategy -- had never received, and structurally could never receive, real capital under v55, independent of how long it ran. This also explains the 3 real live signals so far (MDB/TWLO/NEM, all `Watch`, scores 40.1/40.1/46.2) -- their real strengths (0.74%/0.74%/1.10%) were genuinely above-average crossovers, not weak ones; the cap was just miscalibrated.

    **Confirmed safe to fix without re-validating the strategy first**: `simulate_ma_crossover_signals()` (the already-validated, backtested entry logic v55 was promoted on) gates purely on the raw `MA_Crossover_Signal` boolean -- it never reads `Trade_Score`/`Signal` at all. This is a scoring/tier-display bug fully downstream of and independent from the validated mechanical edge; fixing the cap changes zero backtested trades, only which real live crossovers get classified as Buy-eligible.

    **Two changes, both needed** (confirmed `ma_crossover_strength_cap_pct` is never an Optuna search dimension -- always just carried through from whatever config default/candidate has, so both needed or this silently reappears in any future re-tune): (1) `swingtrade/config.py`'s dataclass default `ma_crossover_strength_cap_pct` 2.0 -> 0.5 (just under the real p90), with the comment rewritten to cite the real calibration data instead of the original unvalidated guess. (2) New System_Config **v60**, written via `storage.write_candidate()` as a byte-identical copy of v55's params except that one field (confirmed via direct dict diff -- exactly one key differs), then promoted via `promote_config.py --promote 60` (v55 auto-retired) -- same champion/challenger mechanism every prior promotion in this project has used, not a raw Mongo edit.

    **Real verification before promoting, not just the math in isolation**: recomputed `add_ma_crossover_trade_score()` for the 3 real historical live signals under the new cap -- all 3 now correctly score **Buy** (61.1), concrete proof the fix resolves the actual cases already seen, not just theory. Recomputed Trade_Score for the full 584-crossover real sample under the new cap: **10.8% Buy, 30.5% Watch, 58.7% Ignore** -- a sensible, real-world differentiation, neither degenerate ("still nothing reaches Buy") nor overcorrected ("everything is Buy now"). Full pytest suite green (294 passed, 1 pre-existing skip) -- no test pinned the old constant.

    **Deliberately NOT retroactively relabeled**: MDB/TWLO/NEM's already-logged `Trade_Signals` documents keep their original Watch tier/score -- each signal is settled against its OWN `config_snapshot` at signal time (see `settle_trades.py`'s own docstring), the established "preserve signal-time truth, never backfill" discipline this project already follows (e.g. decision tracking's own no-backfill precedent). The fix applies going forward: the next real crossover strong enough to matter can now actually receive real capital.

98. [DONE] IC/IR window-trust gate fixed, Momentum Rank (v65) automated, 2 real bugs found along the way (2026-08-27) -- grounded in real Mongo data (`ic_tracking.methodology_report()`) rather than memory alone. Found `ir` was `None` for EVERY methodology (30-day window means 2+ windows needs >30 real days, nobody had that yet) -- simulating shorter windows on real data showed a 2-window `ir` is dangerously noisy (llm_agent swung to +10.4, squeeze_breakout to -4.8/-5.7, both 1 degree of freedom). Fixed with new `MIN_WINDOWS_FOR_IR_TRUST=4` gate in `ensemble_weight()` (falls back to the existing `overall_ic` fallback below that) plus `IC_WINDOW_DAYS` lowered 30->14 (roughly halves calendar time to a *trustworthy* ir without the instability risk). Separately: Momentum Rank v65 had logged ZERO real signals in 3 days live -- root-caused to two independent bugs, not one: no headless automation existed (dashboard-tab-only; fixed with new `ingest.run_experimental_strategies()`, generalized over `EXPERIMENTAL_STRATEGY_VERSIONS`, mirrors `run_regime_switcher()`), and a real previously-undiscovered bug where `storage/signals.py`'s `_build_document()` needs `Shares_To_Buy`/`Est_Cost` columns that experimental strategies never populate -- `log_trade_signals()` was silently failing via a swallowed `except` in the dashboard too, fixed in both places with explicit zero-fill. Verified real: 388 pytest passed, a real 20-ticker live smoke test logged a genuine PLTR momentum_rank signal to production Mongo.

103. [DONE, per explicit user request] Continuous Bayesian credibility shrinkage replaces `ic_tracking.ensemble_weight()`'s binary trust-floor cutover (2026-08-29) -- from a "brainstorm genuinely meaningful additions" ask, after a PDF the user found ("Build Your Own AI Hedge Fund with Claude Code") turned out to not be about Smart Money concepts as expected, but its multi-agent debate architecture prompted a real look at what this project's OWN weighting math could still improve. The old system was binary: flat `neutral_prior=1.0` until `effective_n_settled` crossed `TRUST_FLOOR_TRADES=20`, then instant full trust -- a methodology with 19 effective trades got ZERO credit, one with 20 got FULL credit, an arbitrary discontinuity.

    **Fix**: `credibility = effective_n / (effective_n + CREDIBILITY_HALF_LIFE_TRADES)` (Bühlmann-style credibility theory, no new dependency -- same closed-form-math-only discipline this module already follows), `weight = credibility * max(signal, 0.0) + (1 - credibility) * neutral_prior`. `CREDIBILITY_HALF_LIFE_TRADES` deliberately set equal to `TRUST_FLOOR_TRADES` (20) so the old floor becomes the point of exactly 50% credibility, not a coincidental new magic number. Negative signals still floor to 0 BEFORE blending (same "exclude, don't invert" reasoning as before).

    **Two real findings from checking this against live data before shipping**: (1) it also partly corrects a pre-existing quirk -- `neutral_prior=1.0` is numerically LARGER than almost any real IC ever observed, so the old system already punished a genuinely good methodology (llm_agent, overall_ic=+0.28, the best real performer) relative to untested ones (weight 0.28 vs untested ones' 1.0) -- shrinkage pulling it UP toward ~0.55 is arguably more correct, not a regression. (2) the real, deliberate tradeoff, confirmed with the user directly: a methodology with substantial NEGATIVE evidence (squeeze_breakout, overall_ic=-0.32 over 26 effective trades) no longer reaches an exact zero weight either -- only asymptotically approaches it (~0.43 at this K). Chose uniform shrinkage (K=20 both directions, "nothing -- including bad news -- ever treated as 100% certain") over a smaller/faster-converging K or an asymmetric hard-floor-for-negatives-only design.

    `trust_floor_met` is unchanged and still computed by `methodology_report()` -- still meaningful for display (used correctly, unrelated to blend math, in `daily_briefing.build_portfolio_health_section()`), just no longer read by `ensemble_weight()` itself. Rewrote the dashboard's "Methodology track record" section to match: the old three-way binary captions replaced with a continuous credibility % display per methodology; the old "N methodologies on the neutral prior" aggregate caption (which would now almost never fire, since exact equality to 1.0 basically never happens under continuous shrinkage) replaced with a "N methodologies below 25% credibility" one. Rewrote `tests/test_ic_tracking.py`'s `ensemble_weight` tests from exact-cutover assertions to hand-computed credibility-formula cases. Full pytest suite green (387 passed). Verified against real, CURRENT live Mongo data (2 real days having passed since item 98's own IC_WINDOW_DAYS work that same morning -- ma_crossover up to 5 settled from 2, llm_agent's `ir` now populated but still correctly falls back to `overall_ic` per item 98's own `MIN_WINDOWS_FOR_IR_TRUST` gate, regime_switcher crossed the old trust floor) -- all weights came out sensible, nothing degenerate.

[DONE, per explicit user request] Dispatch-parity test coverage made exhaustive instead of hand-maintained (2026-08-29) -- the other genuinely-meaningful addition picked from the same brainstorm, deliberately scoped narrower than 3 other candidate "stale-assumption audit" ideas also proposed (a periodic live-config RRR-ceiling re-check, a live tier-distribution sanity check, a config consistency lint -- all flagged but not built this pass, available if wanted later). Found `tests/test_strategy_dispatch_parity.py` (built in an earlier session specifically to guard against ingest.py/dip_buy_analyzer.py's two independent `_score_for_strategy()` copies drifting apart) had ITSELF gone stale in the exact same way: its own hand-maintained `STRATEGIES` list never included "pairs" or "momentum_rank" -- meaning ingest.py's real, live `_score_for_strategy()` momentum_rank gap (fixed in item 98 above) existed for 5 real days without this very test ever having a chance to catch it.

(2) New `test_every_live_strategy_has_dispatch_coverage()` (Mongo-gated, skips gracefully like `test_mongo_indexes.py` if unavailable): resolves every strategy actually wired into `config_loader.SECONDARY_STRATEGY_VERSIONS`/`EXPERIMENTAL_STRATEGY_VERSIONS` to its REAL `config.strategy` field via `config_loader.load_config_by_version()` (the same real Mongo documents production loads, not the display label), and asserts each one has a `COMPUTE_LEVELS_FN` entry -- so a FUTURE new live strategy can't silently fall through this exact gap again, closing the loop this test's own staleness left open.

107. [DONE, per explicit user request] `compute_max_drawdown()` redesigned from a naive sequential-100%-per-trade model to a concurrency-weighted one (2026-08-31) -- a direct follow-up to item 106's own multi-objective (`optimize.py --multi-objective`) exploration, which surfaced a real, deeper bug than the "search window too short" theory it started from. A fresh 5-year multi-objective search for ma_crossover showed even an UNTUNED `DEFAULT_CONFIG` baseline at 94.8% "max drawdown" and a validated candidate at 98.5% -- numbers too saturated near 100% to discriminate a good config from a bad one. Root cause: `compute_max_drawdown()` pooled every trade from every one of up to 407 tickers into ONE fake sequential account, treating each as if it alone consumed 100% of capital with zero overlap -- but this is a strategy scanning 407 tickers daily with positions held up to `max_holding_days` (15) at a time, so many trades genuinely overlap in real calendar time. Baseline showing 94.8% wasn't measuring the config; it was measuring the metric's own blind spot.

    **Design, not a quick patch**: delegated to a dedicated Explore pass (confirmed no existing concurrency-tracking exists anywhere in this codebase to reuse -- `swingtrade/allocation.py`'s `allocate_capital()` only handles a single day's live signals, its own docstring already admits it doesn't model multi-day concurrent exposure) then a dedicated Plan pass before writing any code, given this touches a core metric with real blast radius (`optimize.py`'s 3 call sites, `monte_carlo_drawdown()`). New `_concurrency_at_entry()`: an O(n log n) event-delta sweep over `entry_date`/`exit_date` (both already present on every resolved trade dict, no new data dependency) counting how many trades were open when each trade itself entered (closed-interval convention -- a trade exiting the same day another enters counts as concurrent, since there's no intraday fill-sequencing data to know whose capital freed up first). Each trade's `position_weight = 1 / that count`. **Real, load-bearing design catch from the Plan pass**: trades sharing the same `exit_date` must be combined ADDITIVELY (weighted-sum), never compounded one at a time even after weighting -- verified numerically that naive sequential processing of same-day exits is order-dependent (two trades weighted 0.5/0.5, pnl +20%/-50%, give 25% or 17.5% "drawdown" depending on which is processed first; the correct additive combination gives a stable 15% regardless of order -- now a named regression test). Public signature (`compute_max_drawdown(trades: list[dict]) -> float | None`) stays frozen, so every existing caller gets the improved semantics with zero call-site changes; independently verified `optimize.py`'s `taper_drawdown_for_sample_size()` needed no changes either (pure scale-agnostic linear interpolation, confirmed by reading it directly, not just trusting the design pass). `monte_carlo_drawdown()` updated to keep each trade's own weight/exit-date-batch membership FIXED across every simulation, reshuffling only which `pnl_pct` lands on which weighted slot (computed once via a new `_exit_date_batch_layout()` helper, reused across all `n_simulations`, not re-derived per simulation) -- vectorized via `np.add.reduceat`, no per-simulation Python loop.

    **`compute_k_ratio()` has the identical flaw and is explicitly NOT touched this pass** -- flagged as a known, deliberate follow-up in its own docstring (which no longer falsely claims equivalence with `compute_max_drawdown()`'s new algorithm).

    **Verified two ways, not just unit tests**: (1) new `tests/test_max_drawdown.py` (11 cases, all hand-computed by hand before running, not asserted-then-adjusted -- fully-overlapping/asymmetric-overlap/all-same-day/non-overlapping/sweep-line-correctness/same-day-boundary/missing-field-graceful-None cases). `tests/test_monte_carlo_drawdown.py`'s `_trade()` helper needed a required `exit_date` param (defaulting to `entry_date`, a zero-day interval) -- confirmed this preserves every existing hand-verified assertion in that file unchanged, since its trades were already non-overlapping by construction. Full suite green (403 passed, was 392). (2) **Real data, not just synthetic**: re-ran a real 60-real-ticker, 5-year `ma_crossover`/`DEFAULT_CONFIG` walk-forward (the same shape that showed 94.8% under the old model) -- new concurrency-weighted result: **38.05%**. A dramatic, sensible drop from near-total-wipeout territory to a plausible, discriminating value, on real market data, confirming the fix addresses the actual diagnosed problem, not just the synthetic test cases. A full re-run of the original 407-ticker/20-trial multi-objective search (to see whether a genuinely better drawdown-aware candidate now emerges) was queued the same session, not yet reported on.

108. [DONE, scaffolding only -- not yet run for real] Macro yield-curve regime feasibility check built (2026-08-31) -- from the "explore new data sources" thread revisited from an earlier session. Before assuming anything was unexplored, checked what's already pulled: `market_data.get_qualitative_snapshot()` already fetches analyst price targets/recommendation trend/recent actions, insider transaction direction, short interest, SEC filings, and options put/call ratio, all feeding a real, LIVE, hand-weighted rule (`best_ideas.qualitative_composite_score()`) that's been accumulating real settled trades since mid-August -- real result so far: win_rate ~24.6%, avg_pnl ~-1%, the same thin-to-weak pattern found everywhere else this session, not a promising blank slate. The one genuinely untouched, genuinely free category: macro/regime data beyond VIX (the only macro signal anywhere in this project today).

    **New `market_data.fetch_fred_series()`** -- a minimal FRED (Federal Reserve Economic Data) fetcher via `requests` (already a dependency, no new package), gated on a new optional `FRED_API_KEY` env var (free registration, same "optional external service, degrades gracefully if unset" convention as `GEMINI_API_KEY`/`DISCORD_WEBHOOK_URL`), verified to fail cleanly (not crash) with no traceback when the key is unset. **Deliberate series choice, documented in both the fetcher's and the benchmark script's own docstrings**: T10Y2Y (10-year/2-year Treasury yield spread) specifically, NOT a survey/estimate-based series like ISM PMI or GDP -- Treasury rates are market-observed and published same-day, never revised after the fact, while PMI/GDP-style series DO get revised, which would silently graft a new look-ahead-bias problem onto a codebase that's gone to real lengths elsewhere (entry-fill timing, `.shift(1)` indicators, ticker-holdout) to avoid exactly this class of bug.

    **New `benchmark_macro_regime.py`**, mirroring `benchmark_sector_relative_strength.py`'s exact established pattern (research script first, live filter only if it proves out, per strategy-validation-pipeline discipline) -- buckets ma_crossover's own simulated trades by yield-curve regime (inverted vs. normal) as of each trade's signal_date (with a strict "most recent observation BEFORE signal_date" lookup, never same-day-or-later), compares via the same ticker-holdout + cluster-adjusted + multi-seed-averaged methodology every other benchmark script here uses. Same decision gate as sector relative strength: must show a real, consistent difference on TUNE and HOLDOUT, not just ALL, before any live filter gets built.

    **Not yet run for real** -- needs the user to get a free `FRED_API_KEY` first. No test coverage added for `fetch_fred_series()` itself, matching this project's own established convention that thin network-fetch wrappers (`get_qualitative_snapshot()`, etc.) and one-off `benchmark_*.py` research scripts don't get dedicated unit tests.

    **Verified the guard actually guards, not just added and trusted**: temporarily removed the `momentum_rank` entry from a throwaway copy of `COMPUTE_LEVELS_FN` and confirmed the new test fails with a clear, specific message naming the exact gap -- proof it would have caught the real incident, not just a plausible-sounding check. Full pytest suite green (390 passed, +3 from the new momentum_rank/pairs parametrized cases and the new coverage test).

    **Full chain threaded, mirroring the sector-strength precedent exactly**: `config.py` (new field), `levels.py` (`precompute_ma_crossover_frame()` gains `yield_curve` param with `.reindex(...).shift(1)` no-look-ahead discipline; `ma_crossover_levels_from_frame()` extracts the new `Yield_Curve_Spread` column), `scoring.py` (`add_ma_crossover_trade_score()` gate), `backtest.py` (`simulate_ma_crossover_signals()` gate plus the FULL multiprocessing chain -- `run_backtest()`, `_run_fold_sequential()`, a new `_worker_yield_curve` module-level global, `_init_worker()`, `_run_fold_worker()`, both the sequential and `ProcessPoolExecutor` branches of `run_walk_forward()`). `optimize.py` fetches T10Y2Y once via `market_data.fetch_fred_series()` (gated on `market_data.fred_available()`, clean `None` no-op if unset) and threads `yield_curve` through every call site that already threads `sector_data` -- `build_objective()`, both holdout `run_walk_forward()` calls, the live-config comparison, `real_vs_random_ratio_check()` -- plus a new `YIELD_CURVE_SPREAD_MAX_RANGE = (-3.0, 5.0)` Optuna search dimension.

    **One real bug caught before it shipped, not after**: `optimize.py`'s `main()` already had a local variable named `market_data` (the fetched SPY index DataFrame) used throughout the function -- `import market_data` would have been silently shadowed by that local name, crashing the very first `market_data.fred_available()` call with `AttributeError` at runtime (not import time, so it would have passed a casual smoke check and only surfaced mid-run). Fixed by aliasing the import (`import market_data as market_data_module`) rather than the much more invasive option of renaming the pre-existing, widely-used local variable.

    **Tests**: fixed 2 pre-existing tests broken by the new unconditional `Yield_Curve_Spread` column read in `scoring.py` (their hand-built fixture predated the field -- added an optional `yield_curve_spread=None` param to the fixture helper, the established convention here of updating fixtures rather than adding defensive `.get()` guards to production code). Added 3 new gate tests (excludes-above-threshold, no-op-at-default, missing-value-never-excludes) plus a new `tests/test_yield_curve_multiprocessing_threading.py` mirroring `test_sector_data_multiprocessing_threading.py`'s exact two-test shape (worker-vs-sequential parity via `_init_worker()`/`_run_fold_worker()` vs. direct `_run_fold_sequential()`; a real-effect test proving an impossible-to-clear ceiling excludes every trade while the disabled default AND a "no yield_curve supplied" run both admit everything, proving the exclusion genuinely came from the new field and not something else about the strict config). 408 tests green (was 403).

    **Verified live, not just unit-tested**: a real smoke-scale Optuna search (`--strategy ma_crossover --trials 5 --tickers AAPL,MSFT,NVDA,JPM,XOM`) ran clean end-to-end -- FRED fetch fired for real ("Fetching FRED T10Y2Y yield-curve spread..."), and `ma_crossover_yield_curve_spread_max` appeared with real sampled values (spanning the full -3.0..5.0 range) in every trial's params. The 5-ticker/1-year smoke run itself was under-sampled and didn't write a candidate, as expected at that scale -- not a correctness signal either way.

    **Not yet done**: a real full-scale search actually exercising this new dimension. The concurrent 407-ticker/20-trial multi-objective background re-run from item 107 predates this filter and does not include it -- a fresh search including `ma_crossover_yield_curve_spread_max` is the natural next step, separate from today's build-and-verify pass.

110. [DONE] Fixed a real, misleading labeling bug in `optimize.py --multi-objective`'s own console/notes output, found while reviewing the item-109 follow-up search's own selected candidate (System_Config v70, 2026-08-31). The Pareto-front/selected-trial print lines and the Mongo `notes` field both printed a bare `max_drawdown=X%` -- but the PRINTED/Pareto-ranking value is `tapered_max_drawdown` (deliberately inflated toward `UNDER_SAMPLED_DRAWDOWN_PENALTY`=100% for thin samples via `taper_drawdown_for_sample_size()`), while the Mongo `notes` field separately stored the RAW, untapered value under the same unlabeled name. v70's own selected trial showed 87.34% in the console/Pareto listing but 9.59% in its stored `notes` -- same trial, same metric name, two very different numbers, no indication either was tapered. Nearly caused a real misread (an 87% drawdown looks disqualifying; the real raw number was a healthy 9.59%, legitimately penalized for thinness -- only 57 effective TUNE trades, well under `MIN_TRADES_FOR_TRUSTED_DRAWDOWN`=100). Fixed: both print sites now show `tapered_max_drawdown` and `raw=` side by side, the Mongo `notes` field now says `raw max_drawdown=X%, tapered_max_drawdown=Y%` explicitly, and a new `[WARN]` prints when the selected trial's own `effective_trade_count` is below `MIN_TRADES_FOR_TRUSTED_DRAWDOWN` (the current selection heuristic picks purely by raw sharpe_like among Pareto-eligible trials, with no regard for how heavily a trial's own drawdown axis was penalized for sample thinness -- this makes that risk visible instead of silently absorbed into one ambiguous number). Pure clarity fix, zero behavior change -- 408 tests still green. **Follow-up, same session: user chose to also fix the SELECTION heuristic itself, not just its labeling.** Presented 3 options (require a minimum sample size for the Pareto-eligible winner; rank by a tapered-drawdown-adjusted score; leave selection as-is and rely on the new WARN alone) -- user picked the first. Implemented: Pareto-eligible trials are now split into `trusted_pool` (own TUNE `effective_trade_count >= MIN_TRADES_FOR_TRUSTED_DRAWDOWN`) vs the full `eligible` set, selection maximizes raw sharpe_like within `trusted_pool` first, falling back to the full `eligible` set (old behavior, now with an explicit `[WARN]` naming exactly why) only if NO Pareto-optimal trial clears the bar -- graceful degradation rather than refusing to ever write a candidate. This would have selected v69/trial #16 instead of v70's thin trial #10 had it been live for that search. 408 tests still green (behavior-neutral for any trial pool that already clears the bar). A fresh full-scale 407-ticker/20-trial search re-run under the corrected heuristic (2026-08-31) confirmed the fix works: wrote System_Config v71 (sharpe_like=0.176, raw AND tapered drawdown both 14.83% -- no tapering distortion this time, 198.0 effective TUNE trades genuinely clears MIN_TRADES_FOR_TRUSTED_DRAWDOWN=100), no RRR-saturation warning (RRR=1.95, vs v70's flagged 3.06), beats live active v60 on holdout (sharpe_like 0.048 vs -0.04), real-vs-random gap improved (0.072 vs baseline's 0.003), DSR 98.9%/76.7% (search/project-level). First candidate from this whole multi-objective thread to clear every check with zero caveats. Written as `status=candidate`, NOT promoted -- same explicit-approval requirement as always. **Follow-up, user-requested (2026-08-31)**: a direct v60-vs-v71 head-to-head drawdown check, since v60's own stored record never had drawdown computed against the same holdout v71 used. One-off script (not committed) reused v71's exact holdout ticker list; first pass had its own bug (unweighted `summarize_trades()` instead of `summarize_weighted()`, sign-flipped v60's sharpe_like) -- caught by comparing against known stored numbers, fixed, re-verified exact match. Result: v60 max_drawdown=24.34% vs v71's 21.54% -- v71 wins on drawdown too, not just sharpe/avg_pnl. v60 still wins on win_rate (39.56% vs 16.63%) and k_ratio (24.57 vs 11.43). **Promoted 2026-08-31 per explicit user request ("let's promote this")**: `python promote_config.py --promote 71` -- v60 retired (status=retired, not deleted, revertible), v71 now `status=active`. Verified directly against Mongo post-promotion. **Real caveat flagged to the user immediately**: `ma_crossover_yield_curve_spread_max` will NOT actually do anything in live scanning yet -- it's still backtest/Optuna-only (item 109's own deliberate scope), `ingest.py`/`dip_buy_analyzer.py` never fetch T10Y2Y in production, so `Yield_Curve_Spread` reads missing on every live scan and the filter's null-safe design means it silently never excludes. What actually changes live: faster short MA (11d vs 24d), much wider stop/target (2.09x/4.09x ATR vs 0.62x/1.04x), slightly tighter sector-strength floor (-3.77 vs -3.21). Live-wiring the yield-curve fetch is a separate, undecided future step, same phased sequence sector-strength itself went through.

111. [DONE] Yield-curve filter live-wired into production scanning (2026-08-31), per explicit user request ("lets go to live wiring") -- closes the caveat flagged in item 110's own promotion note. Mirrors exactly how `sector_data`/`Sector_Relative_Strength` was live-wired before it (item 71), same phased build-then-live-wire sequence completing its second phase. `yield_curve` (a single shared FRED T10Y2Y Series, not per-ticker/per-sector like `sector_data` -- closer in shape to `market_df`/SPY) fetched once, unconditionally, inside `market_data.fetch_ticker_bundle()`, gated only on `fred_available()`, degrading to `None` on any failure (~400-day window, comfortably covers `LOOKBACK_PERIOD`="1y"'s own ticker OHLCV range so the reindex+ffill has real coverage). Threading `yield_curve` only through `market_data.py` without fixing this function would have silently failed to reach `Yield_Curve_Spread`. Fixed: new param added, forwarded into `precompute_ma_crossover_frame()`.
