112. [DONE] `compute_k_ratio()` reworked onto the same concurrency-weighted equity model as `compute_max_drawdown()` (2026-08-31), closing the exact follow-up item 110's promotion note flagged: k_ratio previously compounded every resolved trade one-at-a-time in `entry_date` order, treating each as 100% of capital with zero overlap -- the identical fiction `compute_max_drawdown()` was redesigned to fix earlier the same day, and its own docstring named k_ratio as a documented, not-yet-updated follow-up.

    **Implementation reuses, not reimplements**: `_batched_weighted_returns()` (compute_max_drawdown()'s own helper) changed to return `(exit_date, batch_return)` pairs instead of bare floats -- `compute_max_drawdown()` updated to unpack and ignore the date half, `compute_k_ratio()` now calls the same helper directly and regresses `log(equity)` against each BATCH's `exit_date` (elapsed calendar days from the first batch), not each trade's `entry_date` -- consistent with compute_max_drawdown()'s own "P&L realizes at EXIT, not entry" choice already established that day. Reduces to the exact old per-trade-in-entry-order behavior whenever there's no real overlap (every weight 1.0, one trade per batch).

    **New failure mode surfaced by the batching, handled explicitly**: fewer than 3 resolved trades can now still collapse to fewer than 3 distinct exit_date batches if several trades share an exit_date (previously impossible since every trade was always its own data point) -- added an explicit `len(batched) < 3: return None` guard rather than letting it hit a `n=2` divide-by-zero in the residual-variance step. Also tightened the missing-field guard to require `exit_date` as well as `entry_date` (previously only checked `entry_date`), matching `compute_max_drawdown()`'s own convention -- verified this doesn't regress the real-world degrade-gracefully case: live Trade_Outcomes Mongo documents pooled into a minimal `{"status", "pnl_pct"}` shape (via `optimize.py`'s `report_live_outcomes_context()`, `settle_trades.py`) already lack `entry_date` entirely, so they were already hitting the None path before this change and still do now.

    **Tests**: `tests/test_k_ratio.py`'s fixtures updated to carry `exit_date` (previously entry_date-only) -- most set `exit_date == entry_date` (an instant round-trip, concurrency=1, weight=1.0) specifically so the existing hand-picked pnl sequences and their expected k_ratio ranges stay valid unchanged, same "update fixtures, not production code" convention used throughout this project. 3 new tests added: `test_k_ratio_none_when_exit_date_missing`, `test_overlapping_trades_collapse_into_fewer_batches` (2 fully-overlapping trades collapse to 1 batch, a 3rd isolated trade makes 2 total -- still below the floor), and `test_k_ratio_matches_max_drawdown_batching_on_realistic_overlap` (asserts `compute_k_ratio()` and `_batched_weighted_returns()` agree on batch count for the same overlapping set, confirming actual reuse rather than a parallel, possibly-divergent batching scheme). 413 tests green (was 410).

    **Verified against real data, per explicit user follow-up request -- and it REVISES the v60-vs-v71 promotion record, not just theoretically.** One-off script (`scratchpad/verify_kratio_fix_v60_v71.py`, not committed) reused v71's own stored 102-ticker holdout set (same tickers, same 2021-08-30..2026-08-30 window, same 54 folds) and re-ran both configs through `run_walk_forward()`/`optimize.summarize_weighted()` fresh. `sharpe_like`/`max_drawdown` reproduced the already-stored values EXACTLY (v60: -0.04/24.34%, v71: 0.048/21.54%), confirming the same real trade set as the original promotion-time run -- a real apples-to-apples comparison, not a different sample. **k_ratio itself moved dramatically and in OPPOSITE directions for the two configs**: v60 (LIVE) OLD 24.57 -> NEW **-2.028**; v71 (CANDIDATE) OLD 11.446 -> NEW **30.778**. The old naive per-trade-sequential model had it backwards -- v60's own promotion note (item 110) cited k_ratio as its one remaining edge over v71 ("v60 still wins on... k_ratio (24.57 vs 11.43, smoother curve)"). Under the corrected concurrency-weighted model, that's reversed: v60's curve is actually NOT consistent (-2.028, coherent with its already-known negative sharpe_like -0.04), while v71's is strongly consistent (30.778, coherent with its positive sharpe_like). **v71 now wins on drawdown, sharpe, AND (correctly measured) consistency -- v60's only remaining real edge is raw win_rate (39.56% vs 16.63%), an expected tradeoff of v71's "fewer, bigger wins" profile, not a data-quality artifact.** No action taken on this (v71 is already promoted and live) -- documented here because it materially changes how last week's promotion tradeoff should be read, and is a concrete demonstration of why the naive per-trade model was worth fixing beyond code cleanliness.

113. [DONE] RSI v66's random-baseline fill-count anomaly (2026-08-31 investigation, flagged same day in the regime_switcher/RSI random-check session, item 111's own predecessor thread) -- resolved as benign, not a bug. Real numbers had shown a real random-entry draw matched to RSI's own signal count only filled ~19% of its target (1786 of a ~9320-trade target). Root cause confirmed by reading the code directly (not guessed): `levels_from_rsi_frame()`'s `buy_signal = (last_close <= buy_price) and (rsi < threshold)` requires price to ALREADY be at/below the resting limit order (`Buy_Price` = trailing `support_lookback_days` low) as a precondition for a REAL signal to fire at all -- so real signal days fill almost immediately by construction. `simulate_random_entries()` picks ANY macro-uptrend/liquid day as a candidate with no such precondition, so most random days start well above the trailing low and rarely fall to it within `max_entry_wait_days` -- hence the low fill rate. This is expected, honest behavior of the check, not a defect in `simulate_random_entries()` itself.

    **The real open question was whether the resulting SMALL realized random sample (a subset of days where a randomly-chosen day's price happened to fall to the trailing low shortly after) was systematically biased (e.g. skewed toward "buying into a fresh breakdown," a worse cohort than a neutral random baseline) rather than just noisier.** Tested directly (`scratchpad/diagnose_rsi_random_fill_anomaly.py`, not committed): ran REAL, the current SMALL random draw (n_trades matched to real's count, same convention `benchmark_random_entry.py` uses), and an EXHAUSTIVE random draw (n_trades set far above any ticker's eligible-day count, so `simulate_random_entries()`'s own `min(n_trades, len(candidates))` cap means literally every eligible day gets a fill attempt) across the full 407-ticker watchlist, 5-year window. Exhaustive realized 45,917 filled trades (24.6x the small draw's 1,782) -- confirms the mechanism's magnitude directly.

    **Result: SMALL and EXHAUSTIVE random samples agree closely** -- win_rate 16.72% vs 17.35%, sharpe_like 0.064 vs 0.072, avg_pnl_pct 0.44 vs 0.54, k_ratio 21.37 vs 17.63 (same order of magnitude, no sign flip, no dramatic reversal). No evidence the small sample was hiding a directional bias -- it was a smaller, somewhat noisier draw from the same underlying distribution the exhaustive sample also produces. **The original "RSI v66 loses to random on holdout" finding is unweakened by this check** -- the fill-count gap is fully explained by the mechanical reason above, not a methodology flaw that was quietly distorting the comparison's magnitude. This run used plain unweighted `summarize_trades()` over the full ALL-ticker/all-history universe (not the project's usual ticker-holdout/recency-weighted methodology), so its own absolute numbers aren't a substitute for the original holdout-based promotion-relevant check -- it exists purely to answer the fill-rate-anomaly question, which it does. No code or config changed; this closes out the last open item from the 2026-08-31 regime_switcher/RSI investigation thread.

114. [SCOPING CHECK, not yet acted on] Real-vs-random gap re-checked on a small-cap universe outside the current watchlist, per explicit user question ("would win rates increase if we got tickers outside the watchlist"). Sourced 289 real, current S&P 600 SmallCap constituent tickers (alphabetically A-K, via Wikipedia's "List of S&P 600 companies" -- not this project's usual point-in-time-historical methodology, see caveat below), confirmed zero overlap with the existing 407-ticker watchlist.txt. Ran the standard `benchmark_random_entry.py` (10-seed ticker-holdout averaging, same methodology as every other validation this session) against this sample for both `ma_crossover` (v71, active) and `rsi_mean_reversion` (v66, the strategy already known to lose to random on the current watchlist).

    **ma_crossover v71: real still beats random, gap is roughly the SAME magnitude as on the current watchlist** -- holdout gap (real sharpe_like 0.028 vs random -0.056) ~0.085, ALL-cut gap ~0.070, both close to the ~0.072 gap found on the original 407-ticker search that got v71 promoted. No evidence small-caps carry meaningfully more exploitable edge for this strategy specifically.

    **rsi_mean_reversion v66: a real reversal.** On the current watchlist this strategy LOSES to random (2026-08-31 finding, same day). On this small-cap sample it consistently BEATS random on every cut: ALL sharpe_like 0.112 vs 0.074, TUNE (10-seed avg) 0.1095 vs 0.0809, HOLDOUT (10-seed avg) 0.1163 vs 0.0523 -- same direction on all three cuts, the same robustness bar this project's own methodology requires. Real's avg_pnl_pct is meaningfully higher than random's (0.98 vs 0.65 on the ALL cut) despite a similar or slightly lower win_rate -- a bigger-payoff-per-trade edge shape, not a hit-rate edge. Monte Carlo drawdown also favors real decisively (mean 10.53% vs random's 22.29%, worst-case 29.76% vs 48.83%).

    **Real caveats, not yet resolved -- this is a preliminary signal, not a validated edge**: (1) current (not point-in-time historical) S&P 600 membership -- introduces some survivorship bias; this project's own prior diagnostic found that effect small/noise-level for the S&P 500 case, but that was never independently re-checked for this specific small-cap sample. (2) Only ~289 of the full ~600 S&P 600 constituents (alphabetically A-K only, due to a Wikipedia fetch truncation) -- a real, substantial sample, but not the full index. (3) Same simplified cost model as everywhere else in this project ($0 commission, 0.1% slippage only on stop-hits) -- small-caps have real wider spreads/slippage in practice that this backtest doesn't model, which could erode some or much of the apparent edge. (4) v66's parameters were tuned on the LARGE-CAP watchlist and just reapplied here unchanged -- no small-cap-specific re-optimization attempted, so this may understate any real small-cap edge. **Flagged to the user, not decided: whether to pursue this further** (full S&P 600 + a broader small/mid-cap universe, a dedicated re-tune, real transaction-cost modeling) or treat as informative-only for now. No code or config changed; scripts not committed (`scratchpad/smallcap_tickers.txt`, `scratchpad/smallcap_rsi_v66.log`, `scratchpad/smallcap_ma_crossover_v71.log`).

115. [REJECTED candidate, real finding stands] Real Optuna re-tune of `rsi_mean_reversion` against the full ~1000-ticker small/mid-cap universe (item 114's follow-up, per explicit user request "let's scope that out" after the reversal replicated at full scale). 20 trials, standard single-objective (maximize sharpe_like) search, same methodology as every prior RSI tune. Best trial #13: TUNE sharpe_like=0.178, DSR 100% search-level / 97% project-wide confidence (a real, non-random find within the search space). Written as System_Config v72, `status=candidate`, NOT promoted.

    **The re-tune's own automatic holdout check caught a real, honest surprise: v72 does NOT beat v66 (the unmodified, large-cap-tuned live config) on this exact 250-ticker holdout it was searched against.** v66 (live) holdout sharpe_like=0.223; v72 (winning trial) holdout sharpe_like=0.121 -- v66 wins by a wide margin. The search's own built-in `[WARN]` fired exactly as designed: "Candidate (0.121) does NOT beat what's actually live (RSI Mean-Reversion v66, 0.223) on holdout." **v72's TUNE-side win_rate (8.77%) is far more extreme than v66's (~14% on the same universe per item 114) -- an even more skewed few-big-wins profile that plausibly overfit to a handful of large TUNE-side winners that didn't repeat in HOLDOUT, a real generalization failure the ticker-holdout check exists specifically to catch.**

    **This does NOT weaken item 114's core finding -- it strengthens it.** v66, tested here via an entirely different tool/methodology (optimize.py's own single-seed holdout split, not benchmark_random_entry.py's 10-seed-averaged one) than item 114 used, shows its best result yet on this universe (holdout sharpe_like=0.223, its own highest across all three scoping checks so far). The real, useful finding from THIS specific search is negative-but-informative: v66's existing large-cap-tuned parameters (rsi_oversold_threshold=55.7, wider ATR brackets) already generalize well to this small/mid-cap universe without modification -- a single 20-trial/single-seed re-tune attempt didn't find anything better, and the resulting candidate's real-vs-random gap (0.165) beating DEFAULT_CONFIG's (0.043) shows the search itself wasn't broken, it just landed on a thinner, less-generalizing local optimum than the parameters already in production elsewhere.

    **Reframes the natural next step**: pursuing a NEW small/mid-cap RSI variant should lean toward reusing v66's already-validated parameters against this new universe (an architecture/deployment question -- e.g. a new dedicated secondary-strategy track scanning this universe) rather than further parameter search, at least not without a materially larger trial budget and multi-seed holdout averaging (matching this project's own established item-69 lesson: single-seed holdout splits carry real noise, and this was only one seed=42 split). **Flagged to the user, not decided.** v72 stays an unpromoted candidate, kept in Mongo for reference. Scripts/lists not committed (`scratchpad/smallmid_tickers_full.txt`, `scratchpad/sp600_raw.csv`, `scratchpad/sp400_raw.csv`, `scratchpad/smallmid_full_rsi_v66.log`, `scratchpad/smallmid_full_ma_crossover_v71.log`, `scratchpad/smallmid_rsi_optuna_retune.log`).

    **Also note (data-integrity finding, worth remembering)**: sourcing the small/mid-cap ticker universe first hit a real problem -- a `WebFetch` call summarizing a truncated Wikipedia table HALLUCINATED plausible-looking but fake tickers past the real content's cutoff (an alphabet-cycling pattern like `MSXA, MSXB, MSXC...`, mixed in with real names like `LULU`/`LYFT` early in the same response). Caught by eye before use, discarded entirely, and replaced with a deterministic `pandas.read_html()` parse of the raw Wikipedia HTML (via `requests` with an explicit User-Agent, since the default urllib UA gets a 403) -- zero LLM involvement, zero hallucination risk, verified real tickers at both ends of both tables (603 S&P 600 + 400 S&P 400, 1 real overlap with the existing watchlist). **Lesson: never trust a WebFetch/summarized extraction of a long structured list (ticker lists, numeric tables) without either a small enough response to fully eyeball or a deterministic parse -- summarization models will silently pattern-complete past a truncation point.**

[DONE, per explicit user request "let's pursue a new strategy"] RSI Mean-Reversion (Small/Mid-Cap) built end-to-end -- a new experimental strategy variant reusing RSI Mean-Reversion's own System_Config v66 UNMODIFIED against a new, dedicated ticker universe (`smallmid_watchlist.txt`, 992 real S&P 600 SmallCap + S&P 400 MidCap tickers, deterministically sourced via `pandas.read_html()` off Wikipedia -- zero overlap with the primary watchlist), the direct follow-through on item 114/115's finding. Used `EnterPlanMode` given the real architectural gap found during research: this codebase had NO existing precedent for a strategy scanning a different ticker universe than the one shared `bundle` every primary/secondary/experimental strategy reuses -- confirmed via 2 parallel Explore agents plus a Plan agent review before writing any code.

    **Design (deliberately NOT generalizing shared plumbing for one case)**: new `config_loader.SMALLMID_RSI_LABEL`/`SMALLMID_RSI_CONFIG_VERSION`/`SMALLMID_RSI_LOG_STRATEGY` constants, kept OUT of `EXPERIMENTAL_STRATEGY_VERSIONS` (that dict's own loop assumes a shared bundle -- generalizing it to serve exactly one new case would have weakened the separation that's `EXPERIMENTAL_STRATEGY_VERSIONS`'s own actual capital-safety mechanism). New self-contained `ingest.run_smallmid_rsi_experimental()` -- own `fetch_ticker_bundle()` call, never capital-allocated (explicit zeroed `Shares_To_Buy`/`Est_Cost`, mirroring `run_experimental_strategies()`), new `--smallmid-rsi-only` CLI flag that skips the whole primary/secondary pipeline. New `dip_buy_analyzer.cached_fetch_smallmid_bundle()` + a new section in the existing "Daily Signals (experimental)" tab, reusing `render_experimental_section()`.

    **A real bug caught during a Plan-agent review before it shipped**: reusing `render_experimental_section()` as-is would have logged this variant's signals under the bare `"rsi"` label (`config.strategy` for v66) -- colliding with ~175 old contaminated pre-2026-08-20 documents from before RSI Mean-Reversion's own v17->v66 rename. Fixed by adding an optional `log_strategy_override` param to `render_experimental_section()` (default `None`, zero behavior change for Momentum Rank's existing call), mirroring `render_secondary_section()`'s own precedent exactly. A second real bug caught the same way: `dip_buy_analyzer.cached_fetch_bundle()` hardcodes `read_ticker_sectors(WATCHLIST_FILE)` internally -- calling it directly with smallmid tickers would have silently returned `{}` sectors for all of them. Fixed with a genuinely separate `cached_fetch_smallmid_bundle()`, not a call into the existing one.

    **New GitHub Actions workflow** (`.github/workflows/smallmid_rsi.yml`) -- deliberately a SEPARATE daily cron, not folded into `daily_run.py`/`daily_run.yml`: ~1000 sequential ticker fetches would meaningfully eat into `daily_run.yml`'s existing 45-minute budget shared with the primary scan/LLM Agent/LLM strategy research/regime switcher. Own 90-minute timeout, same `MONGODB_URI` secret, no LLM/Discord secrets needed (this variant calls neither). Automated DAILY, not deferred to dashboard-only -- a Plan-agent review flagged that deferring automation would reproduce the exact gap `run_experimental_strategies()`'s own docstring documents fixing for Momentum Rank (3 days dashboard-only, zero real signals logged, because nobody opened the tab). Built and committed but not yet enabled for real (matches `monthly_reoptimize.yml`'s own "built but not yet enabled" precedent) -- needs the user's go-ahead before the cron starts actually running.

    **Tests**: new `tests/test_smallmid_rsi.py` (5 tests) -- watchlist parses correctly with real known tickers/sectors (992 tickers, zero overlap with the primary watchlist, GICS sector names normalized to match `watchlist.SECTOR_ETF`'s vocabulary -- 2 of 11 mismatched, `"Health Care"`/`"Information Technology"` vs this project's own `"Healthcare"`/`"Technology"`, fixed at build time), config loads, `run_smallmid_rsi_experimental()`'s zero-capital enforcement + correct log label (monkeypatched, no real network/Mongo), `render_experimental_section()`'s new override param actually changes the logged label while leaving the no-override default path unchanged, graceful skip when the watchlist file is missing. 418 tests green (was 413). Deliberately NOT inserted into the existing dict-parametrized `test_config_candidates_load.py`/`test_strategy_dispatch_parity.py`, which iterate `SECONDARY_STRATEGY_VERSIONS`/`EXPERIMENTAL_STRATEGY_VERSIONS` by design.

    **Real live smoke test** (`python ingest.py --smallmid-rsi-only` against real Mongo): worked end-to-end -- 87 actionable + 268 research signals logged under `strategy="rsi_mean_reversion_smallmid"`, confirmed never capital-allocated. **Real operational finding, not a code bug**: only 617/992 tickers (62%) were actually fetched -- 375 failed with yfinance's "possibly delisted" error, including definitely-not-delisted names (DUOL, DY, DOCN -- verified all three fetch fine in isolation moments later). Diagnosed as transient Yahoo Finance rate-limiting from this session's own cumulative heavy fetching (several ~1000-ticker passes already run today testing items 114/115) -- not expected to be this severe in production, where the daily cron runs once, from a single fresh GitHub Actions runner IP, not stacked on top of same-session local testing load. The code itself degraded gracefully throughout -- no crash, clean per-ticker skip, logged everything it successfully fetched. Worth a real production check once the cron is actually enabled (does coverage stay this degraded, or was today genuinely anomalous).

    **Deliberately NOT done** (matches Momentum Rank's own current state): not added to `best_ideas.METHODOLOGIES`/`ic_tracking` (no composite influence yet), not capital-eligible, not re-tuned (reuses v66 exactly, per item 115's own finding), not added to `SECONDARY_VALIDATION_CAVEATS` (its own honest-disclosure text lives directly in the new dashboard section instead).

    **Committed and pushed 2026-09-01, per explicit user request ("enable both")** -- `git commit` (bundled with item 112/113's k_ratio work, same session) + `git push` to `main`. GitHub Actions picks up a new workflow's cron schedule automatically once it lands on the default branch, no separate manual toggle -- `smallmid_rsi.yml` reuses the exact same `MONGODB_URI` repo secret `daily_run.yml` already has configured, so no new secret setup needed. First real scheduled run: the next weekday 21:35 UTC after this push.

117. [DONE] LLM strategy research loop's 5-straight-zero-trade-cycle streak fixed, per explicit user request to investigate. Root cause was NOT a schema limitation as first hypothesized -- `swingtrade.evaluate_llm_rule_conditions()` and the proposal prompt itself both already fully supported `"logic": "OR"`, confirmed by reading the code before assuming anything. Two real, distinct problems found instead, both fixed in `llm_strategy_research.py`:

    **(1) `run_daily_cycle()`'s sample was never randomized or rotated.** `sample = tickers[:sample_size]` took the literal FIRST 40 tickers of `watchlist.txt`, in file order, IDENTICAL every single cycle -- confirmed via real Mongo data that all 5 real cycles so far tested the exact same, extremely homogeneous slice (NVDA, AAPL, MSFT, AVGO, MU, AMD, INTC, AMAT, LRCX, CSCO... -- entirely mega-cap Technology), zero exploration of the other ~367 tickers. Combined with a rule needing multiple conditions to align simultaneously, this alone plausibly explained the whole zero-trade streak independent of the rule's own design. Fixed: date-seeded `random.Random(f"llm-strategy-research-sample-{today}").sample(tickers, k=sample_size)` -- rotates the sample every calendar day, but stays reproducible if the same day's cycle is re-run (e.g. while debugging).

    **(2) The LLM had never once tried OR logic or fewer conditions across 5 real cycles**, despite the prompt always naming it as available -- each "refinement" instead kept the same 3-4-condition AND stack and only nudged individual numeric thresholds. Fixed: `_build_proposal_prompt()` now explicitly names this exact pattern ("every prior real cycle here has done exactly that... never OR, never fewer conditions") and suggests the alternative directly, rather than leaving the LLM to rediscover an option it already had.

    **Tests**: 3 new (`tests/test_llm_strategy_research.py`) -- the new prompt text is present, the sample is provably not the fixed first-N slice (using an ordered dummy ticker list where a lingering bug would be obvious), and the sample stays reproducible across two calls on the same mocked day. 421 tests green (was 418).

    **Verified live, not just unit-tested -- both fixes worked immediately, on the very first real cycle after the change.** Ran `python llm_strategy_research.py --run-once` for real: the LLM's own rationale explicitly reasoned about the fix ("Prior cycles returned zero trades because they combined 3 or 4 strict conditions... under an AND logical operator... By reducing the complexity to just two... conditions and switching the logic to OR, we allow the engine to trigger whenever a ticker is either deeply oversold OR experiencing a significant squeeze breakout"), and produced **5,189 real trades on the sample** -- versus zero on every one of the prior 5 cycles. Confirmed via Mongo that Cycle 6's real sample tickers (AEE, AMD, KLAC, CLX, DRI, PNC, ZM, V, GDDY, CPRT, FIS, SEDG, STT, UAL, CI, ...) are a genuinely diverse cross-sector mix, nothing like the old fixed tech-only slice. Cycle 6 itself didn't escalate to full validation (REAL sharpe_like 0.072 / win_rate 20.72% both came in slightly below RANDOM's 0.08 / 23.03% -- an honest miss, not a bug) -- the fix's actual goal (a loop that produces genuinely testable results instead of perpetual zero-trade cycles) is confirmed working regardless of any single cycle's own outcome.

    **Committed and pushed 2026-09-01, per explicit user request** -- commit `b4f245d` on `main`. No new automation/workflow needed -- this loop is already wired into `daily_run.py`'s existing 5th step (weekday cron), so the fix takes effect automatically on the next scheduled run, nothing further to enable.

118. [DONE] `reoptimize_sector_rs.recommend_lookback()` gained an absolute-informativeness floor, closing a real, previously-documented gap (flagged in item 76's own write-up, never fixed until now). The function only ever compared candidates RELATIVELY (does the best beat current by `MEANINGFUL_IC_MARGIN`) -- no floor on whether the WINNING candidate was itself actually informative. Real incident this closes: item 76's own full-scale re-run found 21d IC=-0.0028 (essentially zero, not a real edge) still "won" by beating 63d's more-negative IC by more than the margin -- the mechanical recommendation fired on evidence too thin to mean anything either way, a "least-bad of N weak options" trap.

    **Fix**: new `MIN_ABSOLUTE_IC = 0.02`, deliberately reusing `MEANINGFUL_IC_MARGIN`'s own value (the same magnitude this project already uses as "a real difference" is equally reasonable as the floor for "actually informative at all") -- the winning candidate's own IC must now clear this floor in addition to beating current by the relative margin, both required. Applies uniformly whether or not current has a defined IC. Console output updated to explain both bars, not just the margin one.

    **Tests**: the one existing test that had locked in the OLD flawed behavior as "correct" (`test_negative_ics_still_compared_correctly`, asserting -0.01 beats -0.20 and SHOULD recommend) rewritten to assert `None` now, plus 3 new tests (relative-margin logic still works correctly once the winner clears the floor; floor applies even with no current-IC baseline; recommendation fires at exactly the floor boundary). 424 tests green (was 421).

    **Verified against the real documented incident's own numbers, not just synthetic cases**: fed `recommend_lookback()` the actual real IC values from item 76's write-up (21d=-0.0028, 63d=-0.110, all weak-to-negative) -- confirms the fix now correctly returns `None` instead of the flawed real recommendation that fired at the time.
